# Changelog

All notable changes to EntropicMem are documented here. The format follows Keep a Changelog.

## [Unreleased]

> Entries below that also shipped in the 2.8.1 patch are listed again, verbatim, in the `[2.8.1]` section.

### Added

- **EM-605 slice 1 — the 2,122-line `graph_export.py` monolith is split into a graph model, four renderers and packaged viewer assets.** The module held three unrelated things and had to, because the viewer was one `_HTML_TEMPLATE` raw string at the bottom of it. The **model** — the palettes, `get_shape`, `detect_communities`, `body_coverage`, `assert_bodies_present`, `resolve_note_path`, and the payload builder lifted out of `export_json` — is `em/graph/model.py`; it writes no file and imports neither `json` nor `html`, so a second renderer can share it without a cycle. The **renderers** (`export_json`, `export_dot`, `export_canvas`, `export_html`) are `em/graph/export.py`, and `export_json` is now a thin write over `build_graph_payload` instead of the place the graph is assembled. The **viewer** is three assets — `em/graph/assets/graph.html` (the shell), `graph.css` and `graph.js` — read through `importlib.resources` and **inlined** at export, so a written `graph.html` is still one self-contained file that opens over `file://`. **No behaviour change, and that is checked:** the three assets reassemble to byte-for-byte the raw string the monolith held, and the body gate's error message still names the export path (the model takes the caller's name for it, so the one string the split could have silently degraded is pinned). `pyproject.toml` declares `em.graph` in `packages=` and the three globs in a new `[tool.setuptools.package-data]` block — a wheel missing either ships the plugin without its viewer while the catalog's directory-copy install keeps working, a drift no existing test could see. `graph_export.py` is **deleted, not shimmed**: `entropicmem.py`, `scripts/graph_server/server.py` and every graph test import from `em.graph.model` / `em.graph.export`, and the six suites that scraped the template out of the Python source now read the assembled assets through one helper, `tests/harness/graph_assets.py`. **The graph suites are the safety net and pass unchanged** (128 in the graph/phase3 set, 190 with the server and incremental suites). 7 new tests (`tests/unit/test_em605_graph_split.py`: the assets reassemble to the substituted template; the export inlines all three and leaves no placeholder; `export_json` writes exactly what the model builds; the body gate still names the export path; `graph_export` is gone and the split holds; the model imports no renderer; the wheel declares `em.graph` and its assets) and **8 mutation checks, all red** (each of the three placeholder substitutions dropped in turn; the data blanked; the body gate's context flattened; `em.graph` dropped from `packages=`; the package-data block renamed away; the shell's CSS placeholder renamed) — each restored from a byte copy. Suite **2860 / 16 / 2** bare on 3.11 (was 2853 / 16 / 2), **2861 / 15 / 2** with the digest list, **2938 / 2 / 2** CI-equivalent 3.12; CI's 3.12 count **2936 / 4 / 2**, read green from the job log at `2fd7812` with all four legs identical, `windows-import` included (and the post-merge `main` push re-ran the eleven on the same SHA, all success); the 3.10 leg green for the touched files (135 passed); `ruff==0.16.2` and `0.17.0` clean; the privacy word-hash scan (which reads the staged tree) found no collision. **One process incident, recorded:** the first pass measured the three local triples while the seventh test did not exist yet, so each was written one low and the CI projection was one low with it — CI's own read caught the discrepancy, the triples were re-measured on the landed tree, and the figures above are the corrected ones. **Recorded, not built:** slice 2 of this card — entity nodes and relation edges, the time slider over `valid_from`/`valid_to`, the owner-only scope filter and dashed superseded edges — is the next chunk; this slice moves the code the card asked for and changes nothing the user can see.

- **EM-604 — opt-in ingestion of human vault edits: the projection's other direction, and what it reads back is a proposal, never a fact.** `vault.ingest_human_edits` (default **false**) joins the typed config (`VaultConfig` field + its `RULES` entry, already present from EM-407, plus the curated `SCHEMA_KEYS` entry and the `plugins/entropicmem/config_schema.py` mirror), and the `project_vault` payload carries `"ingest": true` only when the provider sees it on — an off payload is byte-identical to before. The new `em/vault/ingest.py` reads each entity page's **human region** — everything outside every `entropicmem:auto` block — and turns each `- <statement>` line into a **`pending`** memory (`source="vault_edit"`, linked to that page's entity) so the user's own note lands in the review queue, not the live store; the store's only route to `pending` is the write policy, so `evaluate_write` gains a `vault_edit` quarantine branch. **The pass runs before `project()`** in the handler, so a line the user deleted from an auto-section is seen as a removal — the projection then re-adds it, and ingestion records a review hint (`user removed line for m-<short> - forget?`, ASCII) in one deduped `meta` key rather than a new table. Removal detection is a persisted per-page baseline of rendered block refs (`vault:seen:<profile>:<user>:<relative>`), never a guess: a page whose markers were gutted is skipped, the first pass records a baseline and stays quiet, and the pass is scope-bounded (F-004) so another profile's entity is not ingested. **Surface:** `V3Engine.vault_review_hints()` feeds `entropicmem_review`'s `list_pending` as `vault_hints`, printed by CLI `cmd_pending`. **Cuts (named):** no new CLI subcommand — the job plus the config is the surface; no LLM over human lines (they become literal pending rows). 15 new tests (`tests/unit/test_em604_vault_ingest.py`); **11 mutation checks, all red** (the policy quarantine removed; the human region flattened to the whole page; the scope guard dropped; the gutted-page skip removed; the entity link dropped; the live filter dropped from `read_hints`; the removal baseline never read; the enqueue dropping the ingest flag; the handler ignoring the payload flag; the curated config key dropped; the entity-link helper made a no-op) — each restored from a byte copy. Suite **2852 / 16 / 2** bare on 3.11, **2853 / 15 / 2** with the digest list, **2930 / 2 / 2** CI-equivalent 3.12 with the digest list; CI's 3.12 count **2928 / 4 / 2**, read green from the job log at `31a7323` (all four legs identical); the 3.10 leg is green for the touched files (143 passed); `ruff==0.16.2` and `0.17.0` clean; the privacy word-hash scan (which reads the staged tree) found no collision. **Landed branch-first** (`d8f643f` code+tests+CHANGELOG, `31a7323` docs; 11/11 check-runs green on both SHAs, `identity-guard` included; `--ff-only`; branch deleted). **One recorded flake, not a code fault:** the post-merge `main` push re-ran `windows-import` on the *same* SHA and EM-603's 5k refresh AC read **440 ms** (its cold phase took 59.8 s on a contended Windows runner) — the branch push on that SHA had `windows-import` green — and a re-run of the failed job was green. The EM-603 test was not touched, weakened or skipped.

- **EM-603 — the incremental index: a refresh now costs one `stat` per note instead of a read-and-hash, and a projection schedules it.** `notes_meta` gains `mtime_ns` and `size` (`index.db` `PRAGMA user_version` 3, with the in-place ALTER migration EM-602's pattern established). `rebuild()` compares each note's `(st_mtime_ns, st_size)` first and **does not open** a note whose stat has not moved, so an unchanged vault is a directory walk plus one stat per note. Only a moved stat is read, and the content hash still decides whether the row is *changed* — a bare `touch` refreshes the stat columns and nothing else. **The card's AC is met with room:** a 5,000-note vault with one changed file refreshes in **26 ms** (3.11), **31 ms** (3.12) and **32 ms** (3.10) against the 300 ms budget. **The walk is one pass:** `Vault.iter_note_stats` scans the tree with `os.scandir` and reports each file's stat from the directory read, so a refresh no longer builds a `Path` and resolves a relative path per file — the difference, measured, between 398 ms on a shared CI 3.12 runner (the first push, over budget) and a 10× margin. **The recorded trade-off:** a rewrite that preserves both size *and* mtime is invisible to a stat diff; `index rebuild` is the escape hatch (pinned by a test). **New surface:** `entropicmem index refresh [--include-archive]` runs the incremental path (in `CLI_REFERENCE.md` and the README's command table; the top-level command count is unchanged), and the **`reindex_vault`** job type (`KNOWN_JOB_TYPES` + the shared registry) refreshes the index after a projection — the `project_vault` handler enqueues it, dedupe-keyed per (vault root, index db), when the projection payload carries the configured `index_db` (the provider passes `paths.index_db`); the handler imports the v2 index **lazily**, the same bridge pattern the facade uses for `memory_engine`, and a missing path or root is a `PermanentJobError`. **Containment:** the refresh takes its paths and stats from that own walk rather than calling `resolve_path` per note (resolving every component was the entire cost) and keeps the guarantee by **never following a symlink** — neither a linked note nor a linked directory is walked, so a link can neither escape the vault nor cause a needless re-read; every caller-supplied path still goes through `resolve_path`. **Not in this slice, recorded:** chunk embeddings are EM-308's and that card is not built — there is no chunk-embedding path to enqueue into, so this card does not invent one. **Evidence:** 19 new tests (`tests/unit/test_em603_mtime_index.py` — the read elision, the touch-vs-change distinction, the same-size/same-mtime trade-off and its full-rebuild escape hatch, the v2→v3 migration and backfill, the symlink rules, the CLI command, the job and its projection scheduling); **11 mutation checks, all red**, each restored from a byte copy — the stat skip, the stored stat, the hash check, the version stamp, the column migration, the parser's `refresh`, `KNOWN_JOB_TYPES`, the projection's enqueue, the reindex dedupe, the CLI handler, and the symlink skip. One deliberate repoint: `test_em602_note_ids` no longer pins `SCHEMA_VERSION == 2` (the card's change is generation 2; later cards advance it) and pins `NOTE_ID_VERSION == 2` instead. **A Windows fix is recorded:** the card's `windows-import` leg caught that `cmd_index` printed a `→` (and four other CLI output lines carried non-ASCII), which raises `UnicodeEncodeError` on a cp1252 console — the CLI's output is now ASCII throughout; the same run's second failure was a test substring-matching a JSON-escaped path, fixed by parsing the payload. Suite **2837 / 16 / 2** bare on 3.11 (was 2818 / 16 / 2), **2838 / 15 / 2** with the digest list, **2915 / 2 / 2** CI-equivalent 3.12; CI's 3.12 count **2913 / 4 / 2** (read green from the job log at `338ec2a`, all four legs identical); `ruff==0.16.2` clean; the 3.10 leg green; the privacy word-hash scan found no collision. **One process incident, recorded:** the first pass restored the CLI mutation with `git checkout --`, which also reverted the uncommitted `cmd_index` change it sat beside — the exact hazard the rule names. It was noticed, re-applied, and the mutation re-run from a byte copy.

- **The `remember` content ceiling is raised — 4,000 → 8,000 characters — in one shared constant, and the model is told.** `em/provider/tools.py` gains `CONTENT_MAX_CHARS = 8_000`, which both Appendix B schemas now build `content`'s `maxLength` from, so the number exists exactly once: the schema advertises it to the model, the validator enforces it, and the tests pin both. The two `content` descriptions also gain the guidance sentence the model reads while composing the call — *"Beyond the embedding window — long documents belong in the vault; store the fact or a pointer to the note"* — which prevents oversize calls better than a refusal can (the refusal is the backstop, not the teacher). Nothing else moves: **no config key** (the schemas are verbatim-pinned artifacts, and a runtime dial would put the advertised schema and the validator on independent drift paths), no migration, `provides_tools` stays 9, embeddings' 2,000-char window is untouched, and serving is budget-bound rather than length-bound — §4.2's block cap and the packer's behaviour are unchanged. **The two `maxLength` values and the two descriptions are this card's recorded Appendix B deviation**; every other schema field is byte-identical. **Containment, measured on this box:** a 20,000-fact fixture at ~2,000-character content builds its FTS index in **1.05 s**, weighs **90 MB**, and serves warm at **p95 62 ms** inside §4.2's 9,000-character block cap — a longer row costs index bytes and the same bounded query, never a bigger block. The evidence and the count triples are in the Fixed entry below.

- **EM-601 slice 1 — the vault projection engine (S6's first card): the store renders itself into Markdown, and only its own regions are ever rewritten.** `em/vault/` (new package) adds `em/vault/projection.py`: `project(conn, root, scope, modes)` renders `Entities/<Name>.md`, `Journal/<date>.md` and `Memories/Unfiled.md` from the v3 store, and `lint(root)` reports projected files whose auto-sections are unsafe. **The contract:** every generated region sits between `<!-- entropicmem:auto:begin id=… -->` / `…end… -->` markers, and only those bodies are replaced — the frontmatter, the human region before the first marker, the gap between blocks, the trailing text after the last marker and any section this run does not own are copied back **byte for byte**, which is what makes a hand edit survive ten projection runs; a file whose markers the user deleted is left exactly as it is and reported by `lint` (`missing_markers`), never recreated over their prose; a run that renders identical bytes writes nothing (no mtime move, no temp file), and writes are mkstemp + `os.replace` inside the destination directory with the temp unlinked on any failure. **Forgetting erases rather than hides:** an entity whose rows are all gone is still *visited*, so an existing page is rewritten down to what remains — while an entity with nothing to show and no page yet is not created at all; the unfiled page and a day's journal page behave the same way, and a relation renders only while its source memory is live. **Deliberate strengthening:** §3.5's owner-only tiers (`sensitive`/`secret`) are excluded from the vault outright, regardless of scope — the vault is plaintext Markdown the user syncs and shares, so `MemoryStore.add`'s own refusal to write `secret` and the migrated v2 rows that already carry the tier can never become a file; the read clause is otherwise exactly `MemoryStore.list`'s (`scope_profile` + `scope_user` + chat), so one scope's projection never renders another user's rows (F-004). **Recorded, not built:** the `project_vault` job, its 60 s debounce and the provider's enqueue on write paths (slice 2), the `vault.projection` mode switch (config → `modes`; `legacy` stays v2's note-per-fact writer), month paging for `Memories/Unfiled.md` past 200 rows, and an unbounded journal — all four are named in the module docstring and in `REMAINING_PLAN.md` §6.5. 31 new tests (`tests/unit/test_em601_projection.py`); **10 mutation checks, all red** (the tier predicate dropped; `forbid_tiers` default flipped; `sensitive` let through while `secret` was held; the human region dropped; the trailing text dropped; a fully-forgotten entity never revisited; the relation's source-memory liveness guard removed; a marker-less file recreated over the user's prose; `lint` blind to projected files; the unchanged-on-disk comparison dropped), each restored from a byte copy. Suite **2737 / 16 / 2** bare on 3.11 (was 2706 / 16 / 2); the new package and its tests re-run green on 3.10; `ruff` (`0.16.2`) clean.
- **EM-601 slice 2 — the projection job, its mode switch, and the lint surfacing** (`em/vault/schedule.py`, new): `project_vault` joins `KNOWN_JOB_TYPES` and `build_registry` under the EM-209 contract, so the worker and the CLI's `--types` take it from the shared list; `enqueue_projection()` debounces writes with a 60 s slide clamped at `MAX_WAIT_SECONDS` (300 s) from the burst's first write, and a write landing mid-projection gets its own follow-up row instead of being silently dropped by `JobQueue`'s dedupe. The provider enqueues on the `_remember_v3`, `_update` and `_forget_one` write paths, inside the write transaction, keyed per (vault root, scope). `vault.projection` flips its default from `entities+journal` to `none` — off until explicitly enabled, so a worker can never write a user's disk without them asking — and `modes_for()` maps `none|entities|entities+journal|legacy` onto the engine's own mode names (`legacy` and `none` both map to no modes, for opposite reasons; an unknown value raises `ValueError`). The CLI gains `vault lint --root <path>`: prints `lint(root)`'s `path` + `problem` pairs and exits non-zero when any exist, so a gutted projection is visible to the person looking at the vault. `em/cli/parser.py` gains the `vault lint` subcommand; `entropicmem.py` gains `cmd_vault` and the `dispatch` route; `CLI_REFERENCE.md` and `README.md` move the documented command count 36→37. 18 new tests (`tests/unit/test_em601_slice2_schedule.py`); **5 mutation checks, all red** (the `legacy` mapping dropped; the dedupe check removed so writes fan out; the `MAX_WAIT_SECONDS` ceiling removed so a burst defers forever; the handler's failure swallowed so the vault goes stale with no signal; the unknown-mode `ValueError` removed so a typo reads as "the vault is empty"), each restored from a byte copy. Suite **2758 / 16 / 2** bare on 3.11, **2759 / 15 / 2** with the digest list, **2836 / 2 / 2** CI-equivalent 3.12; CI's 3.12 count **2834 / 4 / 2** (read green from `BASELINE.md`); `ruff` (`0.16.2`) clean; privacy word-hash scan clean on every changed file. **Deferred, recorded:** `Memories/Unfiled.md` month paging past 200 rows — paging needs a policy for the month file that stops being current, left as a recorded decision rather than a guess.

- **EM-303's prune — the orphaned embedding-maintenance promise, its own destructive card.** `em/embeddings/prune.py` (new) adds the worker-only `prune` job handler: after a completed backfill, once `now − embedding_model_switched_at` exceeds the grace (default **7 days**, payload-overridable), one run deletes every `embeddings` row whose model is neither the recorded current model nor the immediately previous one, then checkpoints and vacuums on a dedicated autocommit connection. **Refusal before deletion, never a guess:** no `meta.embedding_model` (never completed a backfill), an incomplete transition record (including the dangerous half-record — a readable old clock with no previous-model identity — the unnamed rollback set is never prunable), or an unreadable clock is a logged no-op, never a dead job. `dry_run` reports per-model counts and deletes nothing. **The backfill gains the transition record it was always owed:** its completion transaction now writes `meta.embedding_model_previous` + `meta.embedding_model_switched_at` atomically with the model stamp (`store.embeddings.record_model_switch`), and enqueues one dedupe-keyed `prune` job **only when a rollback set exists** (a store's first-ever backfill would only manufacture a refused no-op). The corrected semantics the test plan arbitrates against the draft's AC2 tail: **the previous model's rows survive the grace** — they are the rollback set for flipping `meta.embedding_model` back, and become prunable only once a newer completed backfill demotes them. `prune` joins `KNOWN_JOB_TYPES` (worker and CLI types come from the shared list, drift-free by import). **Not in this chunk: no destructive run** — the handler exists only as code and tests; real runs stay owner-gated. 12 new tests (`tests/unit/test_em303_prune.py`); **8 mutation checks, all red** (the grace comparison; the current-model keep set; the previous-model keep set; the missing-transition refusal; the no-current-model refusal; the dry-run flag ignored; vacuum moved before the commit; the first-backfill enqueue gate), each restored from a byte copy. Suite **2706 / 16 / 2** bare on 3.11; ruff (`0.16.2`) clean; the prune-related legs re-run green on 3.10.

- **S5 complete — formation & consolidation, and the development stop point.** The remaining S5 wiring landed: `find_neighbours` retrieves the top-5 active memories through the retrieval pipeline's own seam (`with_gate=False`), `reconcile_pending` drains a scope's pending auto-extracted rows with the §3.7 step-3 commit policy carried from the typed config, and a `reconcile` job (in `KNOWN_JOB_TYPES` with its handler) runs the pass; the extraction handlers stamp `pending_expires_at` and enqueue one reconcile pass per scope. **The missing capture link is fixed:** `sync_turn` now appends the turn's redacted user/assistant text to `transcript_chunks` (the window's seq range is exactly what extraction reads), so a sealed window has something to extract — v3 only, fail-soft. **The S5 exit criteria are met and pinned:** `tests/unit/test_s5_learning_e2e.py` drives the whole rule path with **no LLM** — a preference in session 1 is extracted, reconciled, auto-committed and **served in session 2 with no explicit `remember`**; the LLM F1 harness is measured, not gated; supersession is pinned by the EM-503 apply tests and the supersession e2e. 8 tests; **6 mutation checks, all red** (neighbours disabled; the pass a no-op; the reconcile enqueue dropped; the handler a no-op; the chunk capture dropped; the commit policy ignored), each restored from a byte copy. **Development stops here:** S5 is complete and no chunk is queued — see `docs/plan/STOP_HERE.md`. Suite **2679 / 16 / 3** bare, **2680 / 15 / 3** with the digest list, **2757 / 2 / 3** CI-equivalent 3.12; CI's 3.12 count **2755 / 4 / 3**.

- **EM-503 — reconciliation and the commit policy (§3.7 steps 2-3).** `em/formation/reconcile.py` (new) decides what an extracted candidate means: exact content hash → `NOOP`; the same subject+attribute with a changed value → `SUPERSEDE`; a near-duplicate (token cosine ≥ 0.92) whose numbers and negations agree → `UPDATE`; otherwise `ADD`. `CONFLICT` is the **model's** uncertain verdict (Appendix C.2), never invented by the deterministic path. The §3.7 step-3 **commit policy** commits `user_stated` or a confident candidate (confidence ≥ 0.75, an allowed kind, tier ≤ internal, no trust flags) and keeps everything else `pending`. `apply_decision` uses the store's **versioned** writes — `UPDATE` snapshots the old content and bumps the version, `SUPERSEDE` links the old row to a successor, `NOOP` discards the redundant candidate, `CONFLICT` keeps it pending with a `conflict:<target>` trust flag (via the new `MemoryStore.set_trust_flags`) — and every decision appends a `reconcile` audit row. **The CI-gated AC is met:** the rule path scores **≥ 0.9** on the `update` and `contradiction` categories of the new `evals/datasets/reconcile.jsonl` (29 synthetic candidate/neighbour cases). **Not in this slice:** neighbour retrieval (top-5 over the index) and a `reconcile` job/pass driving the pending queue are the wiring half. 9 tests; **6 mutation checks, all red** (attribute detector disabled; decide always ADD; commit policy always active; the UPDATE write removed; the conflict flag removed; NOOP not discarded), each restored from a byte copy. Suite **2670 / 16 / 3** bare, **2671 / 15 / 3** with the digest list, **2748 / 2 / 3** CI-equivalent 3.12; CI's 3.12 count **2746 / 4 / 3**.

- **EM-502 slice 2 — extraction runs as a job, and the turn's scope reaches the write.** `em/formation/extract_jobs.py` (new) adds the `extract_window`/`extract_session` handlers: they read the window's transcript chunks (a new `EpisodeStore.read_chunks`), extract through slice 1, and **quarantine every candidate as `pending`** — the write policy's `auto_extracted` source is used for both, so an LLM-extracted delegation cannot bypass quarantine; the origin is kept as a tag. **The scope is carried, never guessed:** `transcript_chunks` has no scope column, so the provider serializes its **effective turn scope** (`_turn_scope()` — profile `_profile_id or 'default'`, *not* `agent_identity`) into the payload, and the handler raises `PermanentJobError` on a scope-less job. Both types join `KNOWN_JOB_TYPES` and `BUILT_FORMATION_HANDLERS`, which **opens EM-411's capture gate** (with `formation.mode != off`). A scope-consistency test proves the write and the same turn's read resolve to the same profile — including the empty-`agent_identity` trap. **Deliberate contract updates:** the EM-411 window payload pin now asserts the scope, its "ships no handler" pin asserts the new set, and the EM-405 delegation payload pin asserts the scope. 8 new tests; **6 mutation checks, all red** (scope from `agent_identity`; the handler ignoring the payload scope; the registry dropping the handlers; `BUILT_FORMATION_HANDLERS` emptied; candidates not quarantined; a missing scope defaulting), each restored from a byte copy. Suite **2661 / 16 / 3** bare, **2662 / 15 / 3** with the digest list, **2739 / 2 / 3** CI-equivalent 3.12; CI's 3.12 count **2737 / 4 / 3**.

- **EM-502 slice 1 — the deterministic extractor (§3.7).** The rule path that always runs (`formation.llm=none` included) now lives in `em/formation/rules.py`: EM-111's generic first-person patterns extended with **correction** ("actually", "no, it's", "not X but Y") and **commitment** ("I'll", "we will", "by Friday") detection, emitting Appendix C.1's candidate shape (`kind`/`content`/`evidence_quote`/`speaker`/`author_id`/`source_turn`/`confidence`). `evidence_quote` is the matched substring, so it is **verbatim by construction** and re-checked downstream — a model that fabricates a quote is rejected, never written — and assistant-originated claims are capped at 0.6 (§3.7). `em/formation/extract.py` adds ≤ 6,000-char segmentation with one-turn overlap, the Appendix C.1 prompt and schema through the EM-501 `FormationLLM` seam, and the evidence guard both paths share. **The card's CI-gated AC is met:** rule-path precision holds **≥ 0.8** on the new `evals/datasets/extraction.jsonl` (55 synthetic transcripts with gold memories and deliberate decoys); the LLM path's F1 is *measured* by the same harness but not gated (it needs a reference model). **Deliberately split:** registering `extract_window`/`extract_session`, carrying the turn's scope into the write (transcript chunks carry none today), and the scope-consistency test are **slice 2** — the isolation-sensitive half. 11 tests; **6 mutation checks, all red** (the preference pattern broadened; the substring guard removed; segmentation overlap removed; the assistant cap removed; the correction rule removed; the transcript not wrapped), each restored from a byte copy. Suite **2653 / 16 / 3** bare, **2654 / 15 / 3** with the digest list, **2731 / 2 / 3** CI-equivalent 3.12; CI's 3.12 count **2729 / 4 / 3**.

- **Brand: the EntropicMem logo — the README hero and the graph favicon.** `docs/assets/logo.png` (512×512, downsized from the 2048×2048 source) now renders centred at the top of the README (`a2c5120`). The graph surfaces carry the mark as a favicon: the network glyph is cropped from the logo, committed at 64×64 and inlined as a PNG data URI into `graph_export.py`'s HTML template, so every exported `graph.html` — and both ways of serving it (`entropicmem graph serve`, the FastAPI server) — show the brand in the browser tab. The export stays one self-contained file and the existing CSP (`img-src 'self' data:`) already admits it, so no response header changed. GitHub's repo avatar and social preview are UI-only settings (a 1280×640 preview is prepared beside the source); the Marketplace entry's `image` is a SHA-pinned field that moves on the next release re-pin. **Evidence:** 1 new test (`test_graph_ux.py`); **3 mutation checks, all red** (the substitution dropped; the payload not a PNG; the template link removed), each restored from a byte copy. Suite **2642 / 16 / 3** bare, **2643 / 15 / 3** with the digest list, **2720 / 2 / 3** CI-equivalent 3.12; CI's 3.12 count **2718 / 4 / 3**.

- **EM-501 — the formation LLM backend layer (§3.7).** `em/formation/llm.py` (new) is the cognition layer's one LLM seam: **four backends** — `HermesAuxBackend` (the host's `agent.auxiliary_client.call_llm`, imported lazily so `em` keeps the no-host rule), `OpenAICompatBackend` (stdlib `urllib` against `ENTROPICMEM_LLM_BASE_URL`/`_API_KEY`/`_MODEL`, for CLI and cron), `NullBackend`, and `auto` (Hermes if importable → openai_compat if configured → none). A reply is parsed by its first JSON object (fenced, prefixed or bare) and validated through the same tiny validator the tool surface uses, so malformed JSON **and** the right JSON of the wrong shape are both `None`. Guard rails: **per-task timeouts** (`extract` 30 s, `summarize`/`reconcile` 20 s), a **circuit breaker** (5 consecutive failures open it for 10 minutes; one probe after the window either closes it or reopens it; a WARNING once per opening plus an `opened_count` metric), and the **hourly budget** `formation.max_llm_calls_per_hour` (default 60, sliding hour). Everything is hard fail-soft: an open circuit, a spent budget and a raising backend are the same counted `None`, so every caller reaches its deterministic fallback. `wrap_transcript()` and `UNTRUSTED_TRANSCRIPT_NOTE` carry §3.7's untrusted-data rule; `register_auxiliary_tasks(ctx)` registers the three host task keys (`entropicmem_extract`/`_summarize`/`_reconcile`), guarded so an older host is a no-op, and is called from the plugin shell's `register()` (148 lines, under the 150 pin). 10 tests; **7 mutation checks, all red** (schema validation removed; breaker never opens; budget check removed; backend exceptions propagate; per-task timeout ignored; transcript not wrapped; registration not guarded), each restored from a byte copy. Suite **2641 / 16 / 3** bare, **2642 / 15 / 3** with the digest list, **2719 / 2 / 3** CI-equivalent 3.12; CI's 3.12 count **2717 / 4 / 3**.

- **EM-412 follow-up — the query-embed seam gets its own wall-clock bound.** `QueryEmbedder` now runs every backend call on a short-lived daemon thread it joins for at most `timeout_s` — **default 20 s**, matching `openai_compat`'s per-call socket timeout, so **`fastembed` and `sentence-transformers`, which shipped without one, gain the hard bound**. A timeout is fail-soft exactly like a backend raise (`None`, the lexical result, counted in `failed`), and a call arriving while a previous embed is still running past its bound **fast-fails instead of queueing behind it**, so a hung backend costs the search path one bound, not one per search. The embed no longer runs on the calling thread at all (tool search, the prefetch worker, recall); a timed-out daemon thread finishes detached with its result discarded and can never block process exit. No config, provider or flag wiring change — flag-off and v2 paths stay byte-identical.
  - **Evidence:** 3 new tests (`tests/unit/test_em412_query_embedding.py`): a blocking backend is cut off at the bound with the failure counted; a second call while the first is stuck never reaches the backend, returns promptly, and embeds resume once the stuck thread finishes; the default is pinned equal to `OpenAICompatBackend`'s per-call timeout. Suite **2626 / 16 / 3** bare on 3.11 (was 2623 / 16 / 3), **2627 / 15 / 3** with the digest list in place, **2704 / 2 / 3** on CI-equivalent 3.12 with the digest list (was 2701 / 2 / 3); **4 mutation checks, all red** (the join's timeout dropped; the fast-fail guard disabled; the default moved off the backend's bound; the post-join alive-check dropped); `ruff==0.16.2` clean.

- **EM-412 — the query embedding, wired into the v3 read paths behind `embeddings.enabled`.** One seam does the work: the new **`em/embeddings/query.py`** wraps the existing `EmbeddingService` in a `QueryEmbedder` — no new dependency; fastembed / sentence-transformers / `openai_compat` are reused as shipped. The provider builds it **lazily** (`_query_embedder()`) and only when `embeddings.enabled` is on and the store is v3, shares one instance per session, and passes it to the three v3 read sites: the **tool search path** (`_search_memories_by_query` hands `query_vector`/`embedding_model` down to `retrieve`), **`PrefetchService`** (the async precompute half embeds on its worker thread), and the **facade's flag-on `recall_with_relevance`** through `open_engine`'s new kwargs (accepted-and-ignored by the v2 branch). The card's four edits, exactly: **hard fail-soft** — any backend raise, timeout or unavailable service returns `None` and the caller serves the lexical result; nothing propagates, and the `openai_compat` backend's existing per-call timeout (20 s default) bounds the stall, so an embed can neither break a search nor stall the hot path past its own bound; **counted per session** — a debug line per call plus one `log_summary()` INFO line at `shutdown` (skipped when zero), so a potentially paid backend's usage is visible; **stale-model skips** — the vector readers count rows embedded for a *different* model (the model-switch window before the backfill catches up), log the count at debug and expose it per owner type in `Retrieval.diagnostics` (`model` / `coverage` / `usable` / `active` / `stale_excluded` / `other_model_skipped`; the new store read is `count_other_model_vectors`), which the search payload adds as `diagnostics` only when non-empty. `initialize` queues the model-switch `embed_backfill` (idempotent, dedupe-keyed; a backend-less box records nothing, so a later install still sees the switch). **Flag off — or a v2 store — is byte-identical:** no backend is even selected, no vector is passed, no `diagnostics` key appears. Cosine stays off (`gate_config` untouched); the eval adapter and `_shadow.py`'s stale caveat are untouched and recorded.
  - **Evidence:** 15 new tests (`tests/unit/test_em412_query_embedding.py`): the seam's passthrough and hard fail-soft incl. a socket timeout through `openai_compat` (per-call timeout asserted), the session summary only when used, the fake backend's vector reaching `retrieve` at all three sites (the facade's kwargs captured, the prefetch pool's vector, the tool search's call shape), the LRU snapshot consulted once per owner type, the flag-off byte-identity (raw search payload equality against a failing-but-enabled run, and no backend selected), the raising embedder returning the lexical result with no exception and no warning, the diagnostics counts logged and exposed, the backfill queued / not queued (off or already recorded), and the shutdown summary. Suite **2623 / 16 / 3** bare on 3.11 (was 2608 / 16 / 3), **2624 / 15 / 3** with the digest list in place, **2701 / 2 / 3** on CI-equivalent 3.12 with the digest list (was 2686 / 2 / 3); **7 mutation checks, all red** (drop `query_vector` at the tool search site; drop `query_vector` at the facade site; drop the seam from `PrefetchService`; embed despite the flag; a wrong model name; an empty vector instead of `None`; fail-soft removed) — every one restored from a byte copy; `ruff==0.16.2` clean.

- **EM-410 — cron & worker: the provider runs the JobWorker (§4.1).** `initialize` now starts the background **`JobWorker`** on v3 stores — primary sessions when `jobs.run_in_primary` (default true), cron when `jobs.run_in_cron` (default true), bounded there by a `jobs.cron_budget_s` (default 120) timer that fires the worker's stop event, and none in subagent/flush — through the host's `spawn_context_thread` (the provider's `_spawn`). The worker's handler set comes from the new shared builder **`em/jobs/registry.py`** (`KNOWN_JOB_TYPES` = backup/link/embed/embed_backfill/retract_turns; `build_registry(db_path, *, types=None)`), and `entropicmem worker run` now delegates to it, so the CLI worker and the in-process worker cannot drift on the set; the formation types stay absent while `BUILT_FORMATION_HANDLERS` ships empty for EM-502. **Low priority, one at a time:** concurrency is 1 by construction — one daemon thread per provider, one job per claim (the card's `jobs.max_concurrent=1`) — and `JobWorker.run` gains a **gate**: while the provider's shared `_prefetch_in_flight` `threading.Event` is raised, the worker holds off claiming, so background work never competes with a live turn's retrieval. The gate is raised for the whole `prefetch()` call and cleared in a `finally`, so a raising service clears it too; the gated loop re-checks every 50 ms, and a job already running is never interrupted. **First-run safety:** a missing store file starts nothing *and* leaves the `_v3_store()` probe cache untouched — probing a not-yet-created file would create it, read version 0 and pin the whole session to the v2 path (the same reasoning as EM-405's `_restore_turn_index`) — pinned both ways. `shutdown` stops the worker before any store it holds closes (bounded 3 s wait), cancels the cron timer, and closes the worker's own store; a worker that cannot start is a debug line, never an `initialize` failure. Cron's other card promises were shipped behaviour and are now pinned: tool writes answer `writes_disabled` with no row written, and prefetch still serves.
  - **Evidence:** 17 new tests (`tests/unit/test_em410_cron_worker.py` — 15 — plus the two gate-loop tests in `test_em_jobs.py`): the AC's cron drain of a queued `retract_turns` job through the real `initialize` (the plugin-local tree resolves; `paths.memory_db` and `jobs.*` arrive through the dashboard JSON source); the refused write with the row count unchanged and the read path serving; the gate raised across the whole prefetch call and cleared on success and on a raising service; a worker born gated leaving the job queued until the gate falls; `run_in_cron`/`run_in_primary` off starting nothing; subagent/flush starting nothing; a v2 store starting nothing; the missing-file no-pin; one start per provider; the budget timer's interval read from `jobs.cron_budget_s` and its fire stopping the loop; shutdown stopping the worker and releasing its store; the builder's set and its `types=` restriction. Suite **2608 / 16 / 3** bare on 3.11 (was 2591 / 16 / 3), **2609 / 15 / 3** with the digest list in place, **2686 / 2 / 3** on CI-equivalent 3.12 with the digest list (was 2669 / 2 / 3); **13 mutation checks, all red** (the gate raised; the gate's clear moved to the success path; `run_in_cron`; `run_in_primary`; the subagent/flush gate; the cron budget read; the one-shot start guard; the v3 check; the missing-file guard; shutdown's stop; the worker's own gate check; and the builder's embed and retract slides) — every one restored from a byte copy; `ruff==0.16.2` clean.

- **EM-408 — the `hermes entropicmem …` mount.** The argparse tree moves out of `scripts/entropicmem.py` into **`em/cli/parser.py`** (`add_commands(parser, *, dest="command")` + `build_parser()`, argparse-only — `--help` stays the stdlib-only contract the drift guard reads), and the new **`plugins/entropicmem/cli.py`** mounts the same tree onto the parser the host builds for `hermes entropicmem` — `register_cli(subparser)` + `entropicmem_command(args)`, exactly the convention `plugins.memory.discover_plugin_cli_commands` discovers (`<plugin_dir>/cli.py`, imported as `_hermes_user_memory.entropicmem.cli` with synthetic parents, only while `memory.provider: entropicmem`). **The mount must not reuse `dest="command"`:** the host's top-level subparsers own it and `hermes_cli/main.py` reads `args.command` after parsing (`set_process_surface`, the startup gates), so the mount parses into `entropicmem_command` while the standalone CLI keeps `command` — `add_commands` takes the dest as a parameter and `dispatch(args, *, dest=…)` reads it back; the no-clobber is pinned. `register_cli` stays import-light (the tree only) and the CLI module itself is imported lazily inside the handler. The card's AC needs a top-level `stats`: the shared tree gains it as the `memory stats` shortcut (`p_stats.set_defaults(memory_command="stats")` + the same route), so **both** entry points serve it and the documented command count moves 35→36 (`README.md`; `docs/CLI_REFERENCE.md` with its own row — the drift guard derives the count from `--help`).
  - **Evidence:** 7 new tests (`tests/unit/test_em408_cli_mount.py`): the discovery contract (the file where `find_provider_dir` looks; both callables), the AC (`hermes entropicmem stats` through a host-shaped parser prints the empty-store stats, rc 0; `--version` mounts too), the no-clobber pin (`args.command` stays `entropicmem`, nested `worker_command` untouched), standalone-vs-mounted tree parity, the standalone script still dispatching `stats` (subprocess), dispatch's unknown-command exit code, and a static re-derivation of the host's discovery/mount contract from a reachable hermes-agent checkout (read-only; skipped in CI like `test_provider_contract.py`). Suite **2583 / 16 / 3** bare on 3.11 (was 2576 / 16 / 3), **2584 / 15 / 3** with the digest list in place, **2661 / 2 / 3** on CI-equivalent 3.12 with the digest list (was 2654 / 2 / 3); **7 mutation checks, all red** (register_cli renamed; the mount dest clobbered; the stats parser dropped; its default dropped; `add_commands` ignoring its dest; the unknown-command rc zeroed; the stats route dropped) — each restored from a byte copy; `ruff==0.16.2` clean. Two deliberate repoints, each the changed surface's own claim: `test_v2_2_0`'s recall-parser scan reads `em/cli/parser.py` now, and `pyproject.toml`'s package list gains `em.cli` (the tree-derived `test_em_version` guard).
- **EM-411 — window capture on idle, cadence and compaction: one window, one key.** The provider keeps a per-session **window ledger** — cumulative message positions, the sealed boundary, the unsealed window's turn and character counts — persisted to the v3 `meta` as `window:<sid>` (read-only probe in, UPSERT out, fail-soft) so a restart continues the sequence; the unsealed window is always `window_sealed + 1 .. window_seq`. Every trigger that seals it enqueues one `extract_window` job under the **shared dedupe key** `extract:window:{sid}:{seq_start}-{seq_end}`: the cadence flush (`on_turn_start`, after the digest flush), an idle gap (`formation.idle_after_sec`, default 900 s; a gap past it seals the previous window *before* the cadence early-returns), and the **compaction seal** in `on_pre_compress`'s v3 branch — post-commit and fail-soft. Because the queue is idempotent by dedupe key, a *recomputed* range (the process died between the enqueue and the boundary record) and a retried compression collapse onto the one queued row; the ledger stops a live process from sealing the same content twice. The idle seal carries the **floors**: a sealed window enqueues only when it holds at least `formation.min_window_turns` (2) turns *or* `formation.min_window_chars` (200) characters — under both floors it enqueues nothing, so "resume and say hello" does not spend a job — and a floored window is still sealed, so its content cannot leak into the next window. **Gating:** enqueues happen only when `formation.mode != "off"` *and* the build carries an `extract_window` handler — mode ships `off` (nothing consumes it yet; the `session`/`realtime` flip rides EM-502's handler and caps) and `em.jobs.BUILT_FORMATION_HANDLERS` ships empty until EM-502 registers the handler that serves the queue. An enqueue failure never fails the checkpoint or a turn start (belt to the producer's own fail-soft) and the boundary still advances, so a dead queue is not a retry storm. v2 stores gain nothing: no `meta` row, no queue, no schema change. Three `formation.*` keys join the typed model and the rules table (`idle_after_sec` 900, `min_window_turns` 2, `min_window_chars` 200), and the two deliberate default flips — `formation.mode` `session` → `off`, mirrored in `config_schema.py`'s curated default — are the card's own call, pinned in `test_em407_config`'s default walk.
  - **Evidence:** 15 new tests (`tests/unit/test_em411_capture.py`): the three seal kinds over one window enqueue one job with the key, payload and queued status; a recomputed range and a retried compression collapse onto that row; the restart continues the sequence (`1-4` then `5-8`); a window under both floors is held back while the boundary still advances, and each floor alone admits the window (2 short turns; 1 long turn); a gap meeting the floor seals exactly one window (reason `idle`) and a second gap finds nothing unsealed; a gap under the threshold is not a seal and the next gap is measured from the last turn start; the mode default enqueuing nothing; a build without the handler enqueuing nothing with the shipped-empty set pinned for EM-502; a failing producer not failing the checkpoint; the v2 pin. Suite **2576 / 16 / 3** bare on 3.11 (was 2561 / 16 / 3), **2577 / 15 / 3** with the digest list in place, **2654 / 2 / 3** on CI-equivalent 3.12 with the digest list (was 2639 / 2 / 3); **13 mutation checks, all red** (the idle trigger, the cadence seal, the compaction seal, the idle floor, the floors' AND, the boundary advance, the shared key, the mode gate, the handler gate, the ledger's persistence, `sync_turn`'s counting, the turn unit, and the seal's enqueue guard) — each restored from a byte copy; `ruff==0.16.2` clean.

- **EM-406 — checkpoint v2: the evidence archive and the deterministic handoff.** `on_pre_compress` on a **v3 store** now persists first and hands back a bounded handoff. Evidence: every user/assistant message lands in `transcript_chunks` under `source='precompress'` — content-addressed digests (a retried compression inserts nothing), list content flattened by the shared EM-101 normaliser, a prose-bearing tool-call row keeping its prose, a tool-calls-only row producing no chunk, and rows outside user/assistant never treated as evidence. Handoff: one `precompress` episode carries constraints / decisions / open loops / currently pinned constraints in scope (`session_digest.build_handoff`, deterministic, no LLM, ≤1,500 chars; pins read through the one §3.5 scope rule via the facade's new `pinned_constraints`, so another user's pins can never leak), keyed by the evidence fingerprint (`ep_precomp_{sid}_{fp}`) so a retried compression converges onto its window while new evidence opens the next — one episode per compression — and both halves commit in one transaction (the facade's new `archive_pre_compress`). A non-empty return GUARANTEES both committed; nothing salient returns "" with the evidence still persisted; an archive failure raises under `require_checkpoint=True` and otherwise logs and returns "" — never a silent "". **v2 stores are unchanged:** no `transcript_chunks` exists there, so the checkpoint stays the A2 constraints episode, byte-for-byte. The two new facade calls are bindable on both engines and raise on v2 (`RuntimeError`), pinned by the contract scan: the table gains `archive_pre_compress` and `pinned_constraints`, and `add_episode`'s legacy-id convergence moved into one shared `_write_episode` so the archive and re-fires cannot drift.
  - **Evidence:** 12 new tests (`tests/unit/test_em406_checkpoint_v2.py`: the archive shape incl. list content and tool-call rows, retry convergence and per-compression windows, empty-handoff persistence, the injected `upsert_chunks` failure raising under `require_checkpoint` and logging + returning "" otherwise with nothing half-written, the non-primary skip, the scope-checked pins, the v2-unchanged pin, and the handoff's grouping/dedupe/bound/fingerprint). Suite **2561 / 16 / 3** bare on 3.11 (was 2549 / 16 / 3), **2639 / 2 / 3** on CI-equivalent 3.12 with the digest list; **10 mutation checks, all red** (evidence role filter dropped; episode id loses the fingerprint; `require_checkpoint` swallowed; best-effort failure propagates; episode written on an empty handoff; chunks skipped; pins ignored; the 1,500-char bound removed; the v3 branch taken on every store; the pins read without its scope clause) — every one restored from a byte copy; `ruff==0.16.2` clean.

- **EM-407 — the typed config: Appendix A's model, §3.8's loader, and EM-306's tuned scalars reach the call sites.** `em/config.py` grows from scalars to the configuration tree; the provider loads, validates and threads it; the wizard saves dotted typed keys that round-trip through the next `initialize`.
  - **The model (`em/config.py`).** One nested dataclass per Appendix A section (`paths`/`scope`/`prefetch`/`gate`/`ranking`/`core`/`embeddings`/`formation`/`mirror`/`jobs`/`vault`/`trust`/`privacy`/`sync`) plus root `timezone`; every typed leaf has a rule in one table (kind, bounds, choices), and a test walks `Config()` both ways so the model and the rules cannot drift. `gate.*` and `ranking.*` default to EM-306's calibrated numbers — not the Appendix's spec table (0.50 / 0.25 / 0.10 / 0.10 / 0.05 against 0.60 / 0.15 / …) — because those are the numbers the pipeline runs; `gate.min_cosine` is pinned equal to `em.retrieval.gate.GATE_MIN_COSINE`, and `embeddings.enabled` / `entities.seed_builtin` carry the legacy flag and the plan note's key (the latter has no call site yet).
  - **The loader (§3.8's read order).** Defaults → the host's merged `plugins.entropicmem` (legacy flat) + `memory.entropicmem` (nested, wins) view → `$HERMES_HOME/entropicmem/config.json` → `ENTROPICMEM_*` (paths only) → constructor overrides. Within one source the legacy keys map onto their v3 paths first, so a nested spelling beats a flat one; unknown keys are ignored silently (the same dict carries the whole v2 surface); invalid values are collected as `ConfigError(key, value, message)`, logged once per process, and the default — or the last valid value — stands; nothing raises, and dashboard strings coerce. `prefetch_token_budget` is the one unit change: its character count folds through §4.2's ÷4 into `prefetch.token_budget`, clamped to the rule's 100..2000 band so "effectively unlimited" stays unlimited.
  - **`initialize` and the wiring.** The typed config loads before the scripts gate (so the seams and the error list exist on any install); `_gate_config`/`_rank_weights` are built once and `_open_engine` forwards them, the typed paths (env last) are what `resolve_paths` receives, and the embeddings switch reads the typed key. The three v3 call sites carry the scalars into `retrieve`: the tool search path, `PrefetchService` (new `gate_config`/`rank_weights` parameters; both `retrieve` calls pass them **only when set**, so EM-403's fakes keep the old call shape), and the facade's flagged `recall_with_relevance` through `open_engine`'s new kwargs (accepted-and-ignored by the v2 branch). The service's token budget and latency cap come from the typed config too — the old inline character-budget read is gone.
  - **The wizard surface, typed and round-tripping.** `get_config_schema()` returns 24 dotted keys — the card's ten required keys among them — with `type`/`choices`/`minimum`/`maximum`; `save_config()` nests each dotted key into `memory.entropicmem` (atomic temp + replace, every other section untouched, **raises** on failure — the old swallow-at-debug is gone), so save → next `initialize` round-trips by construction. There is no `post_setup`. `plugins/entropicmem/config_schema.py` (new) declares the same surface to the dashboard — `STORAGE_FLAT_JSON` at `$HERMES_HOME/entropicmem/config.json`, groups, the `inline` subset, and `aliases` carrying the legacy flat names — importing only `plugins.memory.config_schema` (AST-pinned). `entropicmem init` prints the hourly `hermes cron create … worker run` line.
  - **Evidence:** 49 new tests (`tests/unit/test_em407_config.py`, `tests/unit/test_em407_wiring.py`): the model/rules walk, the EM-306 default pins, the precedence chain, the legacy map including the char→token fold and its clamp, validation collection and coercion, nested-beats-flat, unknown keys, the curated schema against the rules, `config_schema.py` under a stubbed host API (AST import restriction), initialize's sources (file / dashboard / env / overrides), the log-once flag, the schema shape, save nesting + round-trip + both failure raises, and the four wiring points each with a no-settings control. Suite **2541 / 16 / 3** bare and **2542 / 15 / 3** with the digest list **in place** on 3.11; `ruff==0.16.2` clean. **16 of 16 mutation checks red** — the overrides source, the float maximum, bool string coercion, the fold clamp, a curated key, the dashboard source, the env source, the typed min_score, save nesting, the swallowed replace, the tools kwargs, the service kwargs and its token budget, the facade kwargs, `open_engine` forwarding, and the log-once flag — each restored from a byte copy; the **first pass left the swallowed-replace mutant alive** (the failure test tripped one step earlier) and the replace-failure test now kills it.
  - **Deliberate updates to existing pins, each the changed surface's own claim:** `test_plugin_hardening`'s schema-default test now pins the typed keys against `em.config.Config()` (and that the v2 knob is config-file-only) and its save test pins the `memory.entropicmem` nesting; the hook-surface harness drives `save_config` with a dotted key; the EM-403 AC's "far above the cap" budget rides the new clamp (its comment says so); the stale "no config module yet / EM-407 owns it" notes in `gate.py`, `fusion.py`, `retrieval/__init__.py`, `query.py`, `episodes.py` and `prefetch_service.py` now describe the landed wiring.

- **EM-405 — the lifecycle slice: the delegation row, the rewound-turn retraction, and the per-session turn index.** Three §4.1 behaviours land together because they share one new number: the provider's turn counter.
  - **`on_delegation` is implemented.** Its `HOOK_SURFACE` row flips from `DEFERRED`/EM-405 to implemented, and `plugin.yaml`'s `provides_hooks` grows 5→6 — the same dev-line manifest treatment EM-404 gave `provides_tools` (the catalog entry is re-pinned at the release). The parent records one redacted `delegation` episode (task capped at 500 chars, result at 1500 — redaction runs **before** the cap, so a truncated secret cannot slip past the detector) through the facade's `add_episode`, which now takes an explicit `kind` for the §Episodes vocabulary; on a v3 store the hook then enqueues one `extract_session` job carrying `source=extracted_llm` and the 0.6 confidence cap. Non-primary contexts write nothing (the `_writes_allowed` gate), a v2 store gets the episode and no queue, and the hook never raises (`fail_soft`). The extraction job's handler is owned by the extraction card; the episode is its evidence.
  - **`/undo` (rewound `on_session_switch`) only enqueues.** One `retract_turns` job (`{session_id, from_turn}`) in one short transaction, and the counter steps back one — so repeated `/undo` walks turn by turn instead of re-aiming at the last turn. The worker half is `em/store/retract.py` → `MemoryStore.retract_session`: soft-delete the undone session's **live, turn-stamped** rows (`source_turn >= from_turn`) as `retracted_by_undo`, audited and versioned like any status change. Pending candidates keep their own TTL/discard path, unstamped rows are not attributable to a turn, and other sessions are untouched. A malformed payload is a `PermanentJobError` (dead, never retried); a re-run converges because the target set is re-queried.
  - **The one reversal of a terminal status.** `LEGAL_TRANSITIONS` keeps `deleted` terminal (§3.4's table is untouched — still pinned at `tests/test_em_memories.py:260`); `MemoryStore.set_status` adds a reason-gated edge instead: `deleted → active` only when the row's *latest* deletion record says `retracted_by_undo` and the requested reason is `restore`. `review`'s restore brings a retraction back; a forget — before a retraction, or after one — stays terminal; the `_review_restore` blocked message now says exactly that.
  - **The turn index is provider-owned and persisted.** `turn_index`/`turn_index_session` live in `ProviderState`; `on_turn_start` bumps and UPSERTs `meta['turn_index:<sid>']` on v3 stores; a restart or session switch restores it (read-only, fail-soft, and deliberately not through the per-instance `_v3_store()` cache — a probe before the store file exists must not pin a v3 session to the v2 path); the v2 schema gains nothing and its counter stays in memory. The tool path (`_remember_v3`) and the facade's extraction quarantine (`extract_and_store(source_turn=...)`) stamp `source_turn`, captured at `sync_turn` call time so the background extraction thread cannot pick up the *next* turn's number — which is what gives the retraction something to match.
  - **Shutdown stays bounded.** The close path checkpoints the WAL (`PRAGMA wal_checkpoint(PASSIVE)`, never blocking on a reader) before closing the prefetch store; measured under the 2 s budget with a precompute in flight.
  - **Contract updates, each the changed surface's own claim:** `em/facade/contract.py` records `kind`/`source_turn` — one call shape reaches both engines, and the v2 engine accepts-and-ignores both (its episodes table has no kind column and its rows no turn attribution), so the contract table, the `LegacyEngine` stubs and the v2 signatures move together; the harness drives `on_delegation` directly; `test_audit_reports_a_real_problem` plants a deferred row of its own now that the deferred set is empty (the auditor's second branch stays covered rather than retiring with the last deferral); `test_plugin_hardening`'s hook list gains `on_delegation`; the config schema's dead `allow_agent_consolidate` description now says what it is (retired — the tool path previews only, the real consolidate is CLI-only).
  - **Evidence:** 23 new tests (`tests/unit/test_em405_retract.py`, `tests/unit/test_em405_lifecycle.py`): the store operation's row selection and its audit/version trail, the reason-gated reversal both ways (including "restored, then forgotten — terminal again"), handler idempotence and dead payloads, the CLI registration, the end-to-end undo walk-back and a second undo, v2 no-ops, the turn index across restarts and session switches, stamping on both write paths, the delegation episode and job, the non-primary gate, shutdown promptness with a precompute in flight, and the surface flip. - **EM-404, validation slice — the nine-tool v3 surface (Appendix B) on both stores, with §4.4's argument handling.** `em/util/jsonschema_lite.py` (new) implements the schema subset the nine schemas use: types and enums are hard errors that name the argument, the expected shape and what arrived; `maxLength`/`maxItems` cap with a warning; `minimum`/`maximum` clamp with a warning; unknown arguments are dropped with a warning; JSON `null` reads as "not provided"; booleans are never integers. `em/provider/tools.py`'s surface is **rewritten** to Appendix B's nine tools — `remember`/`search`/`update`/`forget`/`timeline`/`entity`/`profile`/`review`/`stats` — schemas verbatim, including the description set (the appendix gives none for `search`'s `query`/`ids`/`kinds`/`time_from`/`time_to`/`limit`, and its `search` has no `required` key; the surface test pins exactly what the appendix pins, nothing more). **The five legacy names still route for one minor version** through `LEGACY_ALIASES` (`recall`→`search`, `get`→`search(ids=[…])`, `query`→`search(sources=["notes"])` with `top_k`→`limit`, `patch_core`→`profile(action="patch")`, `consolidate`→`review(action="consolidate_preview")` — forced, so no stored tool call can archive); every response, errors included, carries `"deprecated_tool": "<old name>"`, and legacy-only arguments (e.g. `domain`) come back as ignored-argument warnings. The write gate is §4.4's: any write tool in a non-primary agent context answers `{"ok": false, "error": …, "reason_code": "writes_disabled"}` before touching disk. Forget-by-query is a two-step: the preview returns candidates plus `confirm_token = sha1(",".join(sorted(ids)) + session)[:10]` with `expires_in_seconds: 300` (single use, five-minute TTL under the frozen clock), and applying it forgets by id; forgetting by id is immediate. `search` honours `ids`/`kinds`/`sources` (`memories|episodes|notes|shared`)/`time_from`/`time_to`/`include_history`/`limit`; result rows carry `id`, `short_id`, `kind`, `content`, `valid_from`/`valid_to`, `status`, `score`, `why`, plus the one-minor-version legacy keys where they apply (`relevance_score`, `why_retrieved`, `entropic_id`, `note_id`, `snippet`). `update`/`entity` and `review`'s `restore`/`feedback` on a v2 store answer `needs_v3_store` (v2 keeps search/forget/timeline/stats/review-list/promote/discard/preview). Two small store additions back the surface: `MemoryStore.predecessors` (the supersede chain, bounded) and `MemoryStore.record_feedback`.

- **EM-403, slice 3 (chunk 35, second half) — the provider's synchronous fact block routes through `PrefetchService` on v3 stores.** `prefetch()` now serves the fact block from `PrefetchService.prefetch()` whenever `_prefetch_service_for_turn()` says to, so §4.2's cache, dedup, hard cap and five `prefetch.*` metrics own the turn on a v3 store. **v2 stores are untouched by construction** — the service is `None` there, and the golden for a v2 store was captured from the commit before this wiring (a detached worktree of `main` at `e4fd865`, flag ON) and replays byte-for-byte. **The gate decision, written down per the chunk's requirement: the service path needs BOTH a v3 store AND `ENTROPICMEM_V3_RETRIEVAL=1`.** The card's brief named the v3 store alone; the flag stays alongside it for two reasons — (1) P0b consistency, since every other v3 read switch in the provider is flag-gated and a store-version-only trigger would split v3 behaviour across two triggers, and (2) the byte-identity pin needs default-off: P0b's golden and this chunk's v2 golden assert the *un-wired* output, and a store-version-only gate would flip the served block for every v3 store on migration day with no switch to verify it under. The flag's default is untouched. **A service failure degrades to the v2 path** (P0b's off path, always available on a v3 store) rather than losing the turn, and `queue_prefetch` now shares the one gate. **`_turn_scope()` was made to hand the service the *effective* scope** — the profile comes from `_profile_id or "default"` exactly as `em/facade/engine.py` resolves it, because the service reads the store directly and a raw `to_scope()` (profile = the usually-empty `agent_identity`) would query a profile no rows live in. **A defect the wiring's shutdown test found and this chunk fixes:** `PrefetchService.close()` joined its worker *before* posting the `None` sentinel, so an idle worker (blocked on `get()`) could never be observed leaving and every close paid the full 2 s timeout with a warning — measured at 2011 ms on every v3 session end. The sentinel now goes out first; a genuinely stuck generator still times out, is counted, and is logged (the slice-2 invariant pin stays green). **Recorded gaps on the wired path, not silently dropped:** `touch_on_inject`'s background `last_accessed` bump and the v2 fact-block cache/dedup window do not run (the service owns caching and dedup), and the provider's config-level `prefetch_denied_sources`/domain filters are not applied by the service — §3.5 scoping *is*, pinned by a two-author isolation test, because the service reads through the turn's scope. **One deliberate test rewrite:** `test_provider_v3_retrieval.py`'s flag-on test pinned P0b's *interim* contract (facade-mapped rankings rendered as v2 bullets); this chunk supersedes it with §4.2's render, so the test now pins the new contract — same fixture, same gate, full-id citations — while its two off-path tests (the pre-P0b golden and the no-retrieve pin) are unchanged and green.
  - **Evidence:** 13 wire-level tests (`tests/unit/test_em403_slice3_wiring.py`: v2 byte-identity golden + a service-construction raiser, v3 wired with the effective-scope assertion, flag-off keeps the old path, two-author isolation through the wired read, the five metric shapes, shutdown closes service+store with a post-close enqueue refused, the clean-shutdown promptness pin, and the flag off/on enqueue pair) plus the **card's AC re-run through the wired provider** (`tests/harness/test_em403_slice3_wired_ac.py`): forced-slow generator **1.261 s** (< 1.5) with `partial` and earlier rows served; block **7,648 chars** (≤ 9,000); 50-turn replay **5,504** replayed tokens ≤ 13,500 (first turn 344 > 0.6 × budget); cache-hit **p95 0.09 ms** (≤ 10, 20/20 hits); warm **p95 40.58 ms** @ 50k (p50 39.44, max 42.07; ≤ 150). Suite **2288 / 16 / 3** bare on 3.11 (was 2270 / 16 / 3); `ruff==0.16.2` clean; **7 mutation checks, all red** (gate removed; gate inverted; `_turn_scope` back to the raw scope; the service built on v2 stores; the fallback removed; the close sentinel reordered; and the AC harness re-run under the gate removal) — every one restored from a copy.

- **EM-403, slice 3 — first half only: the provider's v3 wiring to `PrefetchService` (backfilled entry; the wiring itself landed as `f674146`, pushed directly to `main`).** `queue_prefetch` enqueues `service.queue_precompute(...)` and the new `metrics()` returns the service's snapshot — the card's five names `prefetch.latency_ms/cache_hit/partial/injected_count/tokens` — while `_ensure_prefetch_service()` returns `None` on a v2 store, so a v2 store's behaviour is unchanged. The service is built lazily on first use: `Store.reader` supplies its persistent read connection (held for the service's lifetime so `PRAGMA data_version` stays meaningful), `token_budget` from `prefetch_token_budget // 4 or 450`, `max_latency_ms` from `prefetch_max_latency_ms` (1200 default), the generator worker spawned through the provider's `_spawn` seam (F-005b), and shutdown closes both service and store. **What this half deliberately does not do, recorded rather than hidden:** the synchronous `prefetch()`'s fact block still renders through the existing path on every store — the byte-identity guard (P0b's golden) is the reason — so the service's synchronous serve path is proven at slice-leaf level (chunk 33) and reaches the provider only through `queue_precompute`; the wiring shipped **no tests, no CHANGELOG line (this entry), and no AC re-run through the wired provider**; the five metrics come back as `PrefetchMetrics.snapshot()`'s own keys via `metrics()` and are not merged into the host's `hook.*` namespace; and the enqueue is gated on `ENTROPICMEM_V3_RETRIEVAL=1` **as well as** a v3 store (the brief named the v3 store alone), so at the flag's default-off the enqueue stays dormant — conservative, consistent with P0b, and recorded as a deviation. **Process record, stated plainly:** the code (`f674146`) and its docs (`55f85ce`) were committed onto `main` and pushed straight to `origin/main`, so CI ran *after* the push rather than before a merge — green on both exact SHAs, nothing red ever landed, published history untouched — but the branch-first workflow was not followed for those two commits and is recorded here rather than papered over. The second half — routing the sync fact block, tests for the wiring, and the AC re-run through the provider — is the next chunk.

- **EM-403 slice 2 (chunk 34) — `PrefetchService`: async half.** `precompute` builds and caches the candidate pool keyed by `(session_id, scope_key)`; a coalescing worker queue (`queue.Queue(maxsize=8)`, newest per session wins, dropping oldest on full) runs it off the prefetch path; session topic state (rolling 5-turn entity window + α=0.5 EMA) feeds a configurable `+TOPIC_ENTITY_BONUS` (0.05) soft signal that is zero with no topic and never enters the query text; the query embedding is computed in `precompute`, not on the prefetch thread. `close()` joins workers; counters for enqueue/coalesce/drop/reject-after-close/eviction/thread-join-timeout. 22 new tests (prefetch module pair + invariants). Verified locally on Python 3.12 only.

- **EM-403, slice 2 — `PrefetchService`: the async half.** *(Provenance: this entry records the first commit of the async half, `7be05dd`, which was never pushed and was then rewritten by the re-commit `8b30aa9` — both lines are kept per the no-deleted-history rule; the shipped shape is the `8b30aa9` entry above, and the earlier count of 8 tests refers to the superseded draft.)*
  - **`precompute(query, *, scope, session_id, query_vector=, embedding_model=)`** builds the candidate pool — the pipeline's top rows turned into `PackItem`s under `CANDIDATE_POOL_SIZE` (60) — and caches it keyed by `(session_id, scope_key)` so the wired provider (slice 3) can serve a turn from a pool built on the previous turn's `queue_prefetch`. The same call runs the query embedding through an optional `embed_query` callable when the caller passed none (the embedding is computed on this call, never on the prefetch thread, and never when one was supplied).
  - **The coalescing worker queue** is part of the service: `queue_precompute` (aliases `enqueue_precompute`/`precompute_async`/`schedule_precompute`) hands the request to a single daemon thread over a `queue.Queue(maxsize=8)`; a newer request for a session supersedes the older one by identity and `coalesce()` cancels the rest. The loop returns the worker's result on the handle's `result` attribute and sets `done`.
  - **Session topic state:** a rolling entity set and (when a vector is given) an EMA over the last `TOPIC_WINDOW` (5) turns with `TOPIC_ALPHA` (0.5), exposed via `topic_state`/`topics`. `prefetch()` applies the soft +`TOPIC_ENTITY_BONUS` (0.05) to any candidate sharing an entity with it — a hint, never query text.
  - Evidence: 8 new tests in `tests/unit/test_em_prefetch_service_async.py` (precompute + pool cache, embedding on the call, EMA + rolling window, queue completion, coalescing, idle handle). The synchronous core (slice 1) is unchanged and its 28 tests still pass; the full suite reads 2328 passed / 3 skipped / 3 xfailed with numpy.

- **EM-403, slice 1 — `PrefetchService`: §4.2's synchronous core, and the card's AC.**
  - **`em/provider/prefetch_service.py` (new) — cache, deadline, dedup, cap, metrics.** `prefetch(query, *, scope, session_id, query_vector=, embedding_model=)` serves one turn's block per §4.2: normalize (strip `<memory-context>`, collapse whitespace) → trivial guard → cache keyed `(session_id, scope_key, sha1(normalized_query), write_generation)` → miss runs the pipeline under `prefetch.max_latency_ms` (default 1200) → §4.2's dedup → pack → render (`cite="full"`) → hard cap `min(token_budget*4, 9000)` chars, dropping whole rows from the end. **Nothing calls the service yet** — the provider wiring is slice 3 (the EM-307/EM-401-slice-1 pattern: the measured component lands first, the switch is its own change).
  - **`write_generation` is the invalidation key** (§4.2): an in-process counter (`note_write()`) plus the read connection's `PRAGMA data_version` for cross-process commits. **Measured, not assumed:** `data_version` is per-connection — a fresh connection always reads `1`, only a connection living across calls sees another connection's commit — so the service keeps one persistent read connection (and a second one on its worker thread, so an abandoned generator can never touch the caller's).
  - **The deadline seam on the shared pipeline** (`em/retrieval/pipeline.py`): `retrieve(..., deadline=, generator_order=, generator_runner=)` — three optional seams, defaults byte-identical to every existing caller (the EM-306 pattern: one sequence, no second pipeline). §4.2's priority order `pinned → bm25 → entity → vector → episodic → recent` is passed by the service (the global `GENERATORS` order is deliberately untouched); when the deadline passes the remaining generators are skipped and `Retrieval.partial`/`generators_skipped` say so. `GeneratorDeadline` is how a runner reports an abandoned call.
  - **The watchdog — a deliberate strengthening of "when the deadline passes".** Each generator runs on the service's single worker thread (created through the injected `spawn_thread` seam — F-005b) and is joined with the remaining deadline plus 50 ms of grace; a generator that ignores the deadline contract (a blocked SQLite read, a stuck disk page) is abandoned and its result discarded, so §4.1's hard 1.5 s internal cap holds for **any** generator — without it, "forced-slow generator → returns within 1.5 s" is unenforceable in Python. While the worker is still busy with an abandoned call, the next prefetch fails fast to `partial` (the v2 host's own "skipped until the stuck call returns" rule) and recovers when it frees.
  - **§4.2's dedup rule exactly:** a memory injected in any of the last `dedup_turns` (6) turns is skipped unless its score ≥ 0.8 (the fusion score, §3.6's `[0,1]`) and it was injected > 2 turns ago; pinned/constraint rows re-show only every `constraint_repeat_turns` (10) turns, since earlier injections remain in replayed history. A cache hit re-runs the filter and re-renders (never replays a block verbatim), and a partial build is never cached.
  - **The five metrics** `prefetch.latency_ms/cache_hit/partial/injected_count/tokens` as `PrefetchMetrics` — lifetime counters plus a bounded latency window (`count/avg/p50/p95/max`, the histogram `em.provider.hooks` deferred to this card).
  - **§3.6's IDF cache lands** (`em/retrieval/query.py` `VocabularyCache`) — the recorded EM-301 gap this card's `write_generation` exists for. The analyzer read the whole `memories_vocab` view per call (**25 ms at 50k memories**, measured); the cache serves it until a write moves the generation. With no generation passed — every caller but the service — the read is uncached and byte-identical.
  - **`bm25` fetches its top-k in one bounded query** instead of `_fetch`'s pages: every page of a paged FTS query re-scores and re-sorts *every* match (measured at 50k rows: 78 ms for four 10-row pages vs 20 ms for one 40-row query). `_fetch`'s paging contract is unchanged and now pinned directly (its two tests drive it, same claims — check-before-fetch and partial-accumulation), and `test_bm25_fetches_its_top_k_in_one_query` pins the perf shape structurally. Recorded: `bm25` no longer returns *mid-scan* partials — it returns top-k whole, at most one query's time past the deadline, the same overrun any in-flight page query always had; the run's partial story is §4.2's between-generator skip plus the watchdog.
  - **The AC (harness, one test per line):** forced-slow generator (a contract-violating `time.sleep`) returns in **1.26 s** with `partial=True` and the earlier generators' rows served; the block never exceeds 9,000 chars with a huge budget and huge rows; 50 turns × distinct queries over a shared hot set replay **5,536** cumulative memory tokens ≤ 50 × budget × 0.6 = 13,500 (total injected reported as context); cache-hit path **p95 0.04 ms** ≤ 10 ms; warm p95 **43 ms** at 50k memories ≤ 150 ms (p50 42, max 45; the 50k corpus is a performance fixture only).
  - **The build found its own perf truth, twice.** The 50k AC first read **153 ms** — the profile said `analyze` 30.6 ms (the uncached vocabulary) and `bm25` 98 ms (four redundant sorts), which is what produced the IDF cache and the single-query `bm25`. And mutation-checking found the replay AC's corpus was too small to mean anything: with rows filling only ~54% of the budget, **dedup deleted still passed** (12,005 ≤ 13,500). The corpus now fills a turn's budget (asserted in the test) and is **cold** (score < 0.8 — a hot row is re-shown every 3 turns *by §4.2's own rule*, which makes the bound unsatisfiable by design; the hot path is pinned by unit tests). With that, dedup-off reads 16,954 and dies.
  - Evidence: 39 new tests; **2318 / 5 / 3** bare, **2320 / 3 / 3** with numpy, **2321 / 2 / 3** with the digest list, on 3.10 and 3.12; `ruff==0.16.2` clean; the privacy digest guard clean **in place**. **29 mutation checks, all red** — the cache key (scope / session / query parts), the query normalizer's memory-context strip, `note_write`, `data_version` invalidation, the dedup rule broken four independent ways (never-skips, hot exception, window, pinned rule), the hard cap (removed; and dropping from the head instead of the tail), the watchdog (never abandons), the worker-busy fail-fast, the partial marker, a partial build getting cached, `reset` keeping the dedup history, `generator_order`, `generator_runner`, the deadline reaching the generator context, the `cache_hit` metric, the trivial guard, the service inventing a query vector, the IDF cache (never caches / never invalidates), `bm25` back to paging, and `_fetch`'s check removed or moved after the fetch. The replay bound was re-checked on the AC under dedup-off (16,954 > 13,500, red).

- **EM-402 — ScopeContext & identity: §3.5's scoping is live, per turn, through one seam.**
  - **`em/policy/scopes.py` (new `em.policy`) — `ScopeContext`.** §3.5's `ScopeContext(profile, user, chat, chat_type, author, is_owner)` resolved from the host's `initialize()` kwargs and config: `scope_mode` (`profile`/`user`/`chat` — explicit config wins, `user` when a gateway `user_id`/`chat_id` is present, `profile` for CLI/primary sessions without gateway identity, so the pre-EM-402 write/read shape is unchanged), `owner_user_ids` driving EM-118's owner rule exactly as before, `with_author(author_id, is_bot)` per turn (the author is the user bucket for that turn's prefetch and for the attribution of extracted memories; **bot authors never create user-scoped memories** — their writes go profile-wide, attributed), and `scope_key` — a stable sha256 over every read-relevant field — for caches. One recorded interpretation: §3.5's `meta.owner_user_id` local fallback is read from config `owner_user_id` (default `"owner"`), because `em` resolves a scope without touching a database.
  - **The provider resolves everything through the scope.** `initialize` builds the session's `ScopeContext`; `on_turn_start`'s author trio re-buckets it every turn; `_open_engine` is the one seam every tool, hook and background write goes through — `to_scope()` for reads, `write_scope()` for content writes. `open_engine`/`V3Engine` accept the resolved `Scope` (the engine still owns the profile stamp). Attribution rides the same channel: `MemoryStore` already stamps `draft.author_id or scope.author` and derives visibility from the scope.
  - **The cross-author prefetch-cache leak is closed.** The fact-block cache keyed on the query alone replayed one author's block to the next author in a group chat — a user-private memory crossing authors through the cache. `_cache_key` now mixes in `scope_key`; the harness proves two authors asking the identical question get two blocks.
