  - **`identity_signature()` lands** (`em/provider/identity.py`): `{"provider": "entropicmem", "schema": 3, "scope_mode": <resolved>}`, read from the config file **only** (`memory.entropicmem` merged over `plugins.entropicmem`, mirroring `load_plugin_config` so the signature and the session cannot disagree), cached by `(path, mtime, size)` because the host calls it on every inbound message, no DB, safe on an uninitialized instance — and every failure mode (missing home, missing or unparseable config, no PyYAML) degrades to the default signature instead of raising. `HOOK_SURFACE`'s deferred set shrinks to `on_delegation` (EM-405); `plugin.yaml` is untouched (the catalog rule).
  - **The AC.** *Group-chat harness (3 human authors + one bot, one chat, a v3 store):* each author's memory is attributed to that author (`scope_user`, `visibility='user'`, `author_id`), a bot author's memory lands profile-wide and attributed, and one author's user-private memory never reaches another author's prefetch — **including through the cache**. *`multi_user` eval category = 1.0* on its own isolation surface (`evals/datasets_multi_user/isolation.jsonl`, `Turn.as_user`, the v3 adapter loading `scope_user` and querying through the scope): recall@5 1.0, abstain_correct 1.0, must_not_ok 1.0, substring 1.0, noise 0.0 across 8 turns (committed baseline `evals/baselines/v3-multi-user.json`, lexical).
  - **Recorded deviation from the card's literal AC wording (owner's ruling, 2026-10-13, the isolation reading):** the **frozen hard suite's** `multi_user` category is untouched and reported as-is — recall@5 **0.90 lexical / 0.933 vector**. Its two misses are `multi_user_shared_01` turns 1 and 3, where *"when is the wedding?"* must retrieve *"The event date is set for June 1, 2030"* — **zero shared terms**, missed even by `BAAI/bge-small-en-v1.5`; that is cross-memory coreference (the `ageing` class), a retrieval gap scoping cannot close. Its turns carry no `must_not` and no asker, so as written the category measures retrieval, not isolation. The frozen suites' `scope_user` values no adapter ever honoured; they now load in §3.5 buckets **only** when a scenario's turns name askers (`Scenario.scoped`), so every frozen baseline is byte-identical (verified category-by-category against `v3-hard.json`).
  - **One recorded finding, deliberately not changed:** a `sensitive`/`secret`-tier row is owner-only **even to its own user** under the pinned `row_is_owner_only` rule (`test_em_owner_only_reads`), which is stricter than §3.5's "non-owners see … plus their own". Widening a privacy read rule is the owner's call (like Chunk 13), so it stays as pinned and is recorded for a later ruling.
  - Evidence: 53 new tests; **2279 / 5 / 3** bare and **2281 / 3 / 3** with numpy on **Python 3.10 and 3.12**; `ruff==0.16.2` clean; the privacy digest guard clean **in place**. **21 mutation checks, all red**: the author re-bucketing, a constant `scope_key`, `resolve_scope_mode` flattened, the bot write rule, profile mode keeping the gateway user; the identity cache not re-reading a changed file, re-parsing an unchanged one, the merge order, a parse failure that raises, a missing file that raises; `on_turn_start` not re-bucketing, the cache key losing the scope, `_open_engine` ignoring the passed scope, `initialize` resolving nothing (a survivor first — the resolution path had no test until one was written); the eval load ignoring `scope_user`, `set_turn_scope` ignoring the asker, `score_turn` skipping the seam, and the leak class broken three independent ways (`scope_sql`'s user clause, `_in_scope`'s user clause, and the scoped load).

- **EM-401, slice 1 — the §4.1 hook skeleton, the pinned host contract, and a harness that drives every hook.**
  - **`em/provider/hooks.py` — `fail_soft`.** Every hook the host calls is wrapped: timed, counted (`hook.<name>` metrics), over-budget logged at WARNING, and exceptions swallowed — logging the exception **type only**, because a tool handler's message can carry the user's text (the detail stays at DEBUG). **Fail-closed, and deliberately stronger than the card's carve-out:** `on_pre_compress`, `initialize`, `save_config` and `backup_paths` re-raise, because §4.6 counts *any* non-raising return as a successful checkpoint — swallowing a failed one claims a persist that never happened. The hook that *should* fail soft (a skipped or absent checkpoint) does so inside its own body, returning `""`. Recorded as a deviation from the card's blanket "swallows exceptions".
  - **`em/provider/state.py` — `ProviderState`.** Session id, agent context, last recall count and the core baseline in one holder; the provider's `_session_id` / `_agent_context` / `_core_baseline` are now properties over it, so no call site changed and no second copy can drift. `writes_allowed()` keeps the primary-context rule in one place.
  - **`em/provider/provider.py` — the §4.1 surface table.** One row per hook with its thread, its budget and its status. `on_delegation` (EM-405) and `identity_signature` (EM-402) are the two **deferred** rows, each naming its card and why. `recall_status` and `unavailable_reason` are newly implemented, and the injected count now rides with the cached prefetch block so a cache hit can still report what it injected.
  - **The contract test (EM-401's AC).** `tests/test_provider_contract.py` enumerates `MemoryProvider` from the pinned hermes-agent — pinned as a dated copy at `tests/harness/pinned_memory_provider.json`, so CI needs no host, and **re-derived and compared against the real host whenever it is reachable** — then asserts every method is implemented or explicitly deferred, that the provider's signatures still bind the host's calls, that `pre_compress_checkpoint_api_version = 2`, and that `plugin.yaml`'s `provides_hooks` is exactly the hook set the provider has (the catalog review verifies those lists).
  - **The harness (EM-401's other AC).** `tests/harness/test_provider_hook_surface.py` drives every §4.1 hook — the turn path through FakeHost with an author, the boundary and tool hooks directly — and reads the `fail_soft` metrics to prove each one ran **and none recorded an error**. `on_turn_start` now names the §4.1 author trio, which is what makes the host pass it.
  - **`_backend.bootstrap_scripts_path()`** puts the plugin's own scripts dir on `sys.path` at import time (the same candidates `resolve_scripts_dir` uses), so `em.provider.*` can be imported before `initialize()` has resolved a home.
  - **Slice 2 — the class moves, and the package becomes a shell.** `em/provider/provider.py` now holds `EntropicMemProvider` (the whole provider: hooks, tools, the post-v2 seam — EM-404 later splits `tools.py` out) plus `ProviderHost`, the ABC the plugin package implements; `plugins/entropicmem/__init__.py` is **145 lines** of `register` + a `MemoryProvider` subclass over `_HermesHost`, which injects the plugin's path/config resolvers, the P0a shadow read, the host's context-propagating thread factory, its bounded tool-error formatter and `RecallStatus`. **`em` still imports no host and no plugin package** — an AST guard (`test_the_provider_implementation_imports_no_host`) fails if that regresses, and a second guard fails if the shell grows past 150 lines or the implementation reappears in it. The resolver seam still reads the shell's globals at call time, so the tests that patch `plugins.entropicmem.resolve_scripts_dir` / `load_plugin_config` keep working through the split. Six source-scanning guards that pinned behaviour by reading the old file (`PROVIDER_CALLS`, the raw-`MemoryEngine` drift guard, the deferred-import scan, the injection-marker text, the secure defaults, the `_spawn`/threading rule) were pointed at the implementation deliberately — each had failed the moment the code moved, which is what they are for.
  - Evidence: 40 new tests; **2226 / 5 / 3** bare and **2228 / 3 / 3** with numpy on Python 3.10 and 3.12; `ruff==0.16.2` clean; the privacy guard clean **in place**. **21 mutation checks, all red** — slice 1's thirteen, plus the host-import guard, the 150-line shell, the implementation-reappears-in-the-shell guard, a bypassed config host, an unconsulted thread factory, tool errors skipping the host formatter, silence from `recall_status`, and an unconsulted shadow read.

- **EM-303 observability, promotion evidence, and model-switch hygiene (the owner's precision list).**
  - **Per-query vector observability, so a silent recall drop is visible.** Every vector-mode generator call logs `coverage` and `stale_excluded` on the `em.retrieval.vectors` logger — an INFO line per query, and a WARNING whenever vectors were excluded as stale. A **stale→re-embed round trip is pinned for memories and episodes**: the edit excludes and logs, and the normal `embed` queue returns the row to results without manual repair.
  - **The vector tests cannot be silently skipped.** `tests/test_vector_ci_guard.py` fails if CI's test job loses numpy, stops running the full suite, or either numpy-gated test disappears. Documented: on a bare box the 5 skips are the two numpy tests plus the two internal-ops scripts and the private digest list; with numpy both pass. CI installs numpy (merged in the follow-ups) and runs with the digest list, so the cache/AC tests run on every merge.
  - **Vector-mode evals exist and are labelled.** A new explicit `evals run --embeddings/--no-embeddings` override leaves the frozen suites lexical by default while making a deliberate vector run possible. With `fastembed`/`BAAI/bge-small-en-v1.5`, the hard suite reads **recall@5 0.988, ageing 1.000, paraphrase 1.000, noise 0.172, abstain 1.000** against lexical 0.933 / 0.733 / 1.000 / 0.172; the ci suite is at parity. Committed baselines: `evals/baselines/v3-hard-vector.json`, `v3-ci-vector.json`; both comparisons printed cross-mode and never gated. **Live retrieval remains lexical until EM-401–403.**
  - **Promotion gate recommended and recorded** (owner confirms before the live flip): coverage ≥ 95% with `stale_excluded == 0` on the qualification sample; hard recall@5 ≥ 0.95; ageing ≥ 0.85; abstain ≥ 0.95; noise ≤ 0.172; must_not_ok = 1.0; turn-path p95 ≤ 150 ms at 50k; the shadow's `v3_only == 0` still holding.
  - **Model-switch hygiene.** Measured **≈2.1 KB/row all-in (1.5 KB blob): ≈21 MB per 10k memories per model set** (50k → 106 MB per stale set). Policy decided: prune non-current-model rows after a completed backfill for the current model plus a **7-day grace**, keeping the current and previous models, executed by EM-410's maintenance pass. Tests prove a partial new-model backfill never uses old-model vectors — memories **and** episodes.
  - **Idempotence oracle strengthened:** the replay test now judges by row count plus a sha256 over every column of `embeddings`, not `created_at` alone.
  - **EM-411 stays unbuilt and default-off**; its first implementation commit is specified as the two test groups most likely to waste jobs or double-extract (a shared window key across cadence/idle/compaction seals; the idle `min_window_turns`/`min_window_chars` floor).
  - Evidence: 6 new tests; **2184 / 5 / 3** bare and **2186 / 3 / 3** with numpy on Python 3.10 and 3.12; `ruff==0.16.2` clean. Six mutations red: observability removed, the episode stale count zeroed, CI's numpy removed, a gated test renamed away, and the model filter neutralised for memories and episodes.

- **EM-303 precision pass: version-checked vector rows, the episode read path, and retrieval-mode labels on every eval report.**
  - **Every vector row now records and is checked for model, dimension and text version.** The model id is already the primary key (`embeddings(owner_type, owner_id, model)`), so a model switch can never mix rows — reads filter by the configured model and the backfill adds the new model's rows beside the old. Reads also filter to the query's **width**, so a same-name artifact whose dimension changed is neither padded nor a crash. And the **text version is verified on every read**: a memory vector only serves when its stored `content_hash` matches the memory's current one; an episode vector when its stored stamp matches `updated_at` (an empty stored hash means "unverified", kept for pre-EM-303 rows). A stale vector is excluded — coverage drops and the queued re-embed restores it — never silently mixed. `embed_backfill` now writes real hashes/stamps instead of empty strings.
  - **Episode vectors are no longer write-only.** `candidates.episodic` appends vector-ranked episodes under the same 50% coverage gate as memories, and the pipeline hands their cosines to the gate's support test, so EM-307's renderer prints a vector-only paraphrase under "Recent episodes". Lexical and vector hits of the same episode collapse to one row.
  - **Backfill idempotence and resume are pinned.** A replayed backfill changes nothing (rows, `created_at`, vectors all byte-identical — the no-rewrite tell), and a mid-run backend failure leaves the first 64-row page intact with the second run completing to exactly the expected rows, no duplicates.
  - **Every result file and comparison carries `retrieval_mode`** (`lexical`/`vector`), and `evals run --compare` prints both modes: a cross-mode comparison (including against a pre-label file) is context only and never gates. All six committed baselines are labelled `lexical` — verified by construction, since `ci`/`hard` force embeddings off. `evals tune` records its mode too.
  - The status doc now says plainly that **live retrieval is lexical until EM-401–403**; no semantic-retrieval claims without that wiring. The capture feature's design points (shared window dedupe key across all triggers, post-commit enqueue that cannot fail the checkpoint, idle minimum-content floor, and the 4–9 extraction-jobs-per-100-turn estimate) are written into **EM-411**'s spec, still unbuilt.
  - Evidence: 12 new tests; **2178 / 5 / 3** bare and **2180 / 3 / 3** with numpy on 3.10 and 3.12; `ruff==0.16.2` clean. Seven mutations red, each against the guarantee it targets: the memory hash check, the episode stamp check, the width filter, episode vector recall, episode gate cosines, the v3 mode label, and cross-mode detection.

- **EM-303's follow-ups: episodes become embedding owners, evals can embed, and numpy runs in CI.**
  - **Episodes are embedded too.** `EpisodeStore.add_episode`/`upsert_episode` enqueue an `embed` job shaped `{owner_type: "episode", owner_id, stamp}` (`updated_at` is the version, since episodes have no `version` column); the handler embeds `title + summary` and re-checks the stamp **inside the write transaction**, so the same race guard the memory path has applies. `embed_backfill` now pages memories **and** episodes (`pending_embed_texts`), and `meta.embedding_model` still records only on completion. The memory vector generator deliberately ignores episode rows (they render through EM-307's episode sections).
  - **The v3 eval adapter can produce query embeddings.** With `disable_embeddings=False` (what `evals run` passes for any suite that is not `ci`/`hard`), the adapter embeds the scenario's documents at `load()` and the query in `_run()`, passing both into `pipeline.retrieve` — the vector generator and the gate's cosine condition become measurable end-to-end. The frozen suites pass `True` and stay byte-identical lexically; a `none` backend changes nothing anywhere.
  - **CI installs numpy**, so the vector-cache agreement tests and the card's 50k p95 < 25 ms AC actually run in the ordinary test job instead of skipping (the same fix fastapi got earlier).
  - Evidence: 9 new tests; **2166 / 5 / 3** on 3.10 and 3.12 on a bare box, **2168 / 3 / 3** with numpy installed (`ruff==0.16.2` clean). Five mutations went red: the episode enqueue removed, both episode stamp guards removed, the backfill's episode branch removed, document embedding removed, and query embedding removed. Two first-draft test gaps were found and fixed on the way — the episode stale test needed a real race (a replay is harmless because the handler re-reads), and the first mutation attempts hit the wrong call sites, which is itself the check working.

- **EM-303's closing slice — backends, jobs, model identity, and the numpy vector cache.**
  - **Backends** (`em/embeddings/backends.py`): `fastembed` (ONNX, no torch; default `BAAI/bge-small-en-v1.5`, 384-d, preferred), `sentence_transformers` (default `all-MiniLM-L6-v2`), `openai_compat` (stdlib `urllib`, env `ENTROPICMEM_EMBEDDINGS_BASE_URL`/`_API_KEY`/`_MODEL`), and `none`. `auto` picks the first available in that order; an explicit name that is unavailable **falls back to `none`**, never raises. The optional stacks are imported lazily, so `em` stays stdlib-only.
  - **Service** (`em/embeddings/service.py`): batches of ≤ 64, `summary or content` (≤ 2,000 chars) for memories, `title + summary` for episodes, idempotent `warm()`. No prefetch path may call it.
  - **Jobs** (`em/embeddings/jobs.py`): `embed` re-checks the row's `version` **inside the write transaction** — a lease-expired duplicate that read v1 and embedded while v2 landed cannot clobber the newer vector. `embed_backfill` pages 64 at a time, heartbeats between batches, and writes `meta.embedding_model` only on completion; `ensure_embedding_model` queues one backfill per new model (dedupe key). Without a backend both are a clean no-op — no dead jobs on a box without fastembed, and the backfill catches up once one is installed. The worker CLI registers both types.
  - **Vector cache** (`em/embeddings/cache.py`): one snapshot per `(db, model, scope, mode)`, invalidated by `(row count, max rowid, write generation)` — `put_embedding` bumps the generation. Numpy gives one matrix-vector product per query; without numpy the same ranking comes from the pure-Python cosine. Scope filtering stays in `scope_sql` (the doc records why not "id→scope masks": a second copy of the §3.5 rule would drift). Migration **0005** indexes `embeddings(owner_type, model)`. `candidates.vector` and MMR now read through the cache.
  - **Measured:** 50k × 128 vectors, warm search **p95 3.86 ms** (p50 3.68, max 3.88) against the card's 25 ms AC; cold cache build 297 ms. The AC test needs numpy — now installed by CI (see the follow-up entry above), so it runs there; the pure-Python agreement path is tested everywhere.
  - **Recorded caveat:** a raw-SQL rewrite of a vector that leaves count, max rowid and the generation unchanged is not re-read until the cache resets — the same shape v2's cache had; every write through the store helper is seen. Pinned by a test.
  - **Extras follow the card:** `semantic` is now `fastembed>=0.3`; the v2 re-ranker stack moved to `semantic-st`.
  - **A latent test flaw the change exposed:** the backup tamper test wrote `\x00` over the last byte, which after migration 0005 is already zero — a no-op "tamper". It now flips the byte; the refusal is still asserted.
  - Evidence: 34 new tests; **2157 passed / 5 skipped / 3 xfailed** on Python 3.10 and 3.12 (`ruff==0.16.2` clean). Mutations proven red: the selection order swapped, the batch cap doubled, the model record dropped, the write-time version check removed, the snapshot's tie-break sort removed, and the cache fingerprint ignored. Two first drafts survived and each exposed a real gap — the stale-job test had no race and the tie sort had no observable SQL order — both fixed (the handler gained the write-time recheck) and re-run red.

- **EM-303 — MMR's embedding path.** `diversity.mmr` takes an optional `similarities` callable, called as `similarities(candidate, chosen)` and returning a redundancy in `[0, 1]`; `pipeline.retrieve` supplies one when the caller gave a query vector and a model — **cosine when both candidates have a stored vector (clamped at 0, because an opposed pair must not earn a bonus), token Jaccard per pair otherwise**. With `similarities=None` the behaviour is exactly what every previous caller had. Nothing embeds; no backends, cache or job yet.
  - Evidence: 4 new tests; **2125 passed / 3 skipped / 3 xfailed** on Python 3.10 and 3.12; `ruff==0.16.2` clean. Three mutations went red: the pipeline's similarity suppressed, the negative-cosine clamp removed, and `mmr` ignoring a supplied callable.

- **EM-303, first slice — the vector generator searches embeddings *already stored*.** `em/store/embeddings.py` decodes the blobs v2 already writes (native float32, one query per search) and computes cosine in stdlib; `candidates.vector` joins `embeddings` to active, in-scope memories and runs only when the caller supplies **both** a query vector and a model, **and** §3.6's coverage gate holds (≥ 50% of active memories in scope carry a usable vector for that model). `retrieve(..., query_vector=…, embedding_model=…)` is the seam: it passes both to the context and turns the gate's cosine condition on for that call, but only when no explicit `gate_config` was given. **The default call passes neither**, so the generator returns `[]` and issues no SQL — the lexical path is unchanged and the eval adapter is untouched.
  - **What this slice deliberately does not include, recorded:** the embedding backends (`fastembed` / `sentence_transformers` / `openai_compat` / `none`), the `embed` job, the model-switch backfill, and the numpy matrix cache. Nothing on this path embeds — asserted by a test that the generator issues no SQL without a query vector. An unknown model gets no invented threshold: §3.6 names two, and a hit for any other model cannot pass on cosine.
  - Evidence: 11 new tests; **2121 passed / 3 skipped / 3 xfailed** on Python 3.10 and 3.12; `ruff==0.16.2` clean. Three mutations went red: the coverage floor removed, cosine left unnormalised, and the pipeline failing to enable cosine when a query vector is supplied.

- **EM-307 — the token packer and §3.6 renderer.** `em/retrieval/packer.py` greedily spends `prefetch.token_budget` (default 450) by score: a row is rendered in full when the block still fits, otherwise as its summary, otherwise skipped — never cut mid-sentence. `em/provider/render.py` emits §3.6's block (Constraints, About the user, Relevant memories, Recent episodes, Open follow-ups), cites `em.clock.short_id` as `[m·…]` / `[e·…]`, and places a superseded note as `(kind · updated <date>; was: <old>)`. Untrusted text has `<memory-context` / `</memory-context` broken with a zero-width space and a leading `#` escaped as `\#`. Injection-flagged rows keep v2.7's warning marker; `on_flagged="drop"` is the EM-703 hook and is not the default.
  - **Landed unwired.** The library commit did not call it from `pipeline.retrieve`, the eval adapter, or the provider. The entry below wires a retrieval through to the renderer; the adapter and the provider still do not. `em/config.py` is not read.
  - **The card's ±15% tiktoken check does not hold, and the estimate was not changed to make it.** §3.6 names `ceil(len/4)`. On a 222-character prose sample, cl100k (also o200k, p50k, r50k) counted 42 tokens against this function's 56 — about 33% high. The error is conservative (we pack less, not more). Importing tiktoken when present would pack differently per machine, and `em` is stdlib-only. A repeated-stopword sample can be forced inside 15%; that would be fitting the check.
  - Evidence: 13 new tests; **2103 passed / 3 skipped / 3 xfailed** on Python 3.10 and 3.12; `ruff==0.16.2` clean. Mutation checks that removed the summary fallback, the tag break, the heading escape, the score order, and the section order each went red.

- **EM-307 — a gated retrieval can be rendered.** `pipeline.retrieve(..., with_gate=True)` now returns the predecessors the collapse already computed, and only for keys MMR kept. `load_pack_items` reads content and summary as separate columns, quotes the newest predecessor as `was`, and turns an episode's open loops into follow-up rows at a slightly lower score. `render_retrieval` packs that into §3.6's block. `cite="full"` puts the stored id in the brackets; the default stays the short citation.
  - **The eval adapter still does not call it.** Its prefetch is still `- [stored id] text` with no budget. A short citation would stop `parse_injected_ids` matching, and the 450-token budget can drop an id the old bullet emitted. That switch stays a separate change, and it should be measured on the hard suite before anyone makes it. `em/config.py` is still not read. The flag stays off.
  - Evidence: 7 new tests; **2110 passed / 3 skipped / 3 xfailed** on Python 3.10 and 3.12; `ruff==0.16.2` clean. Three mutations went red: the predecessor map dropped, `was` cleared, and `cite="full"` turned into a no-op.

- **EM-306 — the calibration harness: `python -m evals tune` splits the hard suite by id hash, searches a pre-declared grid, and commits what it chose.** The split is `sha256(scenario_id)[:8] % 100 < 70` → dev, so a rerun on a later commit scores the same holdout — the rule and its concrete ids are recorded in the result file. The objective is the card's, on the **dev** split only: `0.4*recall@5 + 0.3*mrr + 0.3*abstain_correct − 0.2*noise_rate`. The grid is declared in code before any run (`gate.min_score ∈ {0.25, 0.30, 0.35}`, `gate.min_coverage ∈ {0.30, 0.34, 0.40}`, three rerank-weight presets), and selection is "highest dev objective; ties prefer the candidate closest to the spec" — the first pass found a flat tie in the `min_score` band, so a flat objective must not move a threshold by accident.
  - **One seam in the real pipeline, not a second pipeline.** `pipeline.retrieve` takes optional `gate_config`/`rank_weights` (defaults: the spec) and the v3 adapter threads them, so every tuned number is a number for the code the provider will call. Tests show an override reaches the stage it names, and that `None` is the spec.
  - **What the run found, holdout included.** 27 candidates on 44 dev scenarios (126 turns); the holdout (16 scenarios, 28 scored turns) was never optimised. Chosen: the gate thresholds **unchanged at the spec's 0.30/0.34** (the dev objective was flat across the band) and the rerank weights moved to `importance_heavy` (rrf 0.50 / importance 0.25 / recency 0.10 / confidence 0.10 / feedback 0.05) — dev mrr 0.917 → 0.919, holdout mrr **0.865 → 0.881** with the same recall (0.869) and noise (0.115). Committed as `em/config.py` scalars and `evals/results/tune-hard-4eb8097.json`; `em/config.py` links the file back and a test pins the two together.
  - **Deliberately not tuned, recorded in the result file's `deferred` block:** the per-intent `GENERATOR_WEIGHTS` tables (a 4×6 space the card does not name) and the per-model cosine thresholds (grid empty until EM-303).
  - **CI's eval gate stays v2, decided here and written on the job:** the tuned defaults are not wired into call sites until EM-401–403, so a v3 gate today would enforce the spec defaults, not the calibration; `tests/evals/test_adapter_v3.py` remains v3's regression protection in the ordinary suite.
  - Evidence: 18 new tests (14 in `tests/evals/test_tune.py`, 3 in `tests/unit/test_em_retrieval_pipeline.py`, 1 in `tests/evals/test_adapter_v3.py`); **2090 passed / 3 skipped / 3 xfailed** on both Python 3.10 and 3.12; `ruff==0.16.2` clean; **12 mutation checks, all as expected** — each override dropped, the split re-hashed, the objective re-weighted, the tie-break removed, and the armed value, config, result and rule each drifted, all caught.

- **A cited-SHA guard: every SHA written into a doc or a landing commit must resolve.** `tests/test_cited_shas.py` scans every 7–40 character hex token in tracked markdown and in the messages of commits not yet on the base branch (`main`, falling back to `origin/main`), and fails on any token that does not resolve to a commit unless it is in an explicit allowlist with a reason. The allowlist is the closed set of tokens cited on purpose that cannot resolve — the seven dead SHAs from the scrubbed history, the upstream fork commit, and the date in the `rollback/pre-s1-20260924` tag name — and an honesty test fails if an entry stops being cited or starts resolving, so it cannot rot into a place to park a real SHA.
  - **Why it exists:** a docs commit cited a SHA that did not exist (recorded at `7c2fdf9`), and the document-control guard caught it only by luck of position — the token sat in the `Last reconciled` line, the one place that guard checks. The same token in any other doc, or in a commit message, would have landed, and a citation that does not resolve makes the record unfalsifiable. Published history is deliberately out of scope: it is never rewritten (rule 9) and legitimately cites dead and upstream SHAs, so the check is the docs tree plus the commits about to land.
  - **Not a vacuous scan:** sanity floors (≥ 20 markdown files, ≥ 50 tokens) fail if the regex or the glob breaks, and a pin shows a blob hash is not a commit citation. Evidence: 6 new tests; **2072 passed / 3 skipped / 3 xfailed** on both Python 3.10 and 3.12; `ruff==0.16.2` clean. **11 mutation checks, all behaving as expected** — including a blindness proof (disabling the resolution check lets a fabricated token through, which is why that check is the guard) and one defect ruff found: an undefined name in the failure path would have made the commits half crash instead of assert, the exact "cannot fail for the right reason" shape.

- **A guard for the pre-flight count itself: the three pages that state the expected suite size must now agree.** `tests/test_master_todo.py` extracts the `passed / skipped / xfailed` triple from `MASTER_TODO`'s gates table, `NEXT_CHUNK.md`'s pre-flight and `REMAINING_PLAN.md` §11's verify block — one anchored line each — and fails if they disagree, if an anchor stops matching exactly one line, or if a page drops out of the comparison. **Cross-file agreement only, deliberately:** comparing against a live collection would need a nested pytest run, which trades a silent drift for a flaky gate (and `--collect-only` cannot see collection-time skips, so the number would not match regardless). The drift it exists for is real, not hypothetical: Chunk 18's pre-flight said **1995** while `MASTER_TODO` and the actual run said **1998**, and the rule on mismatch is "stop and report" — so one stale page costs a full re-measure and invites exactly the wrong conclusion ("assume the older number is right").
  - **The comparison is a pure function (`_baseline_disagreement`) fed synthetic violating inputs**, because the docs agree today and a guard whose only input is an agreeing tree cannot be mutation-proven: weakening its `len(...) > 1` test slipped through green until disagreement was handed to it directly. Nine mutation checks — **eight caught**; the one survivor is a knowingly redundant assertion (the in-doc drift probe), which the synthetic tests already cover, and it is labelled belt-and-braces in the code rather than left looking load-bearing.
  - Evidence: 5 new tests; **2066 passed / 3 skipped / 3 xfailed** on both Python 3.10 and 3.12; `ruff==0.16.2` clean. No product code touched.

- **P0c — the shadow readout: a divergence log can now be *scored* against the frozen observable, and collected honestly.** The promotion observable (`≥ 200 turns, copy within 900 s, divergence ≤ 10%, v3_only never non-zero, off-turn p95 ≤ 150 ms`) has been fixed in `_shadow.PROMOTION` since Chunk 17, but nothing could read a log against it, and the live store holds **zero facts** — so the only sample a dev box could produce was one that measures nothing. Two pieces landed; **no threshold was moved**:
  - **`_shadow.evaluate / read_log / report / render / main`** score a log in place, next to the thresholds it uses, so the two cannot drift. Thresholds are read from `PROMOTION` *at report time* (tightening the dict changes the verdict — a copied literal cannot). The verdict is `met` / `not met` / `cannot conclude`, and the rules that make a green light mean something are all asserted: **a short, unreadable or no-signal sample is never `met`** (an empty store makes v2 and v3 both inject nothing, and a naive rate reads that as 0% divergence — a fake pass handed straight to the owner); **a violation outranks a short sample** (one fabricated hit in three turns is `not met`, so "we need more data" can never dissolve a finding); and the writer's own fields are **cross-checked**, not trusted — `stale` against the bound in both directions, `divergence`/`v3_only` against the ids on the line, with disagreement refusing to read as fresh.
  - **Margin, distribution and findings, not just a pass/fail**, because that is what the owner asked to see: every number carries how much room was left before it would have failed (rates in points, not bare fractions — the seeded run showed the difference), `p50/p95/max` together (the `perf-smoke` tell: a low p50 with a huge max is one noisy sample), the alternate denominators as context *while the verdict uses the frozen one*, and the first offending line numbers and ids. **Findings cite line numbers and ids and never question text** — the log holds the user's question, and a report is something you paste into a ticket or to an agent.
  - **The runner is `python plugins/entropicmem/_shadow.py report [--log PATH] [--json]`, deliberately not an `entropicmem shadow report` CLI command.** `plugins/entropicmem/__init__.py` imports `agent.memory_provider`, so the v2 CLI cannot import the provider package in a plain shell; going through it would have failed closed with an `ImportError` and no clearer error than this note. The structural reason is pinned: a test parses `_shadow.py` and fails if it ever gains a module-level relative or host import, because that breaks the collector silently and nothing else would. Exit code is the verdict: 0 met, 1 not met, 2 cannot conclude.
  - **`scripts/shadow_collect.py` collects a sample.** It copies the store it reads with `sqlite3.Connection.backup` from a `mode=ro` connection, runs the **real v2 engine** on the copy for `v2_ids`, and drives the real `_shadow.run` per turn — so the migration path, the S3 pipeline and the latency are all genuine. **It never writes the source** (asserted on bytes *and* mtime, not by reading for a `mode=ro`), it restores the two env vars it borrows, and it refuses a `--source` that is not there rather than degrading to an empty store. A test proves the ids arrive from the engine, because a stub returning `[]` would still exit 2 with "no signal" and look like a clean plumbing run.
  - **What the two real runs measured.** (1) Against the actual live store (zero facts, 12 turns): every condition reported, divergence `no signal`, verdict **cannot conclude**, p95 1.48 ms / max 32.46 ms — the correct answer, and the case the old naive reading would have called a pass. The live store's sha256 and mtime were identical afterwards. (2) Against a temp store of 8 invented facts, 240 turns: `turns` **met** (240/200), `copy staleness` **met** (max age 0.5 s), `v3_only` **met** (0 lines), `p95` **met** (1.89 ms), `divergence` **not met — 37.5%** (90 of 240 turns).
  - **The finding, and why nothing was tuned.** That 37.5% decomposes into **150 ids v3 missed and 0 ids v3 added**: on every line, v3 kept the strongest hit where v2 injected two or three. The condition the observable itself calls decisive — *never fabricate* — passed on all 240 turns; the symmetric ceiling failed because it counts a miss and an addition alike, which is what EM-305's gate was built to do (the adapter measured noise 0.172 vs v2's 0.219 for the same reason). **The threshold was not moved** — `NEXT_CHUNK.md`'s guard says a reading that needs a threshold moved is not a reading. What is handed back is the *definition* question, and it must be answered **before** real turns are read: either divergence stays symmetric (a promotion bar of "v3 shows what v2 shows"), or it splits into a fabrication ceiling (already `v3_only == 0`) and a separate miss ceiling. A synthetic 8-query mix repeated 30× over 8 facts is not evidence about real turns — it is evidence about what the metric does with a selective second engine.
  - **CI's deliberately-v2 eval gate is now written where the next person will see it:** a comment on the `evals-ci` job itself says `--adapter v3` exists and scores, that making v3 fail a build is EM-306's decision, and that until then `tests/evals/test_adapter_v3.py` is v3's regression protection.
  - Evidence: **36 new tests** (`tests/unit/test_shadow_report.py`, `tests/unit/test_shadow_collect.py`), **2055 passed / 3 skipped / 3 xfailed on both Python 3.10 and 3.12**, `ruff==0.16.2` clean, v2 eval gate unchanged. **Eighteen mutation checks; seventeen caught first pass and one survived, and the survivor was a gap in my tests rather than in the code**: the privacy mutation added the question to the *findings*, and my assertions only checked the human render, not the `--json` body that prints them. Both privacy tests now assert against the serialised report, and the mutation dies.

- **P0b — a v3 store serves prefetch *from* S3, behind `ENTROPICMEM_V3_RETRIEVAL=1.** `V3Engine.recall_with_relevance` gains a second mode: with the flag on it calls `em.retrieval.pipeline.retrieve` — the **one** shared pipeline the eval adapter and the shadow read call — and maps the pipeline's memory rankings back into the v2 `StoredFact`s the provider renders. This is what completes the cutover re-decision condition (a): until now, even on a v3 store the provider served **v2's** scoring, borrowed through the facade's lazy `memory_engine` import. The flag is strictly `== "1"` (the `ENTROPICMEM_ALLOW_LIVE_MIGRATION` shape), read per call so a store is comparable both ways without a redeploy, defaults off, and exists only on the v3 path — a v2 store never constructs this class. The cutover decision stays the owner's and the live store is unmoved.
  - **The off path is the pre-P0b path, proven twice rather than assumed.** A golden test pins the full provider response (system prompt, context query, first prefetch, *cached* second prefetch, abstaining query) and the engine-level result byte-for-byte against output captured from the commit before the flag existed — and the fixture was re-run in a detached worktree of that base commit, differing only in the per-run ULID diagnostics, so the transcript and engine results are identical from the first content line down. A second, independent test blocks `pipeline.retrieve` with a raiser and shows the off transcript still answers exactly as the golden says. Mutation-checked: forcing the flag on, forcing the branch on, and loosening the flag's accepted value each turn it red.
  - **The served block is still the provider's own renderer, so no renderer was added.** The on path returns `StoredFact`s and `_format_block` is untouched; a test pins the exact bullet lines (`- [id] text (domain · date) [score:x]`) and asserts nothing EM-307 owns leaks in (the superseded note is not rendered, no token packer). `id` is `legacy_id` when the row carries one, so the provider's dedup and `touch` keep resolving what was injected.
  - **Two deliberate differences on the on path, recorded rather than implied.** (1) **Episodes are skipped:** the pipeline also ranks `owner_type='episode'`, a v2 fact has no episode shape, and §3.6's render is EM-307's — so served prefetch is memories-only, pinned by a test that shows the episode *is* ranked by the pipeline and *is not* served. (2) v2's `min_relevance`/decay/evergreen parameters are **not** applied on top of S3's gate: they belong to the v2 formula this path does not run, and the gate's support test + `min_score` is the threshold there. `domain` is a post-filter, so it can return fewer than `top_k` — the honest answer for a filter the generators do not understand.
  - **Latency measured on the turn path, against the pre-declared p95 ≤ 150 ms.** At 1000 memories the provider's `prefetch` (cache off, including engine construction) goes from p95 **4.18 ms** off to **9.32 ms** on, and the engine call itself from **1.01 ms** to **6.14 ms** (p50s 3.75 → 8.89 and 1.00 → 6.05). The budget was declared before measuring and is untouched; the on-path number is also inside CI's 10 ms warm budget at this scale.
  - Evidence: 21 new tests (`tests/unit/test_em_facade_v3_retrieval.py`, `tests/unit/test_provider_v3_retrieval.py`, and two in `tests/unit/test_em_retrieval_gate.py`), **2019 passed / 3 skipped / 3 xfailed on both Python 3.10 and 3.12**, `ruff==0.16.2` clean, v2 eval gate with no gated metric regressed. Thirteen mutation checks, all caught first pass. **P0c (reading the shadow data against its pre-declared observable) is next**, then EM-306.

- **P0 — the v3 **shadow read**: exercise S3 on real turns without committing the store.** `plugins/entropicmem/_shadow.py`, enabled only by `ENTROPICMEM_SHADOW_V3=<path>` (**unset is the default and does nothing**). The turn is served by v2 exactly as today; afterwards, off the turn path, S3 runs over a **v3 copy** and one divergence line is appended. This is the answer to the circle the cutover deferral left: a v3 read path only exists once the store *is* v3, so the only way to get real-turn signal without committing the store is to read a copy of it.
  - **The two properties the owner asked to be asserted rather than inspected are asserted.** (1) The **full provider response** is byte-identical with the shadow on and off — `system_prompt_block`, the context query, the first prefetch, the *cached* second prefetch and an abstaining query, not just the block. (2) The shadow is **not on the turn path**, proven by blocking the shadow thread on an event and showing the response still returns; the server thread would deadlock rather than fail if it were, so the assertion cannot pass by luck. A third test proves the flag actually enables the work, because byte-identity is meaningless if the flag does nothing.
  - **Both biases travel with every number, because neither can be designed away.** The copy **lags**, so divergence is a **lower bound** and would read as "v3 is fine" when the truth is "the copy is old" — every line carries `copy_age_s`, `copy_refreshed` and `stale`. And v3 scores today are lexical/entity/episodic with **no cosine condition** until EM-303, so a line also carries a `caveat` saying so: these are **not** an apples-to-apples quality comparison against v2.
  - **The promotion observable was fixed before any data was collected**, so the threshold cannot be fitted to the sample: ≥ 200 turns with a copy inside the 900 s bound, divergence ≤ 10%, **`v3_only` never once non-zero** (the failure that matters is v3 fabricating a hit, not missing one), and off-turn p95 ≤ 150 ms — §4.2's warm prefetch budget. It is an agent proposal, not an owner decision.
  - **The live store is never written** (rule 3): the refresh opens it read-only and writes only the copy. A test asserts the live store's bytes and mtime are unchanged. The refresh also **migrates** the copy, so the shadow exercises the real cutover path on throwaway data on every refresh.
  - **The pipeline was extracted, not copied.** `em/retrieval/pipeline.py` now holds the one `analyze → GENERATORS → fuse → rank → [gate → collapse → mmr]` sequence, and **both** the eval adapter and the shadow call it — two "identical" pipelines is how they stop being identical. Proven behaviour-preserving: re-running the v3 ci suite leaves **40 of 45 metrics byte-identical**, and the only five that moved are `latency_ms`, the metric the runner documents as wall-clock noise.
  - **Two real findings from the suite, both kept.** `tests/regressions/test_findings_v27.py::test_f005_uses_spawn_context_thread` — a passing regression test — rejected the first draft, which used a bare `threading.Thread`: F-005b/H3 requires every background thread to come from `_spawn`, and the shadow now does. And `test_plugin_imports.py`'s loader never registered its own module, so `from . import _shadow` failed with a `ModuleNotFoundError` for a module sitting right there — a harness artefact that would have hidden a genuine relative-import break; the loader now registers itself as a real import does.
  - **A defect the end-to-end run found and the unit tests could not.** v2 and v3 name one memory differently — v2 uses the 16-hex content id, v3 a `mem_…` ULID, keeping the v2 id in `legacy_id` — so comparing the raw sets marks **every** memory as a divergence, reports "v3 fabricated a hit" on every line, and would have tripped the `v3_only == 0` promotion condition on the first day of collection. The shadow now compares `COALESCE(legacy_id, id)`: a migrated row compares by the id v2 already uses, and a row written straight to v3 keeps its own so it still reads as v3-only. **Every unit test passed while every real line would have been meaningless** — which is the argument for running the thing once against a real store, and why the regression test drives a real `MemoryEngine`-written store through a real migration rather than a fixture.
  - **What that end-to-end run showed, beyond the fix:** on a migrated copy of a real v2 store, v3's pipeline injected **exactly the memory v2 injected** for the same query — divergence empty, `v3_only` empty, at 38.6 ms off-turn. That is the first real-turn-shaped evidence the shadow existed to produce.
  - Evidence: 18 new tests (`tests/unit/test_provider_shadow.py`), **1998 passed / 3 skipped / 3 xfailed on both Python 3.10 and 3.12**, `ruff==0.16.2` clean, the v2 eval gate still green and v3's ci numbers unchanged (recall@5 1.000 · abstain 1.000 · noise 0.143 · must_not_ok 1.000). **P0's second half — a v3 store serving prefetch *from* S3 rather than from the v2 scoring the facade borrows — is not in this chunk**, so the cutover re-decision condition (a) is still open.

- **The v3 eval adapter — the first end-to-end number for the v3 retrieval pipeline.** `evals/adapters/engine_v3.py` drives `analyze → GENERATORS → fuse → rank → gate → collapse → mmr` over a freshly migrated v3 store, so a score here is a score for the pipeline the provider will call, not a re-implementation of it. This is one of the two conditions the owner set for reconsidering the cutover, and it is now met.
  - **The result, against the v2 adapter on the same run:** the **ci** suite is at parity on every gated metric (recall@5 1.000, abstain_correct 1.000, noise_rate 0.143, must_not_ok 1.000). On the **hard** suite v3 gives **recall@5 0.933, abstain 1.000, noise_rate 0.172, must_not_ok 1.000** against v2's **0.939 / 1.000 / 0.219 / 1.000** — parity on recall, **lower noise**, because the abstention gate is doing its job. `hard/ageing` is **0.733 for both**, which is the number §6.2 predicts for a lexical-only baseline: the four paraphrase misses share no words with the stored fact and vectors are EM-303's. That the v3 pipeline reproduces the known limitation exactly is a cross-check on the adapter, not a coincidence.
  - **The one asymmetry with the v2 adapter, mirrored from it:** `search` runs the pipeline **without** the gate, because ranking metrics measure ORDER and letting the filter truncate there would score abstention twice — the same reason v2 recalls with `min_relevance=0.0`. `prefetch` runs **with** it and returns `''` when nothing survives, which is what `abstain_correct` reads. A test asserts the asymmetry in both directions.
  - **What it deliberately does not do, recorded rather than implied:** no vector generator and no cosine support condition (EM-303 owns the backend, so a v3 score today is lexical, entity and episodic only); no token packer or §3.6 render (EM-307), so `prefetch` emits the minimal id-bearing bullet form the runner parses with **no token budget** — `prefetch_tokens` is an "info" metric and not a gate, so that cannot fail a compare, but it is not the shipped render; and it never touches a live store, which a test pins by asserting the database is under a temp directory.
  - Baselines committed as `evals/baselines/v3-ci.json` and `evals/baselines/v3-hard.json`. **CI's gate is deliberately unchanged** — `evals-ci` still runs v2 against `v2.8.0-ci.json`. Making v3 scores fail a build is a decision about what CI enforces, and it belongs with EM-306's calibration, not with the adapter that makes measuring possible.
  - 10 new tests (`tests/evals/test_adapter_v3.py`), which is also what exercises the adapter in CI.

- **EM-305 — the abstention gate, supersession collapse and MMR diversity (§3.6).** `em/retrieval/gate.py` and `em/retrieval/diversity.py` complete the v3 retrieval pipeline's decision stages: the gate decides whether a memory block is shown at all, the collapse removes duplicates and superseded duplicates, and MMR keeps the surviving block diverse. The card's two AC rows are both met — abstention scenarios at 100% (budget ≥ 0.95) and the `update` split asserted at this layer.
  - **The "post-stem" coverage trap is resolved exactly, not approximated.** §3.6 defines coverage as "matched non-stopword query terms (**post-stem**) / total query terms, computed in Python on the candidate text", and §3.2 forbids non-stdlib dependencies (no Python porter stemmer). A naive comparison under-counts exactly where the tokenizer disagrees with the surface form — `preferences` against a stored `preferred`, `runs` against `running` — and because the gate is a **hard filter**, an under-count silently abstains on an answer the generators had already found. `coverage` therefore measures with **the same tokenizer**: the candidate texts go into a transient in-memory FTS5 table declared exactly as `memories_fts` is (`porter unicode61 remove_diacritics 2`), and one `MATCH` per query term reads back which candidates contain it. Post-stem is then true by construction. It is also cheap: one table build and at most 12 probes, regardless of how many candidates are gated. A mutation that swaps the tokenizer for plain `unicode61` reddens the test that names the shapes.
  - **§3.6's "exact-hash duplicates across scopes" cannot use the hash, and the reason is recorded.** `MemoryStore._content_hash` mixes the scope *into* the digest — deliberately, so two users' identical sentences stay two facts — so two copies in different scopes hash differently. The collapse groups on the loaded **text** instead, which is what the hash was derived from, and prefers the **narrower** scope: two users storing one sentence is one answer, and the row belonging to the asking user is the one to show.
  - **"Drop `superseded`" is a property of the pipeline, not a filter here.** `status='active'` is enforced from the generators all the way through both loaders, so a superseded row is never a candidate. The collapse's remaining supersession work is the §3.6 note: a successor whose predecessor changed inside 30 days gains the `superseded_note` flag, and its predecessors come back on `CollapseResult` for the renderer. §3.6's other disjunct ("or the old one was a candidate") cannot arise in an active-only pipeline and is recorded as such rather than faked. The **chain guard** covers what *can* arise — a `restore` leaving two live members of one chain — and keeps the successor.
  - The gate is §3.6's four support conditions (coverage ≥ 0.34, cosine ≥ `gate.min_cosine[model]`, an entity hit, pinned) and then `score ≥ 0.30`; **pinned bypasses outright** (§3.6's generator table: "bypasses gate (still budgeted)"), which the tests pin in both directions. The cosine condition is present but **off**, because it needs the embedding backend and a model name (EM-303) — a caller with a cosine can enable it and pass one, and no cosine is invented.
  - MMR is λ = 0.7 over the top 20 with the **token-Jaccard fallback** §3.6 names (the embedding-cosine path is EM-303's), greedy and deterministic with ties going to the better rank, and the tail past the top 20 keeps its rank order.
  - **Gaps recorded, not invented:** `gate.*` config is a `GateConfig` defaulting to the spec's numbers (EM-407 owns config, exactly as `ranking.*` does), and the `include_history` *flag* on the `update` AC belongs to the memory tool — this layer supplies the predecessors it needs, and `MemoryStore.history()` already walks the full chain.
  - **Two couplings are pinned rather than trusted, both found by review.** (1) Coverage measuring with the index's tokenizer is what makes the gate exact, and it also *couples gate semantics to index configuration* — so `gate.index_tokenizer` reads `memories_fts`'s declaration from the schema and `test_the_gate_tokenizer_matches_the_index_tokenizer` fails if the two ever diverge, rather than a future index change silently starting to abstain on answers already found. (2) The diversity-key bug was a **class**, not an incident: a `texts` mapping keyed by bare id makes every lookup miss and degrades MMR to pure relevance and the collapse to "no duplicates" while looking green. `diversity._require_texts` now refuses that mapping outright, and the test that used to pass for the wrong reason is joined by one asserting the rejection. Both guards were proven red by mutation (the tokenizer constant diverged; the presence check removed).
  - Evidence: 54 new tests, **1970 passed / 3 skipped / 3 xfailed on both Python 3.10 and 3.12**, `ruff==0.16.2` clean, eval gate with no gated metric regressed. Twenty-three mutation checks, **all caught on the first pass** — including the tokenizer swap, both threshold boundaries, pinned-at-both-ends, the scope and active filters in both loaders, the chain guard inverted, the note window ignored, and the gate being made to sort. Three of them were caught only after fixing tests I had got wrong: the diversity mappings were keyed by bare id instead of `Ranking.key` (so every text lookup returned `""` and the tests passed for the wrong reason), the chain guard dropped the successor rather than the ancestor, and one abstention label asserted a match porter itself does not make (`deployment` → `deploy`), where abstaining is the correct behaviour.

- **EM-304 — fusion, rerank and explainability (§3.6).** `em/retrieval/fusion.py` is the stage between the candidate generators and EM-305's gate: weighted RRF over the generators that actually ran, then §3.6's feature rerank, ordered by a total tie-break and carrying the per-hit explanation. EM-302's generators and EM-301's analyzer now have a consumer.
  - **The `G_active` normalisation is a trap the plan names, and it is implemented and pinned.** `rrf_n(d) = rrf(d) / Σ_{g ∈ G_active}(w_g / 61)` where `G_active` is the generators that **ran and returned at least one candidate** — a generator that ran and found nothing does not count. Normalising over all six configured generators would give a bm25-only rank-1 hit `(1/61)/(3.7/61) ≈ 0.270`, under a `gate.min_score` of 0.30 (§3.6), so a perfectly good lexical hit would be silently discarded. `test_the_g_active_trap_a_single_generator_hit_is_not_capped_at_a_quarter` asserts both halves: the real value is 1.0, and the wrong value is below the gate.
  - Every other rule is implemented and hand-checked to 1e-9: the rank-1 reference denominator, `Σ_g w_g/(60+rank_g)`, a duplicate within one generator counting once at its best rank, the four-intent weight table **verbatim**, and the rerank `0.60*rrf_n + 0.15*importance + 0.10*recency + 0.10*confidence + 0.05*feedback` with the `evergreen`/`standard`/`volatile` recency rules, `age_days = now - max(updated_at, last_accessed_at, valid_from)` and `feedback = clamp(0.5 + 0.1*(helpful - 2*unhelpful), 0, 1)`. `age_days` is floored at zero so a future stamp cannot push `recency` above 1 and break the score's `[0, 1]` range.
  - **The tie-break test needed care and is worth reading.** `updated_at` also *feeds* `recency`, so varying it changes the score and the `(score desc, updated_at desc, id asc)` tie-break never runs — the first version of that test proved nothing. `evergreen` is what makes an exact tie reachable (its recency is 1.0 whatever the timestamp), and the test now asserts the two scores are equal before asserting the order, so it cannot silently stop testing the tie-break. Insertion order is also pinned independently, since a stable sort would otherwise decide it.
  - `load_features` reads the rerank columns (which `Candidate` deliberately does not carry — the plan fixes it at three fields) and **re-applies §3.5 through the same `scope_sql`** as the generators, plus `status='active'`. The redundancy is deliberate: this is the last point before a row's content would be shown, so a scope mistake in a generator cannot surface here. A fused key with no features is dropped, never ranked.
  - **Episodes get named defaults, not silent zeros.** §3.6's rerank is written for memories and `episodes` has no `confidence`, `decay_class`, `pinned`, `last_accessed_at` or feedback counters, so each default is declared in `EPISODE_DEFAULTS` — a record is fully trusted and undecayed — and the episode's `start_at` (world time) fills the `valid_from` slot the age formula asks for.
  - `ranking.*` config does not exist yet (EM-407 owns it), so weights and half-lives are a `RankWeights` parameter defaulting to the spec's numbers, exactly as EM-301's `extra_stopwords` was. `legacy_tokens()` produces §3.6's flat `why_retrieved_tokens` form; wiring it onto the recall path belongs to the provider card. `superseded_note`, the third §3.6 flag, is EM-305's collapse and is deliberately not emitted.
  - Evidence: 53 new tests, **1916 passed / 3 skipped / 3 xfailed on both Python 3.10 and 3.12**, `ruff==0.16.2` clean, eval gate with no gated metric regressed. Twenty-two mutation checks, each red for the right reason with the tree restored byte-identical. **Two escaped on the first pass, and both were real test gaps rather than fixed tests:** the zero-denominator mutation was a no-op (so the guard is now removed for real *and* a new test covers an all-zero-weight query, which is EM-308's state today), and the tie-break test was not testing a tie, as above.

- **EM-301 — the `QueryAnalyzer` (§3.6).** `em/retrieval/query.py` now turns one user message into an `AnalyzedQuery`: `<memory-context>` blocks stripped, `\w+` tokens, stopwords and 1-character tokens dropped, up to 12 terms chosen by IDF, intent classified, entities resolved, and a temporal window detected. EM-302's generators now have a real producer for the shape they consume.
  - **The term-frequency source has a subtlety §3.6 does not account for, and it would have silently disabled IDF.** `memories_fts` is tokenized with `porter`, so the `memories_vocab` view §3.6 names holds *stems* — `stage`, not `staging`. A raw-token lookup would therefore miss for every word whose stem differs (`running`→`run`, `preferences`→`prefer`), report df 0, and collapse IDF into a length ordering. The view is still the base map, exactly as §3.6 says; each requested term the view does not carry is counted with an FTS5 `MATCH`, which applies the *same* tokenizer and so cannot disagree with the query the generators will run. `test_vocabulary_bridges_the_porter_stem_gap` pins the premise and the bridge.
  - **An all-stopword query keeps its own tokens, and §3.6 does not say so.** `"who am I"` is the `profile` intent's own example and every word in it is a stopword, so the literal reading would make an identity question retrieve nothing. v2's EM-104 rule (R2) is carried forward instead: the raw tokens come back with the length rule applied as far as possible without emptying the query. Recorded as a deliberate addition, not a silent one.
  - **The intent heuristic is measured, not asserted.** 42 real phrasings are labelled in `tests/unit/test_em_retrieval_query.py` — nine per intent, plus **three that the heuristic genuinely misses** and two that match more than one pattern so the declared precedence is pinned. The AC is "≥ 30 labelled queries, ≥ 90% accuracy"; the table delivers 39/42 = 92.9%, and the test asserts both the budget and that no unexpected query starts failing. The three misses are declared by name rather than dropped from the table, so the number means something.
  - Entity detection resolves normalised n-grams up to 4 tokens, longest first, in **one indexed `IN` probe** rather than one query per n-gram. The lookup joins through `entities` to filter on the profile: `entity_aliases` alone is keyed by alias, so two profiles that both know an "Acme" would otherwise resolve to whichever row the primary key found first.
  - Temporal detection covers §3.6's common shapes — an ISO date (the whole day), "since X", "before X", "between X and Y", "last N days/weeks/months/years" as a *range*, with the day clamped so 31 March minus a month is 28 February. **The full grammar is EM-310's** and is deliberately absent rather than half-implemented; a reversed `between` returns no window instead of falling through to the bare-date rule and answering a different question.
  - **Migration `0004`** creates `memories_vocab` (`fts5vocab(memories_fts, 'row')`). The plan calls it "migration 0003"; that number is taken by `0003_audit_append_only`, so it is 0004. Its name is a space-free slug, which the fresh-subprocess migration test requires by construction.
  - `em/retrieval/stopwords.py` is **the same 180-word list the v2 engine already uses** (`scripts/stopwords.py`, written for EM-104), copied rather than re-derived, and pinned identical by a test: the two engines cannot silently disagree about which words carry no discriminative power.
  - Two gaps §3.6 names that have no source in this repo, recorded rather than invented: **`write_generation`** (nothing defines the counter the IDF cache is meant to key on, so the view is read per call) and **`query_rewrite`** (no config module to enable it and no rewrite hook to call). `extra_stopwords` works as a parameter and EM-407 owns its config source.
  - Evidence: 37 new tests, **1832 passed / 3 skipped / 3 xfailed on both Python 3.10 and 3.12**, `ruff==0.16.2` clean, eval gate with no gated metric regressed, `tests/unit` green standalone. Nineteen mutation checks, each red for the right reason with the tree restored byte-identical. **One escaped and was worth keeping:** the first "rare term outranks a common one" test tied on length, so it passed with the IDF lookup entirely unwired — the replacement makes the common term *longer* than the rare one, so only a working document frequency puts the rare term first.

- **EM-302 — the candidate generators (§3.6).** `em/retrieval/candidates.py` now implements the five generators that do not need an embedding backend, plus the shapes and the scope helper they share. **The card text was recovered** (the master plan is on the owner's machine at `Documents/EntropicMem Dev docs/EntropicMem_v3_Master_Plan.md`), so this is built against the real card and §3.6, not against the summary — and the card text is now transcribed into `REMAINING_PLAN.md` §6.2 so the next agent does not have to find it again.
  - Generators: `bm25` (`memories_fts`, with §3.6's `bm25(memories_fts, 1.0, 0.5, 0.3, 0.1)` column weights), `entity` (`memory_entities` + one hop through `relations`), `episodic` (`episodes_fts` + the analyzed time window, `owner_type='episode'`), `recent` (the 48 h window, gated to the `temporal`/`lookup` intents), `pinned` (`pinned=1` or `kind='constraint'`). Each is `(ctx: RetrievalContext) -> list[Candidate]`, and `Candidate` is exactly §3.6's `(owner_type, owner_id, raw_score)`.
  - **`vector` is deliberately not here.** §3.6 gates it on an embedding backend and forbids re-reading vector blobs per query, so it belongs with the backend and the numpy cache in EM-303. `GENERATOR_LIMITS` still carries its `k` because that is §3.6's table verbatim.
  - **Deadline discipline is structural, not hoped for.** Every generator asks the context whether the wall has passed *before* touching the database, and again between result pages, so an expired generator issues no SQL at all and one that expires mid-scan returns a partial list. Both halves are tested — the "no SQL" half deterministically, the "partial" half by driving the module's clock, so neither races the machine.
  - **`scope_sql` gained the chat dimension of §3.5.** `MemoryStore.list` has always emitted `scope_chat=? OR scope_chat=''` while the row predicate `_in_scope` did not — a live drift between the rule and one of its SQL callers. Both now call `em.store.types.chat_in_scope`, so the two forms agree. It is a no-op until something sets `scope.chat` (nothing does today), and the scope cross-check test now proves the agreement over a row matrix that includes chat rows.
  - Evidence: 38 new tests, **1795 passed / 3 skipped / 3 xfailed on both Python 3.10 and 3.12**, `ruff==0.16.2` clean, eval gate with no gated metric regressed. Fifteen mutation checks, each red for the right reason with the tree restored byte-identical: the guest tier exclusion dropped and always emitted, the chat clause dropped, the predicate's chat check dropped (a row in another chat), `is_owner` ignored, the bm25 column weights swapped, the `status='active'` filter dropped, the deadline never consulted, the episode owner type and the time window, the recent intent gate, the entity hop factor and its relation-profile check, the FTS prefix rule moved to v2's threshold of 3, and `_fetch` ignoring its limit.
  - **Two gaps recorded, not papered over:** `vector` (EM-303), and §3.5's `visibility='user'` owner-only clause for profile-wide rows. The latter is a *privacy* gap and is now on the status page: `_in_scope` implements the tier half but not the visibility half, and implementing it changes which existing rows a non-owner may read, so it needs its own card rather than a quiet edit.

- **S3 begins: the `em.retrieval` package, with `scope_sql` — EM-302's first piece.** EM-302 is "candidate generators", and the one part of it that is fully specified in the repo is `scope_sql(scope) -> (clause, params)`: the §3.5 scope rule as SQL, the single helper every generator must filter through, with a unit-tested truth table. **The generators themselves are deliberately not written yet** — see the gap below.
  - **`scope_sql` and `MemoryStore._in_scope` now share one definition of the owner context.** `may_read_owner_only(scope)` moved into `em/store/types.py`, next to `Scope` and `OWNER_ONLY_TIERS`; `_in_scope` calls it as a predicate and `scope_sql` calls it to decide whether to emit the SQL exclusion. The plan said "if the two ever disagree, `_in_scope` wins" — sharing the function makes disagreement impossible by construction, rather than a thing a test has to catch. The tier names come from `OWNER_ONLY_TIERS` on both sides for the same reason.
  - The tests **do not restate the rule**. They run both forms over the same real rows (a matrix of profile × scope-user × tier) and assert they select the same set, plus a guard that the matrix actually discriminates (a guest sees strictly fewer rows than the owner) so the cross-check cannot pass vacuously.
  - Evidence: 6 new tests, **1757 passed / 3 skipped / 3 xfailed on both Python 3.10 and 3.12**, `ruff==0.16.2` clean, eval gate with no gated metric regressed. Six mutation checks, each red for the right reason with the tree restored byte-identical: the owner clause never emitted, always emitted, the profile check inverted, the profile-wide branch dropped, `is_owner` ignored, and only `sensitive` excluded. The `is_owner` mutant reddens four tests across two suites, which is the shared predicate doing its job.
  - `em.retrieval` is added to `[tool.setuptools] packages` (invariant 9); the packaging guard now derives it from disk, so the omission could not have shipped.

- **EM-211 (Chunk 10.2): the facade grows the CLI's maintenance calls.** The second slice of the CLI-parity route — six methods that change state rather than list it: `promote_pending`, `discard_pending`, `reinforce`, `rebuild_fts`, `timeline` and `recall_episodes`. As in 10.1 the return shape is the CLI's, and the tests assert the **transition** (through `get_fact`/`stats`) rather than a return value.
  - **`promote_pending` is where v3's model shows.** v2 looked the quarantine row up, re-`remember`ed it into durable memory and deleted the quarantine row, so promotion produced a *new* id; on v3 the pending row **is** the memory, so promotion is the `pending -> active` edge and the id is unchanged — one row, one history. Same for `discard_pending`: v2 deleted the row, v3 takes `pending -> deleted` so it stays for audit, and the boolean means the same either way.
  - **Recorded deviation:** v2 re-stamped `source="promoted"` and added a `"promoted"` tag on promotion. v3 keeps the original `source` (the store does not allow editing it) and adds no tag, so a promoted memory stays `auto_extracted` and is eligible for consolidation after the age/access window like any other.
  - **`rebuild_fts` is deliberately a report, not a repair.** v2 needed it because a delete path could skip its FTS cleanup; v3's `memories_fts` is maintained by `memories_ai`/`ad`/`au` triggers in the same transaction as the row, so there is nothing to repair. It returns v2's `{fts_before, fts_after, facts}` and touches nothing.
  - `recall_episodes` applies its date window in the facade rather than handing it to `EpisodeStore.search_episodes`, because that filters `created_at` while v2 (and `list_episodes`) use `start_at` falling back to `created_at` — filtering in one place keeps the two episode reads consistent. A shared `_episode_to_v2` mapper now backs both, so they cannot drift.
  - Evidence: 15 new tests (`tests/unit/test_em_cli_maintenance.py`).

- **EM-211 (Chunk 10.1): the facade grows the CLI's read/listing calls.** The first slice of the CLI-parity route. Eight methods, each returning the shape **the CLI command prints** rather than a v3 shape, because that is the job: `list_facts` (v2's importance-then-recency order, `StoredFact` objects), `list_pending` (v3's `pending_reason` surfaced as v2's `reason`), `list_audit` (`seq`→`id`, `target_id`→`fact_id`, newest first), `get_versions`, `episode_stats`, `embedding_stats`, `list_episodes` and `recall`. Nothing calls them yet — the CLI still refuses a v3 store (10.0) and is routed in 10.4.
  - **Where v3 cannot do what v2 did, the method refuses by name** rather than returning a quietly different answer: `list_episodes(domain=...)` (v3 episodes carry `kind`, not `domain`) and `recall(scope="shared"|"all")` (the shared publish store is S5). A named absence is recoverable; a silent partial answer is not.
  - **Two normalisations in `get_versions`, both verified against v2 rather than assumed.** v2's version rows were pre-edit snapshots (its `snapshot_version` ran only before an edit) and v3 also writes one at creation, so the first edit would list its content twice; consecutive identical contents are collapsed, and the list is reversed because v2's was newest-first. Probed v2 directly: create A, update B, update C gives `['B', 'A']` on both. The live content is deliberately not a version row, matching v2.
  - `episode_stats` keeps `by_domain` present-but-empty so the CLI's loop still runs, and offers the real breakdown as `by_kind`; `embedding_stats` reports v2's `available: false` shape, because v3 has no embedder wired yet (EM-303).
  - **Plan correction found by measuring:** `profile_id()` was in 10.1's list, but its only CLI caller is `cmd_migrate --status`, which is in the *refuse* group — so it was dropped rather than implemented unused.
  - **Known papercut, recorded for 10.4:** `remember` returns the v3 id while every read presents the content-derived `legacy_id` (the card's rule for `StoredFact.id`), so `entropicmem remember` prints one id and `entropicmem memory list` shows another. Both resolve, and v2 printed the same id in both places. Not changed here — it is Chunk 5's documented behaviour and this slice is reads; 10.4 is where a user-visible id mismatch would matter.
  - Evidence: 15 new tests (`tests/unit/test_em_cli_reads.py`), **1707 passed / 3 skipped / 3 xfailed on both Python 3.10 and 3.12**; eval gate with no gated metric regressed; `perf-smoke` warm p95 4.811 ms against the 20 ms budget; `ruff==0.16.2` clean. Nine mutation checks, each red for the right reason with the tree restored byte-identical: the domain filter, the scope rule, the ordering, the `reason` and `fact_id` mappings, both `get_versions` normalisations, and both refusals.

- **EM-211 (Chunk 7.1): entity linking runs as a job — `link:<memory_id>:<version>`.** `EntityLinker` and `EntityStore.link` already existed (EM-208); what was missing is that nothing ever *called* them on v3. No write queued a link job and no worker registered a `link` handler, so a v3 memory was never bound to an entity and the graph stayed empty. `MemoryStore` now enqueues `link:<memory_id>:<version>` from the write path, next to the existing `embed` enqueue, and never runs the linker inline — entity work inside the write transaction is exactly what invariant 2 forbids.
  - The handler (`make_link_handler` in `em/formation/entity_linker.py`) opens its own short transaction through `ctx.store.transaction()`. It is idempotent, because delivery is at-least-once: `EntityStore.link` upserts (`INSERT OR IGNORE`) and a proposed phrase's sighting counter counts *distinct memories*, so a lost-lease retry converges instead of inflating the graph. That is asserted by re-running the same job.
  - Two inputs dead-letter rather than retry: a payload with no `memory_id`, and a memory that no longer exists. A memory that is merely not live — pending, archived, or forgotten — is **not** a failure: there is nothing to link, so the job is done. That distinction matters because deletion is a status change, not a row delete, so a link job queued before a `forget` can still be claimed.
  - The row's own scope is used for linking, never a default — linking under the wrong profile would put one profile's names into another's graph. Guarded by a test.
  - **Promotion queues a link job too.** A memory quarantined at write time has no link job, so the `pending -> active` edge in `set_status` now enqueues one; a content `update` re-queues at the new version. The two paths deliberately agree with the `embed` enqueue rather than differing by accident.
  - `entropicmem worker run` registers the handler and knows the type (`--types link`), so a cron can drive it.
  - **Known limitation, asserted so it cannot drift:** the two-sighting rule is EM-208's, so promotion links whichever memory's job trips the counter and does *not* retro-link the earlier one — its own job has already run by then. Backfilling that is the plan's `reconcile` job (S5), not this chunk.
  - Evidence: 17 new tests (`tests/unit/test_em_link_job.py`), **1643 passed / 3 skipped / 4 xfailed on both Python 3.10 and 3.12**; eval gate vs `v2.8.0-ci.json` with no gated metric regressed; `perf-smoke` warm p95 3.820 ms against the unchanged 20 ms budget; `ruff==0.16.2` clean. Seven mutation checks, each red for the right reason with the tree restored byte-identical: the enqueue, the dedupe key's version, the promotion enqueue, the update enqueue, the not-live guard, the missing-memory dead-letter, and the CLI registration. One existing test was updated rather than weakened: `test_repeated_add_does_not_pile_up_embed_jobs` counted *all* jobs and now counts them per type (`{"embed": 1, "link": 1}`), which is what it always meant.
  - The §3.5 owner-only rule for sensitive reads is **not** here — it needs the gateway identity threaded from the provider, and is the next chunk (7.2).

- **Docs: the Hermes Marketplace rules are now in the repo, not just in a hand-off.** The Marketplace working guidelines lived outside the repo, which is the one place the project's own rule says guidance must not live — an agent on another machine could not read them. `docs/MARKETPLACE.md` now carries the durable version: the two repos and the private archive, the branch model and the dead SHAs, that **the pin is the release** (the entry names an exact 40-hex commit, and that commit is what every install clones), the four rules that follow from the pin, the owner-gated release procedure in order (tag before the pin moves, so a force-push can never drop the pinned commit), catalog-PR mechanics including the fresh-branch rule, fresh-`HERMES_HOME` install verification, and the pitfalls that have already cost time. `AGENTS.md` points at it from the read-order paragraph, `MASTER_TODO.md` gains a Marketplace section, and the README documentation table lists it.
  - The entry was **verified live** while writing this rather than copied on trust: `gh api repos/NousResearch/hermes-agent/contents/plugin-catalog/entropicmem.yaml` returns version `2.8.1`, pinned at `7e02412366408234629e759a575edc31eb138721` (the `v2.8.1` tag commit on `release/2.8.x`), subdir `plugins/entropicmem`, maintainer `Ufonik88`, tier `community`, and capabilities matching `plugin.yaml` exactly (7 `provides_tools`, 5 `provides_hooks`). The private archive is still private and `35a02f4` is still an ancestor of `main`. Documentation only.

- **Docs: EM-211 is code-complete, and the wiring is documented as owner-gated with the reason.** `MASTER_TODO.md` moves 7.2 to done, records the `is_owner` decision and the two-sighting linking limitation in its "deviations a reader must not fix" section, and moves its gates table to 1669. More importantly it records the finding that closes the "why is the facade still unwired?" question: `V3Engine.__init__` calls `migrate()`, so pointing the provider at a v2 store migrates it in place — **wiring and the v3 cutover are the same act**, which is why wiring is owner-gated rather than a quiet refactor, and why the recommended shape is engine selection by `PRAGMA user_version` so the wiring can land and be tested without touching anyone's data. `REMAINING_PLAN.md` §2/§5/§6.1/§9/§11 follow, and `NEXT_CHUNK.md` files 7.2 under Part A and scopes **Chunk 8, EM-212's package move** in Part B — chosen over the wiring precisely because it is safe and unblocked, with the 20 top-level modules and the six the AC names measured from the code and a proposed 8.1/8.2 split. `V3_FOUNDATIONS.md` gains the read-rule note and the code-complete status. Documentation only.

- **Docs: Chunk 7.1 closed out, Chunk 7.2 scoped, and the status-page guard taught sub-chunk ids.** `MASTER_TODO.md` moves EM-211 to "nearly done", lists the branch and commit in In flight, moves its gates table to 1643 and its "what is next" to the owner-only rule. `REMAINING_PLAN.md` §2/§5/§6.1/§9/§11 follow, with the 7.1 ledger row. `NEXT_CHUNK.md` files 7.1 under Part A and scopes **7.2** in Part B, measured from the code: `_in_scope` already exists and is where the rule belongs, `MemoryStore.get` already takes a scope that `get_fact` simply does not pass, and the identity would come from the provider's `_gateway_user_id`. Part B names the one genuinely owner-facing judgement rather than burying it — `Scope.is_owner` defaults to `True`, which is fail-open; silently inheriting that would leak sensitive rows to guests, so the default must be chosen deliberately and the reason written down. `tests/test_master_todo.py` now understands decimal chunk ids (7.1, 7.2) in both the table and the In flight section, so sub-chunks cannot slip past the "committed is not merged" check. Documentation only.

- **EM-211 (Chunk 6): the mirror call — the provider's last raw database read is gone.** `_locate_mirror` used to run `SELECT id, content, tags FROM facts` against `engine.db`, the v2 engine's raw `sqlite3.Connection`. That was the one thing in the provider a v3 facade could not serve, because there is no `facts` table on v3.
