Both engines now answer `find_mirrored(needle) -> Optional[str]` — the id of an `active` memory tagged `mirrored` whose content contains `needle` — and the provider calls that instead. `PROVIDER_ATTRIBUTES` is now **empty**, so nothing in the provider touches a raw connection, and `BEHAVIOURS["mirror-scan"]` moves from the last OPEN item to a cited behaviour with five parity scenarios. `open_items` in the coverage test is now an empty set, which is the chunk's exit signal.
  - v2's implementation is the scan that was in the provider, moved behind a method: CSV tags split and whole-tag matched, empty needle returning `None` rather than matching every row.
  - v3's adds the three filters v2 got for free. `status='active'`, so a forgotten mirror is no longer locatable and a replace cannot resurrect it. The §3.5 scope rule (invariant 5), so one user cannot locate another user's mirror while a profile-wide row stays visible to everyone in the profile. And `mirrored` as a whole tag: v3 stores tags as a JSON list, so the quoted `LIKE '%"mirrored"%'` is only a cheap pre-filter and the parsed list decides — which also has to insist on a *list*, because `tags` holding the JSON scalar `"mirrored"` would pass both the pre-filter and a plain `in` test on the decoded string.
  - The `make_id(previous_content)` fast path is unchanged; only the substring fallback moved.
  - Evidence: 15 new tests (5 parity scenarios × 2 engines, 5 v3 unit tests), **1624 passed / 3 skipped / 4 xfailed**; eval gate vs `v2.8.0-ci.json` with no gated metric regressed; `perf-smoke` warm p95 3.666 ms against the unchanged 20 ms budget; `ruff==0.16.2` clean; `tests/unit` green standalone for `windows-import`. Nine mutation checks, each red for the right reason with the tree restored byte-identical: the `active` filter, the scope filter, the whole-tag check on both engines, the `isinstance(list)` guard, the empty-needle guard, `db` re-admitted to `PROVIDER_ATTRIBUTES`, `find_mirrored` missing from `PROVIDER_CALLS`, and the provider reading `engine.db` again.
  - One escaped mutation was worth keeping: dropping v3's whole-tag check stayed green, because the quoted JSON pre-filter already excludes `mirrored-archive`. The parity test was passing for a reason other than the code it claimed to cover, so the layer the pre-filter cannot reach — an unparsable or scalar `tags` value — is now pinned separately, and that mutation is caught.
  - The facade is still **not wired into the provider**; this changes one call site, not the engine the provider constructs.

- **Docs: the "blocked on GitHub auth" note is retired.** `MASTER_TODO.md` and plan §9/§11 said Chunks 5 and 6 could not be merged because `gh` on the build machine was unauthenticated. That is no longer true: the git credential helper holds a valid OAuth token for `Ufonik88` with `repo`, `workflow`, `user:email` and `read:user` scopes, `git push` uses it, and `~/.local/bin/ghx` runs the GitHub CLI with the same token read from the macOS keychain at call time (never written to disk). The `ENTROPICMEM_PRIVACY_DIGESTS` repository secret exists, so CI's privacy guard can run. Branch protection on `main` forbids force-pushes and deletion. The merge is therefore mechanical rather than blocked; the docs now say so instead of telling the next reader to wait for a session with push access. Documentation only — no code, no behaviour, no test count change.

- **Docs: the status page now says outright that the 2.8.x line is finished.** A request to "finish the 2.8.1 sprint" cannot be acted on, because 2.8.1 is already implemented, tagged, published as the repo's Latest release, catalog-pinned and install-verified — and `main` is not a superset of it. `MASTER_TODO.md` now states that under "Released line", names the two conditions that would create new 2.8.x work, and spells out that the lines are not patchable into each other (`main`'s provider, CLI and engine import the `em` package). Without this a reader can spend a session building a sprint that ended in September. Documentation only.

- **Docs: Chunks 5 and 6 are on `main`, with the CI findings recorded.** `MASTER_TODO.md`, plan §2/§5/§9/§11 and `NEXT_CHUNK.md` Part A all move from "committed, not merged" to merged at `e25db32`, and the In flight sections now say nothing is in flight. The status page also records *how* the merge went rather than only that it happened: the first CI run was red on a Python-3.10-only timestamp bug and a shallow-checkout failure in the new doc guard, both fixed in the merge — a clean-looking history would be less useful to the next agent than the knowledge that local green on 3.12 was not enough. Documentation only.

- **Docs: Chunk 6 closed out, Chunk 7 scoped.** `MASTER_TODO.md` moves to three-quarters of EM-211 with five unmerged commits listed by SHA, its gates table to 1624, its "what is next" to the linker, and its blocker section to name the four CI-only jobs plus the privacy guard as genuinely unverified locally. `REMAINING_PLAN.md` §2 stops claiming Chunk 5 is on `main` (it never was — it is on the unmerged branch), §5 gains ledger rows for Chunks 5, 6 and the document-control work with their real SHAs marked **unmerged**, §6.1 and §9 and §11 all move to Chunk 7, and the state table's date and In flight row match the branch. `NEXT_CHUNK.md` files Chunk 6 under Part A and scopes Chunk 7 in Part B, measured from the code: `EntityLinker` and `EntityStore.link` already exist and `purge` already forgets sightings, so what is missing is the enqueue, the handler, its registration in `em/jobs/cli.py`, and the §3.5 owner-only rule — with a size guard that splits the chunk into 7.1 (the job) and 7.2 (the scope rule) if the rule turns out to need provider changes, since a wrong answer there is a privacy leak rather than a bug. `V3_FOUNDATIONS.md` and `ARCHITECTURE.md` gain the facade engine and the `find_mirrored` scope rule.

- **`MASTER_TODO.md`: the canonical status page, and a document-control rule that agents cannot skip.** The plan had been pointing at a `MASTER_TODO` that lived only in one agent's private memory — unreadable by the next agent on a different harness, which is exactly how a chunk gets redone or a merge gets assumed. The file is now in the repo: where the project is, what is in flight (including what is *not* merged), what is next, the gates and their expected numbers, the owner-only blockers, and the recorded deviations a reader must not "fix".
  - `AGENTS.md` gains a **"Document control — first and last"** section: reconcile the planning docs *before* starting a task as well as after, never end a development session without it, and write down what is actually true — a commit on an unmerged branch is "committed, not merged", an unpushed branch has no CI, and a gate that could not be run is named rather than implied to have passed.
  - `tests/test_master_todo.py` enforces it, in the same family as the docs-link and CLI-drift guards: the file must exist and carry its sections, be **linked** from `AGENTS.md` and the README (a bare mention is not a link), record a SHA that is a real ancestor of the branch, and not report a chunk as `Done` while its In flight section admits the same work is unmerged. Six mutation checks, each red for the right reason. Documentation only — no product behaviour changes.
  - The README's Documentation table now leads with it, so a human reading the repo lands on the same page an agent does.

- **EM-211 (Chunk 5): the legacy facade's writes — `em/facade/engine.py`.** The seven `NotImplementedError` stubs are now real write paths over `em.store`, so `V3Engine` implements the whole provider-facing `LegacyEngine` contract. `remember` is the load-bearing one: it stamps `legacy_id = sha256(content)[:16]`, which is what makes the provider's mirror lookup `get_fact(StoredFact.make_id(content))` resolve — the read half has always resolved that id, and this is the write that produces it. Because `memories.legacy_id` is UNIQUE and content-derived, the stamp is applied only to a profile-wide write (v2 had one owner per database); a user-scoped write leaves it empty, so two users storing the same sentence stay two facts instead of colliding on one hash. `MemoryStore.add` carries the whole v2 pipeline (policy, PII redaction, injection screening, exact-duplicate collapse, version row, sync outbox, audit row, queued embed job), so `remember` is translation rather than a second implementation.
  - `forget` and `consolidate` move rows through the §3.4 state machine (`deleted` / `archived`) instead of deleting them or copying them into a side `facts_archive` table. `get_fact` reads a terminal row as absent and `stats` leaves it out of the live count, so callers see v2's behaviour while the row and its version history stay for the audit trail. `forget(id, confirm=True)` returns `False` the second time, matching v2's DELETE reporting.
  - Both destructive paths take a throttled snapshot (`snapshot_if_due`, reason `pre-forget` / `pre-consolidate`) *outside* the write transaction, per §3.3. **EM-210's acceptance criterion is now met through the facade too: 5 forgets in an hour write exactly 1 snapshot**, verified against the backup directory.
  - `extract_and_store` quarantines each candidate into `pending` — the write policy routes an `auto_extracted` source there — and `promote=True` commits it over the `pending -> active` edge, which is what v2's separate promotion write did. One versioned row instead of v2's two, and the extraction record is the same row. No LLM call is added or changed.
  - `add_episode` puts the episode id in `episodes.legacy_id`, which is what `next_episode_wave` reads, so the cadence-digest numbering survives the cutover. A caller-supplied `episode_id` is replaced in place (the provider derives it from the session id and refires it); without a session key the episode is `manual`, keeping it out of a session's window numbering.
  - The parity harness is now single-path: `v3-facade` joins `WRITE_ENGINES`, the `_remember` seed dispatch and its `NotImplementedError` fallback are gone, and **6 write-path scenarios run against both engines on their full API. No assertion changed.**
  - Recorded v3 semantic changes, each pinned by a test: `remember` does not fuzzy-overwrite (v2's EM-109 near-duplicate rule could rewrite a stored fact in place; v3 collapses only *exact* duplicates and otherwise inserts with a version row — silent overwrite is what the card forbids). `add_episode` accepts and ignores `linked_fact_ids`, `domain` and `source`: v3's `episodes` table has no column for them and adding one would mean a migration, which this chunk does not take. No deprecation warnings are emitted — the card asks for once-per-process warnings on methods "suled for removal in 3.1", v2 emits none, no list of which methods is recorded anywhere, and the provider calls all of them every session, so the facade matches v2 and that requirement stays open.
  - Evidence: 56 new tests (`tests/unit/test_em_facade_writes.py`) plus 6 new parity params; **1604 passed / 3 skipped / 4 xfailed** (1542 baseline + 56 + 6); eval gate vs `v2.8.0-ci.json` with no gated metric regressed (recall@5 1.000, ndcg@5 1.000, abstain 1.000, noise 0.143, must_not_ok 1.000); `perf-smoke` warm p95 3.693 ms against the unchanged 20 ms budget; `ruff==0.16.2` clean; `tests/unit` green standalone for the `windows-import` job. Twelve mutation checks, each red for the right reason with the tree restored byte-identical: the `legacy_id` stamp dropped, the same stamp applied to a scoped write (IntegrityError on the unique index), `forget` reporting True for an already-deleted row, `forget` proceeding without `confirm`, `forget` skipping its snapshot, `touch` returning the requested count instead of the matched one, extraction never promoting, `consolidate` dropping the durable-importance rule, `prune_pending` dropping the age cutoff, `add_episode` never upserting, the store dropping `legacy_id`, and the parity fixture bypassing `remember` again.

- **EM-211 (Chunk 4): the legacy facade's reads — `em/facade/engine.py`.** `V3Engine` implements the read half of the provider-facing `LegacyEngine` contract over the v3 store: `get_fact` (resolving the v3 id, a unique id prefix, and — the load-bearing one — `legacy_id`, so `get_fact(StoredFact.make_id(content))` finds what the v2 migration and the future `remember` stamp; the returned `StoredFact.id` is `legacy_id` when the row carries one, else the v3 id), `stats`, `recall_with_relevance`, `recall_hybrid` and `next_episode_wave` (counting over legacy `ep_*_wN` episode ids). Recall reuses v2's scoring helpers and its FTS builder through a lazy `memory_engine` import — deliberately not module-level, because at 3.0 `memory_engine.py` becomes a shim over this facade and a top-level import would be a cycle — so the parity suite pins identical behaviour until S3 ships the v3 retriever. Only `status='active'` memories recall and a `deleted` row reads as absent, matching what v2's row-removing `forget` meant to callers. The facade is registered in both parity harnesses: `_implementations()` in `tests/unit/test_em_facade_contract.py` (signature-binding, so the Chunk-5 write stubs pass it), and `ENGINES` in `tests/parity/test_engine_parity.py` for the read-path scenarios. The writes chunk replaces the stubs; nothing here is wired into the provider. Evidence: 17 new tests (`tests/unit/test_em_facade_engine.py`); 5 read-path parity scenarios now run against both engines; mutation checks in both directions on the `legacy_id` id rule, the deleted-row hide, the recall status filter, the `stats` active-only count, the tags JSON parse and the wave `+1` (each went red for the right reason, code restored); 1542 passed / 2 skipped / 4 xfailed; eval gate vs `v2.8.0-ci.json`: no metric regressed (recall@5 1.000, ndcg@5 1.000, abstain 1.000, noise 0.143 unchanged).

- **EM-210 (Chunk 3.2): the once-per-hour-per-reason snapshot throttle.** `BackupManager.snapshot_if_due(reason, *, window=3600)` writes a snapshot only when that reason has had none inside the window and returns `None` while one is still young, so a destructive caller that fires in a loop cannot fill the backup directory: 100 `forget` calls inside an hour write exactly one snapshot, which is the plan's acceptance criterion. The window is derived from the `created_at` every manifest already carries, so there is no new table, no bookkeeping file and no migration. `snapshot()` and `create()` stay unthrottled on purpose: the migration hook, the daily job and the tests keep taking a snapshot on every call, and a caller that must never be suppressed keeps using those. Two exemptions, both deliberate and both tested: safety reasons (`pre-migrate*`, `pre-restore*`) are never throttled, because a stale copy there is a data risk rather than noise, and a `window` of zero or less never throttles. A clock moved backwards leaves the age negative and still throttles, and an unreadable `created_at` does not block a real backup. Evidence: 10 new tests (47 in the file), 1519 passed / 2 skipped / 4 xfailed, and three guards mutation-checked in both directions (the window comparison, the zero/negative-window exemption, the safety-reason exemption), each red for the right reason with the tree restored byte-identical.

### Security

- **Chunk 13 — §3.5's `visibility` half, both sides: the write stamp and the read guard.** *Provenance: proposed by the implementing agent and implemented under the owner's authorisation; **ratified by the owner on 2026-10-07 as a ruling** on the behaviour change — `row_is_owner_only()` as the single owner-only predicate, the restored `_outbox` sensitivity gate, and `_resolved_visibility()` stamped at insert. **Internal only:** it is in no release and must not reach the marketplace until the owner explicitly approves a release. No migration, so it is reversible with a code revert alone.*
  - **The bug, measured.** `MemoryDraft.visibility` defaulted to `'user'` and **no call site anywhere set it**, so every `MemoryStore.add` — the facade's `remember` included — stamped `'user'` whether the row was profile-wide or user-scoped. §3.5 pairs `'user'` with a *user-scoped* write and `'profile'` with a profile-wide one, and makes a profile-wide row carrying `'user'` **owner-only**. The store was therefore producing, for every write, exactly the row shape its own plan calls private.
  - **The direction of the fix is the whole point, and it is why the read clause could not ship alone.** Implementing the read clause on its own would have hidden *every* profile-wide memory from non-owners — the opposite of §3.5's intent that profile-wide `public`/`internal` rows are shared knowledge. The write stamp is the real fix; the read guard is the defence for rows that already exist or that a caller deliberately stamps. `test_a_non_owner_still_sees_an_ordinary_profile_wide_row` is the regression guard, and the cross-check matrix in `tests/unit/test_em_retrieval_scope_sql.py` gained the row shape so the SQL and the predicate are compared on it.
  - **What changed.** `MemoryDraft.visibility`'s default is now `''`, meaning *derive from the scope* — a plain `str` default cannot tell "the caller asked for `user`" from "nobody said anything", and the difference is load-bearing. `MemoryStore._insert` resolves it (`'profile'` for a profile-wide write, `'user'` otherwise, a caller's explicit value preserved and never overridden). On the read side `em.store.types.row_is_owner_only` now owns *both* owner-only conditions — the tier, and a profile-wide row stamped `'user'` — and `_in_scope` and `em.retrieval.candidates.scope_sql` both call it, so the predicate and the SQL cannot drift.
  - **A publication consequence, fixed rather than inherited.** `_outbox` queues a row for sync when `visibility in ('profile','shared')`, and its docstring said "anything sensitive stays local" — but the code only ever checked `visibility`. That was sufficient *by accident*, because the old `'user'` default meant a profile-wide write was never queued at all. v2's gate was `_publish_allowed(sensitivity)`, so **v2 published every non-sensitive fact and this restores that**, with the tier check now explicit. Without it a profile-wide `sensitive` row would have started reaching the sync outbox — a leak this chunk would otherwise have introduced.
  - **`MemoryStore.list` was investigated and left alone, with the reason now recorded.** Its `scope_user=?` is *exact*, so a scoped caller can only receive rows already scoped to them — every one of which they may read, whatever its tier — and the profile-wide rules govern rows it cannot return. The Chunk 7.2 note that it "is not tier-filtered" is therefore not a leak; `test_list_never_returns_a_profile_wide_row_to_a_scoped_caller` pins it.
  - **The fix is forward-only, and no existing data needs repairing — stated because silence would be read as "history is clean".** Nothing in the released world was ever affected: the v2→v3 migration stamps **`visibility='profile'`** for every migrated fact (so migrated rows are profile-readable, *not* the owner-only shape), the only producer of the owner-only shape was `MemoryStore.add`'s old default, and the live store is `user_version=0` with v2 tables only. **There is therefore no repair path, no backfill, and nothing hidden from a non-owner today.** A v3 store written *before* Chunk 13 and read *after* it would keep its historical stamps, by design — those rows are owner-only, which fails closed — and since no such store exists outside test fixtures, that is a statement about the contract rather than about data.
  - **Reversible, and it adds no migration.** `LATEST` is unchanged, asserted by `test_the_change_adds_no_migration`. Existing rows are never rewritten: only new writes derive a stamp, and the read guard changes only what a non-owner sees. Rows written while this is live keep their stamp, so a revert restores future behaviour rather than rewriting history — and with v3 stores unreleased and the live store v2 with zero facts, there is nothing to unwind.
  - **The contract for rows that already exist is derive-on-write-only, deliberately.** Only *new* writes derive a stamp; historical rows are never rewritten, and the read guard changes only what a non-owner sees. The consequence is stated rather than hidden: a pre-existing profile-wide row carrying the old `'user'` default is **owner-only** under §3.5's literal rule — it fails closed. Re-deriving those rows would be a bulk change to who may read what, so it is a one-off owner-approved migration if it is ever wanted, never a silent rewrite. In practice there are none: v3 stores were never released and the live store is v2 with zero facts, so this exists only in test fixtures. `test_reads_do_not_rewrite_the_stamp` and `test_the_change_adds_no_migration` pin it.
  - **Nothing consumed the rows this gate stops, and nothing had been queued by the old path.** The only consumer of `sync_outbox` is v2's `MemoryEngine.publish()`, which drains `emitted=0` into the shared `sync_events` log; on a v3 store that path is *unreachable*, because `entropicmem publish`/`pull` are refused by name until S5, and **no `em/` code reads the outbox at all** (it writes, and `forget` deletes). Nothing queued a profile-wide sensitive row before Chunk 13 either: v2's `_publish_allowed` refuses `secret`/`sensitive` outright, and v3's old default stamped `'user'` so `_outbox` returned early — no call site ever combined an explicit `'profile'` visibility with a sensitive tier. So there is nothing to replay, drop or backfill; already-queued rows are all non-sensitive and stay queued, and the v2→v3 migration leaves the table untouched (legacy `fact_id`s preserved until EM-309).
  - Evidence: 30 new tests, **1862 passed / 3 skipped / 3 xfailed on both Python 3.10 and 3.12**, `ruff==0.16.2` clean, eval gate with no gated metric regressed. Thirteen mutation checks, each red for the right reason with the tree restored byte-identical: the draft default reverted, the derivation removed, both wrong derivations, an explicit value overridden, the predicate losing each half of its condition, `_in_scope` dropping the check, `scope_sql` losing the visibility clause and losing the profile-wide guard, the outbox gate removed and inverted, and the outbox payload carrying the raw stamp.

- **EM-211 (Chunk 7.2): sensitive memories are owner-only on read — the §3.5 rule, enforced.** The facade's reads were profile-wide. In a profile with a gateway user, **any user of that profile could read a `sensitive` or `secret` memory**: `get_fact` called the store with no scope at all, and `recall_with_relevance`, the literal-LIKE fallback and `find_mirrored` built SQL with no scope or sensitivity clause. Chunk 5 fixed the write side; this is the read side. A row whose tier is `sensitive` or `secret` is now visible only to its owner, enforced in `_in_scope` — the one function invariant 5 names — so every read that already goes through it inherits the rule, and the facade's own SQL paths were routed through the same function rather than given a second copy of the rule that could drift.
  - **The decision, and why.** `Scope.is_owner` now defaults to **`False`** — fail-closed. Only a scoped caller can leak (a guest is exactly a caller with a non-empty `user`), so the default makes such a caller *assert* ownership rather than inherit it. A wiring mistake then hides the owner's own sensitive rows, which is a **visible** bug, instead of showing them to a guest, which is a **silent** one. A **profile-wide** caller (`scope.user == ""`) is the owner context regardless — the v2 single-owner, no-gateway and CLI case — which is why the default `V3Engine(db)` and the CLI keep working unchanged. This mirrors the provider's own `_is_guest()`: gateway user set, owner list non-empty, and the user not in it. The default config has an empty owner list, so nobody is a guest there.
  - `V3Engine` takes the identity as `is_owner`, a constructor argument, never an environment read. Supplying it from the gateway is the wiring chunk's job; the facade is not wired, so this closes the rule without changing what any live user sees.
  - Evidence: 26 new tests (`tests/unit/test_em_owner_only_reads.py`: a 15-case `_in_scope` truth table, the fail-closed default, and the four read paths plus a migrated `secret` row), **1669 passed / 3 skipped / 4 xfailed on both Python 3.10 and 3.12**; eval gate with no gated metric regressed; `perf-smoke` warm p95 3.927 ms against the 20 ms budget; `ruff==0.16.2` clean. Six mutation checks, each red for the right reason with the tree restored byte-identical: the owner-only check in `_in_scope`, the `is_owner` default, and the scope filter on each of `get_fact`, FTS recall, the LIKE fallback and `find_mirrored`. No existing test needed changing.
  - **Known gap, deliberately left and now recorded:** `MemoryStore.list` still applies only profile+user, not the tier rule, so the facade's maintenance paths (`prune_pending`, `consolidate`) are not tier-filtered. They are the owner's operations, and the facade is unwired; this is noted for the wiring chunk rather than changed here.

- **Vector embeddings are opt-in: no model is downloaded unless you enable them.** With `sentence-transformers` importable in the host venv, `remember` built `SentenceTransformer("all-MiniLM-L6-v2")` with no config gate, so the first write downloaded a model from Hugging Face (network egress from a plugin disclosed as local-only). `embeddings.get_embedder()`, the one place a model is built, now requires an explicit opt-in: `embeddings_enabled: true` in the provider config (default `false`), or `ENTROPICMEM_EMBEDDINGS=1` for the CLI. `remember`, `recall_hybrid`, `rebuild_embeddings` and `embedding_stats` all honour it. When embeddings are off, stored vectors are kept and recall uses FTS5 only. **If you relied on semantic recall, set `embeddings_enabled: true`.**
  - Encoding now also runs **before** the engine's write lock is taken, not while it is held. A first-use model download used to stall every other writer for its whole duration.
  - Docs said the model was "BGE-small"; it is, and always was, `all-MiniLM-L6-v2`. README, ARCHITECTURE and MEMORY_MODEL are corrected.
  - Tests use a fake model class that counts constructions. Mutation-checked for: gate ignored, engine gate ignored, encoding inside the lock, provider ignoring the config. Reported in the Hermes catalog review of 2.8.0.
- **CLI `ingest` re-validates every redirect hop (SSRF via redirect).** `validate_url` checked only the first URL, and `urllib` then followed redirects on its own, so a public page answering `302` to `http://169.254.169.254/` (cloud metadata) or a loopback port reached it anyway. All fetches now go through `safe_fetch`, whose redirect handler runs the same validator on each `Location`, refuses non-http(s) targets and stops after 5 hops. A drift-guard test fails if `urlopen` is used directly again. Reported in the Hermes catalog review of 2.8.0 (NousResearch/hermes-agent#122476). Mutation-checked: without the per-hop check, the metadata and loopback redirects both go through.
- **Fresh v3 installs now refuse edits to the audit log (migration `0003_audit_append_only`).** `0002_v3_core` creates the `audit_no_update`/`audit_no_delete` triggers only on the v2-to-v3 path; its fresh-install branch returns before that step. So every new store, and every store created in tests, had a hash-chained `audit_log` that still accepted `UPDATE` and `DELETE`. The chain would *detect* tampering, but nothing *refused* it, and §3.3 requires both. `0002` is applied and checksummed, so the fix is a new migration that adds the triggers where they are missing (`IF NOT EXISTS`, a no-op on migrated v2 stores) and keeps the purge-token escape. Five tests: fresh install refuses both, a store already at v2 gains them, a migrated v2 store keeps exactly one of each, the purge token still works. The migration tests that pinned "0002 is the latest" now assert what they mean (0002 keeps its slot, stores reach `LATEST`).
- **Private identifier denylist (privacy guard v2).** The privacy guard no longer ships any identifier list, not even as SHA-256 digests: unsalted hashes of short words (names, a company, a bank) are reversible with a word list, so a public hash list republished what it was meant to protect. Digests now come from the `ENTROPICMEM_PRIVACY_DIGESTS` Actions secret (CI) or a local file (`~/.config/entropicmem/privacy-digests.txt`); where secrets exist CI sets `ENTROPICMEM_REQUIRE_PRIVACY_DIGESTS=1`, so a missing list fails instead of skipping. Failure output names file, line and list position only, never the word (CI logs are public). The scan now also reads fixture databases (`.db`/`.sqlite`) as raw bytes, and a test fails if a digest list is committed again. The phone-number rule stays public.
- **Identity guard fails closed.** `scripts/check_commit_identity.sh` no longer swallows `git rev-list` errors (an unresolvable range used to report "nothing to check" and pass); on push it now checks the whole history reachable from the pushed commit, which also covers force-pushes.

- **Docs: Chunk 3.1 closed out on the plan.** `REMAINING_PLAN.md` §2 moves to `2d60cccfb` and names Chunk 3.1, §5 gains its ledger row, §6.1 records the snapshot half as done with the throttle left as Chunk 3.2, §9 now asks when to start 3.2, §11's state table and ordered next steps lead with 3.2, and the pre-flight count moves to 1509. `NEXT_CHUNK.md` files Chunk 3.1 under Part A and scopes Chunk 3.2 in Part B: one throttled entry point for destructive callers, explicitly **not** `snapshot()` or `create()` themselves, with the window derived from the `created_at` already in every manifest so there is no schema and no migration, and a mutation check in both directions.
- **EM-210 (Chunk 3.1): `snapshot(reason) -> Path`, and a snapshot now covers both databases.** `BackupManager.snapshot()` writes `backups/<reason>-<stamp>/` holding `memory.db`, `index.db` when the index exists, and a `manifest.json` that lists every file with its role, source name, sha256, size, row counts and `user_version`. `verify()` re-hashes and re-inspects each one, so a damaged, truncated or missing index is caught rather than assumed. `create()` stays as an alias for its three callers (the migration hook, the daily job through `make_backup_handler`, and the tests) and returns the same details. Restore stages every file first and swaps only when all of them verify, so a half-restored store cannot happen, and a snapshot without an index never touches the live one. Pre-3.1 flat backups stay listed, verified and restorable, and rotation applies one policy across both layouts. A snapshot that cannot copy one of its databases fails whole, with the partial directory removed, instead of quietly omitting a file. Evidence: 14 new tests, the per-file hash check and the index-inclusion branch mutation-checked, 1509 passed / 2 skipped / 4 xfailed.
- **Docs: Chunk 3 is split, and 3.1 is the next piece of development.** The EM-210 gaps are two independent changes and no longer travel together. `docs/plan/NEXT_CHUNK.md` Part B now scopes **only 3.1**: the plan's `snapshot(reason) -> Path`, covering `index.db`, written into `backups/<ts>-<reason>/`, with today's flat backups still readable and `create()` kept as a thin alias for its three callers. It records the sizing (90 to 120 minutes of active work, because the cost is the single-file-to-multi-file model change and not the index file: `_inspect()` already probes any database shape), the tests to write, and a size guard that says stop and report if restore's dual-layout handling outgrows them. **3.2** (the once-per-hour-per-reason throttle behind the "100 `forget` → ≤ 1 snapshot/hour" acceptance criterion) appears in the out-of-scope list only, deliberately unplanned until 3.1 closes. Plan §5's ledger, §6.1's EM-210 line, §9's two open decisions (when to start 3.1; keep 7 routine + 5 safety or adopt EM-113's rule), §11's next-piece row and ordered steps, and the EM-210 row of the deviation table in `docs/V3_FOUNDATIONS.md` all say the same thing. Nothing was started: no code, no test, no migration.
- **Docs: the cold-start state survives documentation commits.** `REMAINING_PLAN.md` §11 now says `main` at `8202081af` **or later** (as §2 already does) and states outright that a docs-only commit moves the tip without changing code, tests or counts, so the next reader does not treat a newer SHA as a plan/state mismatch.
- **Docs: the repo guards and the v3 layer map are written down where the next reader looks.** `docs/V3_FOUNDATIONS.md` gains a "Repo guards that are not invariants" table (identity, privacy, docs links, CLI reference drift, plugin imports, backend namespace, and the two budgeted tests that have flapped) and its layer map now shows `em.jobs.cli`, the `entropicmem worker run` entry point. `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` points at the plan for state and next steps, and README's Documentation table lists `V3_FOUNDATIONS.md`.
- **Docs: `NEXT_CHUNK.md` matches `main` again.** Part A records the 2026-09-29 hygiene batch alongside Chunk 2, and Chunk 3's pre-flight now expects `main` to contain `8202081af` and **1495 passed / 2 skipped / 4 xfailed** instead of the pre-batch 1467, with a pointer to the plan's cold-start section before anyone acts on a mismatch.
- **Docs: the plan now says where the project is and how to pick it up.** `docs/plan/REMAINING_PLAN.md` gains **§11 (cold start)**: the released line against the development line, the last landed chunk, what is in flight (nothing), the next chunk, the exact verification commands with the expected counts (1495 passed / 2 skipped / 4 xfailed, `ruff==0.16.2` from the CI pin), the live-store proof, what stays frozen until the owner says otherwise, and the rule for a red gate (confirm a flake on the same SHA; never widen a threshold). §2's status date moves to 2026-09-29, and README's Documentation table now leads with project status.
- **Docs: the plan records the 2026-09-29 hygiene batch.** `REMAINING_PLAN.md` §2 now carries `a38a47f54`, §5 lists the batch's seven commits, and `NEXT_CHUNK.md` no longer offers the four scouted candidates as available work, since all four landed the same day. Plan bookkeeping only.
- **The docs' command count is now pinned to argparse.** `tests/test_cli_reference_drift.py` reads the top-level command list from the CLI's own `--help` and asserts three things: the count stated in `docs/CLI_REFERENCE.md`, the count stated in `README.md`, and a whole-token reference entry for every command. The count drifted once — EM-209's `worker run` needed `34` → `35` fixed by hand in both files — so the next command fails the suite until its doc line lands. Mutation-checked: a wrong count in either file, and a command whose reference row is renamed, each go red.
- **Docs link guard, and the one link it found.** `tests/test_docs_links.py` resolves every relative link in `docs/`, `skills/` and the four root Markdown files (25 docs) and fails when the target is missing *or* sits on disk without being committed — CI checks out tracked files only, so a local-only target is a broken link there, the same trap as an untracked fixture. It found one: `docs/BACKFILL_PROCEDURE.md` linked `MULTI_PROFILE_PROVENANCE_SPEC.md`, an internal design doc deliberately kept out of this repo. That reference is prose now. Verified red-then-green.
- **Docs: `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` now covers the v3 storage core.** The architecture doc described only the v2 modules the provider runs today, while `main` also carries the `em.*` package that S2 built. It gains a section mapping `em.clock`, `em.store.*`, `em.jobs.*`, `em.formation` and `em.facade.contract` to what each holds, states that the core is not wired into the plugin yet (that is EM-211), and repeats the live-path refusal. Documentation only — no code, no behaviour.
- **Docs: Chunk 2 closed out on the plan, and Chunk 3 written.** `REMAINING_PLAN.md` §2 carries `8fca999de` and the post-Chunk-2 counts, §5 gains the chunk's commits, §6.1 records EM-209 as closed (its wording differences now live in the deviation table), §9 waits on Chunk 3, and §10's stale-branch list matches reality. `NEXT_CHUNK.md` Part B holds Chunk 3: the EM-210 plan gaps (a `snapshot()` that covers `index.db` in `backups/<ts>-<reason>/`, and the once-per-hour-per-reason throttle behind the "100 `forget` → ≤ 1 snapshot/hour" acceptance criterion).
- **EM-209: `entropicmem worker run`.** Claims and runs queued v3 jobs (`--once`, `--types`, `--max-seconds`). Today the only registered handler is `backup`. Unknown types are an error. A live path is refused unless `ENTROPICMEM_ALLOW_LIVE_MIGRATION=1`, and a database without the v3 `jobs` table is refused and left untouched. The command never migrates. One JSON line reports `done`, `failed`, `dead` and `lost`; exit code 1 if any job is dead.
- **EM-212's remaining plan AC is now tracked by a strict xfail.** Master plan §5 EM-212 requires the engine modules to move under a package namespace, so no unprefixed `vault`/`index`/`security`/`policy`/`embeddings`/`retrieval` module is registered in the host process. `test_em212_plan_ac_no_unprefixed_engine_modules_in_process` loads the engine in a clean interpreter the way the plugin does and asserts that. It fails today for the right reason (`vault`, `policy` and `embeddings` are registered) and flips when the package move lands. The plan, the foundations doc and the finding map now say EM-212 is only partly done.
- **Docs: `docs/plan/REMAINING_PLAN.md` and `docs/plan/NEXT_CHUNK.md`.** Development now stops at the 2.8.1 safe point and continues only in small, planned chunks. The remaining plan collects every rule, the ledger of what's done, all remaining cards by sprint, recorded deviations, known limitations and budgets. The next-chunk file holds exactly one next step. Master plan §5 card text is still to be pasted in verbatim by the agent that holds the plan.
- **Docs: `docs/V3_FOUNDATIONS.md` and `AGENTS.md`.** The foundations guide maps the v3 layers (`em.store`, `em.jobs`, `em.formation`, `em.facade`), states ten invariants with what enforces each, and gives recipes for adding a job type, a migration and a store module, plus backup/restore usage and what each remaining card builds on. `AGENTS.md` holds the working rules for agents: never / always / when to ask the owner.
- **EM-211 foundation: the provider's engine contract, pinned, plus a parity suite.** `em/facade/contract.py` records the exact API the Hermes provider uses on the engine: 13 methods, the keyword arguments it passes to each, and one raw attribute (`engine.db`). It also records the behaviour the provider relies on that no signature shows (`BEHAVIOURS`).
  - `tests/unit/test_em_facade_contract.py` re-derives the call table from the provider source by AST scan and fails if it differs from the recorded contract, so a new call site cannot slip past the facade. It also checks that every registered engine accepts every recorded call. Mutation-checked in both directions.
  - `tests/parity/test_engine_parity.py` runs one test per behaviour against every engine in `ENGINES`:
    - content-derived ids resolving (the provider's mirror lookup: `get_fact(make_id(content))`);
    - idempotent `remember`;
    - `forget` needing `confirm=True`;
    - tags as a list;
    - recall scores in [0, 1], sorted and filtered;
    - `touch` refreshing `last_accessed`;
    - monotonic episode waves;
    - the lock being released after an exception.

    Today the only engine is v2. The EM-211 facade is done when it is added to `ENGINES` and passes the whole file unchanged.
  - Writing the suite corrected one assumption: v2 `touch` refreshes `last_accessed` but does not bump `access_count`. The contract now says so.
  - One item is recorded as open: `_locate_mirror` reads `engine.db` directly (`SELECT … FROM facts`), which a v3 facade cannot offer. EM-211 must replace it with an engine method on both engines.
- **EM-210: backup manager.** `em/store/backup.py` adds `BackupManager` (`create`, `list`, `latest`, `verify`, `rotate`, `restore`) and a daily `backup` job for the EM-209 worker (`enqueue_daily_backup`, `make_backup_handler`).
  - **A backup counts only once it has been verified.** Each snapshot goes through the SQLite backup API into a `.partial` file, so it is consistent under a live writer and includes commits still in the WAL. The copy, not the source, is then checked: `integrity_check`, `user_version`, per-table row counts, and the audit hash chain on v3. Only then are it and its sha256 manifest renamed into place. A failed check deletes the partial and raises `BackupVerificationError`, so an unverified file never looks like a good backup. Backups are standalone single files (no `-wal`/`-shm` sidecars) at 0600, in a 0700 directory.
  - **`verify()` re-hashes a stored backup against its manifest** (catches bit rot, truncation and a manifest that lies about counts) before opening it.
  - **Restore refuses by default.** It refuses a live path unless `allow_live=True` (the same guard migrations use), refuses while the provider holds `<db>.lock`, and refuses a backup that no longer verifies. It keeps the current database first: a verified `pre-restore` backup, or, if the live database is too broken to snapshot, its raw `.db`/`-wal`/`-shm` files byte for byte. It deletes the old database's WAL before swapping, because frames from the replaced file would otherwise be replayed onto the restored one and silently revive what the restore removed (pinned by a test with real WAL frames).
  - **Rotation keeps safety backups on their own limit,** so routine backups can never push out a `pre-migrate`/`pre-restore` copy. Rotation only deletes files that have a manifest, never a hand-made copy or a pre-EM-210 backup.
  - **Manifests record the source file name, never its path,** because paths contain the user's home directory and backups travel.
  - **Migrations use it:** `migrate()`'s pre-migration backup now goes through `BackupManager`, so it is verified and manifested before any migration runs, and a backup that fails verification stops the migration. The file name is unchanged (`pre-migrate-v{f}-to-v{t}-<ts>.db`); the timestamp gains milliseconds, and a same-millisecond collision gets a `-2` suffix instead of overwriting.
- **EM-209: durable job queue and worker.** `em/store/jobs.py` adds `JobQueue` over the v3 `jobs` table; `em/jobs/` adds `JobWorker`, `HandlerRegistry`, `JobContext` and `PermanentJobError`. This is the path for everything slow or retryable (embeddings in EM-303, summaries, maintenance), so nothing slow ever runs inside a write transaction.
  - **Handlers run outside every transaction.** The worker uses one short write transaction to claim, one per heartbeat, and one to complete or fail. The handler runs between them, so an embedding or LLM call never holds the SQLite write lock. A test asserts the writer is not in a transaction while the handler runs.
  - **Claims are atomic across processes.** The claim runs under `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`. The acceptance test runs 4 worker processes against 300 jobs behind a start barrier: every job runs exactly once, and the work is shared. Claiming outside the transaction was mutation-checked and produces double runs.
  - **Leases, and a crash counts as an attempt.** A claim sets `locked_by`/`locked_until` and increments `attempts` up front, so a job whose handler kills the worker still reaches `max_attempts` and goes `dead` instead of taking down every worker in turn. An expired lease is reclaimable. `complete`, `fail` and `heartbeat` report a lost lease instead of overwriting the new holder's state. Delivery is at-least-once, so handlers must be idempotent.
  - **Retry and dead-letter:** exponential backoff from 30 s, capped at 1 h, with ±10 % jitter; `PermanentJobError` goes straight to `dead`; `retry_dead`, `prune` (done after 7 days, dead after 30), `stats`, `list` and `next_run_after` cover operations.
  - **A worker only claims types it has a handler for,** so an older build leaves newer job types queued instead of failing them.
  - **Dedupe:** one row per `dedupe_key`. A queued or failed job is refreshed; a running, done or dead one is left alone. `MemoryStore` now enqueues its embed job through `JobQueue.enqueue`, so exactly one place writes `jobs` rows.
  - **`last_error` holds only the exception type and a first line capped at 200 characters,** and the worker logs job id and type, never the payload.
  - Priority: a lower number runs first (0 is most urgent, 5 is the default).
- **EM-208: entities, aliases, relations, and the entity linker.** `em/store/entities.py` adds `EntityStore` (entities, aliases, memory links, relations, `entity_card`) and `em/formation/entity_linker.py` adds `EntityLinker.link(memory)` plus `time_link` for the benchmark.
  - **A capitalised phrase is only promoted to an entity on its second sighting, in a different memory.** One mention is a capitalised word, not an entity. The sighting counter lives in `meta` as a bounded list of distinct memory ids, so the decision survives a restart, is shared by every writer, and one memory mentioning a phrase five times still counts once. Without the threshold the graph fills with entity spam within a week.
  - **A registered entity links on first sighting.** The two-sighting rule only applies to *proposals*; something already in the graph is matched immediately.
  - **Alias lookup is n-gram (1–4 tokens), casefolded, punctuation stripped.** A short name, a two-word name and the same two-word name in shout case with a trailing comma all resolve to the same entity, and a 4-gram claims a mention before a 1-gram can, so a two-word name is not also linked as its own first word.
  - **Query text can never become a lookup key that scans.** `find_entity` is a single indexed probe on the normalised key; the n-gram list is built in memory.
  - **The built-in seed is off by default** (`entities.seed_builtin` is false, and the typed config is EM-407, so it is a parameter). Only the generic list from `triple_extract` is seeded, never anything user-specific.
  - **`entity_card` shows recently closed relations too**, because "used to work on X" is often the most useful line on a card, and it excludes memories whose status is `deleted`.
  - **AC met with room to spare:** linking p95 is 0.18 ms per memory against the 2 ms budget with 10,000 entities in scope (11x headroom), measured on a copy of the live database that already held 5,170 real entities.
- **EM-207: episodes and transcript chunks.** `em/store/episodes.py` adds `EpisodeStore` with `upsert_chunks`, `add_episode`, `upsert_episode`, `get_episode`, `list_episodes`, `search_episodes`, `prune_chunks` and `prune_episodes`.
  - **Chunks are content-addressed, which is what makes retries safe.** `transcript_chunks` is keyed by `sha256(session_id, seq, role, text)`, so re-running `upsert_chunks` with the same messages inserts nothing and returns 0 — the AC holds because the primary key is the content, not a counter. `seq` is part of the digest so a sentence repeated within one session stays two chunks.
  - **`add_episode` requires an explicit `session_id` for every kind except `manual`.** It used to default the session key to the new episode's own id, which made every episode its own `window_seq` 0 and silently destroyed window ordering. A manual episode still uses its own id, as the plan specifies.
  - **`upsert_episode` is a separate method** for the case where the caller knows which `(session_id, kind, window_seq)` it is filling and wants a retry to converge rather than raise. `add_episode` is a plain insert, so a duplicate attempt fails loudly instead of overwriting a window.
  - **`search_episodes` cannot be made to execute FTS syntax.** Query tokens are reduced to alphanumerics and quoted, and a query with no usable tokens falls back to a `LIKE` scan, so `" OR "x`, `NEAR(a b)` and `col:val` are searched for literally rather than parsed.
  - **`prune_chunks` keeps a session's chunks while *any* of its episodes is unsummarised.** Testing only the newest episode would let a summarised first window mask an unsummarised third one, and the summariser would be starved of exactly the transcript it still needs. Pending-ness is inferred from an empty summary because the v3 `episodes` table has no status column.
  - Retention window defaults to the plan's `privacy.transcript_retention_days` of 30. The typed config module is EM-407, so it is a parameter with that default rather than a config read here.
- **EM-205: the v3 memories repository.** `em/store/memories.py` adds `MemoryStore` with `add`, `update`, `supersede`, `set_status`, `get`, `list`, `history`, `touch` and `purge`, plus `em/store/types.py` for `Scope`, `MemoryDraft`, `MemoryPatch`, `WriteResult` and the §3.4 status machine. `InvalidTransition` refuses every edge the plan's diagram does not draw, and also refuses a legal edge with the wrong reason code.
  - The add pipeline runs in the plan's order: normalise → policy → PII redaction → injection screen → hash → duplicate check → insert → outbox → audit → embed job. **No embedding is computed inline**; `add` enqueues a job keyed `embed:<id>:<version>` and returns, so an embedding round trip never sits inside a SQLite write lock.
  - **Dedup is a query, not a caught constraint violation.** `ux_mem_hash_scope` is a *partial* unique index (`WHERE status IN ('active','pending')`), so superseded and deleted rows are exempt and the same content may legitimately be re-added. Leaning on `IntegrityError` would have permanently blocked re-adding anything that had once been superseded. `add` and `update` both query the hash under the same `WHERE` clause.
  - `update` re-redacts and re-hashes a changed content field *before* writing, and reports `duplicate_content` instead of aborting the transaction when the new hash collides with another live row.
  - `touch(ids, field="accessed")` never touches `last_injected_at`. A memory that has only been read must not look injected to the retrieval layer, which would skew the injection signals EM-2xx reads back.
  - `get` returns `None` for an ambiguous id prefix rather than picking an arbitrary row, so a short id can never silently fetch the wrong memory.
  - `_in_scope` implements the minimum viable §3.5 read rule (same profile, same user or profile-wide). The owner-only rule for sensitive rows needs the gateway identity and lands with the facade (EM-211).
  - The injection screen stores only finding *shapes*, never the matched evidence text, so a trust flag stays safe to write into the audit trail and the sync outbox.
- **EM-206: the v3 audit log is now hash-chained, verifiable, and readable from the CLI.** `em/store/audit.py` adds `append()` (called inside the caller's transaction, so a row and the change it describes commit together), `verify() -> VerifyResult(ok, first_bad_seq)` and the frozen digest `sha256(prev_hash || ts || action || actor || target_id || detail)`. The formula is duplicated from migration `0002` on purpose: that migration verifies the chain it rebuilt, so importing a shared helper would let a later edit silently invalidate a historical database. `detail` is stored canonically (`sort_keys`, no whitespace) so key order cannot change a digest, and any value longer than 128 chars is rejected, which is what keeps a fact body or transcript chunk out of the log.
  - New CLI: `entropicmem audit verify` prints `{"ok": true, "checked": N}` and exits non-zero with `first_bad_seq` on a break. The existing `entropicmem audit` listing is unchanged. The command reads the chain directly rather than through the v2 engine, because the v2 `audit_log` is unchained and would have reported OK on a tampered v3 store.
  - Verified against real data: a copy of the live 2.8.0 database migrates to 7168 chained rows and `verify()` confirms every one.
  - **Known limitation, now pinned by a test:** deleting a middle row *and* re-chaining the tail produces a chain that is internally consistent, so no adjacency check can detect it. Seq contiguity is not available as a defence either, because `0002` deliberately preserves the original v2 ids in `seq`. Closing this needs an anchor outside the database (publishing or signing the head digest); deliberately not built here.

- **EM-204: migration `0002_v3_core` — the v3 schema and the lossless move of every v2 row into it.** Creates the v3 tables (`memories`, `relations`, `entities`, `episodes`, `jobs`, `audit_log`, `meta`, plus external-content `memories_fts`/`episodes_fts` with their sync triggers), renames each v2 source table to `v2_<name>` and keeps it for one release, then copies the data across with new type-prefixed ULIDs while remembering the old id in `legacy_id`. The original ids stay readable: the migration never commits, and a fresh v2 store ends up on `user_version=2` with all v3 tables.
- **EM-204: in-migration parity verification that aborts rather than migrates quietly.** After the data move the migration re-derives expected counts from the renamed v2 tables and compares them per table; it also recomputes `content_hash` for every moved memory, recomputes the audit chain hash for every row, and probes both FTS indexes. Any mismatch raises `ParityError` inside the migration transaction, so the runner rolls the whole thing back and the database stays at its previous version. Seven independent mutations (drop a row, corrupt content, break the chain, drop an FTS trigger, skip dedup, skip episodes, tamper a stored audit row) were each confirmed to be caught, rather than assumed to be.
- **EM-204: v2 rows that v3 cannot represent are preserved, not dropped.** Orphaned `embeddings` and `fact_versions` rows (rows whose parent no longer exists — the real store has 6 and 40) stay in the `v2_*` tables and their counts are recorded in `meta` as `orphans_*`, so the parity arithmetic still balances to the exact v2 row count. Nothing is deleted and nothing dangles under `foreign_keys=ON`.
- **EM-204: rich synthetic v2 fixture builder** (`tests/fixtures/db/build_rich_v2.py`). The committed v2 fixtures hold only two rows, which is far too little to exercise dedup, episodes, archives, orphaned children or the timestamp shapes the real store actually contains, so the builder reconstructs a v2.7 database with all of them from `0001`'s exact schema. It deliberately opens with `foreign_keys=OFF`, because that is how v2 runs and is the only reason orphaned rows can exist in a real store at all — a builder that enforced foreign keys could not reproduce the condition the migration has to survive.

- **EM-203: migration framework — `em/store/migrations/`.** Each migration is a module named `NNNN_<slug>.py` exposing `VERSION: int`, `NAME: str` and `def up(conn)`. `discover()` loads every numbered module in version order and rejects malformed modules, duplicate versions and gaps in the numbering (migrations must be contiguous from 1, otherwise `user_version` stops meaning "everything up to N has been applied"). `migrate()` backs the database up first via the SQLite backup API to `backups/pre-migrate-v{from}-to-{to}-<ts>.db` (0600, consistent even with a live WAL), then applies each pending migration in its **own** transaction together with its `schema_migrations` row and the `PRAGMA user_version` bump, so a failure mid-migration leaves the database exactly at the last good version. Re-running is a no-op and writes no extra backup; a partially migrated database resumes at the next version instead of re-applying. A database newer than the build raises `DowngradeError`. Editing an already-applied migration raises `MigrationChecksumMismatch` (the recorded `checksum` is `sha256` of the module source) rather than being silently treated as applied.
- **EM-203: development safety guard — migrations refuse to touch a live database.** `assert_safe_db_path()` resolves the path (so `.../entropicmem/../entropicmem/memory.db` cannot dodge the check) and raises `MigrationRefused` for anything outside a temporary or test location; `~/.hermes/entropicmem` and `~/.hermes/entropicmem-live-2.8.0` are denied outright. In-memory databases are always allowed. `migrate()` runs the guard *before* opening a backup or writing anything, so a refused database is not touched at all. The real 3.0 cutover opts in with `ENTROPICMEM_ALLOW_LIVE_MIGRATION=1`, which nothing in development or CI sets.
- **EM-203: migration `0001_baseline_v27`** — the frozen copy of v2's `MemoryEngine._init_schema()` that brings any v2.x store (or an empty database) to the exact v2.7 shape. Every statement is `IF NOT EXISTS` or guarded by a `PRAGMA table_info` check, so it is idempotent across v2.3/v2.5/v2.7/v2.8. `up()` never commits (the runner owns transaction boundaries; `executescript` is deliberately avoided because it issues an implicit COMMIT that would break atomicity). Faithfulness was verified against the real historical engines rather than by reading source: `embeddings` is created unconditionally (v2 gated it on optional deps, but EM-204 moves its rows), `facts_archive` is only *upgraded*, never invented, and `SHARED_SCHEMA` is excluded because it belongs to the separate shared store.
- **EM-203: committed v2.x migration fixtures** (`tests/fixtures/db/v2_{3,5,7}_0.db`) plus `generate_fixtures.py`, which extracts each release commit with `git archive` and runs that release's **real** `MemoryEngine` against a fresh database. An earlier regex-scraping version of the generator was measurably wrong — it emitted `embeddings` and `facts_archive` tables that real v2.7.0 code does not create — so the generator executes the historical code instead of approximating it. Fixtures are ~25-32 KB, contain exactly two clearly synthetic rows, and are committed so CI needs neither git history nor the historical imports.
- **EM-202: portable storage core — `em/store/` (`db.py`, `locking.py`).** `open_db()` is the single connection factory for the v3 core: WAL, `synchronous=NORMAL`, `busy_timeout=5000`, `foreign_keys=ON`, `temp_store=MEMORY`, `mmap_size=64MiB`, `isolation_level=None` (explicit transactions only), readonly connections via the SQLite URI `mode=ro`, POSIX perms 0600 file / 0700 dir. `write_txn()` is a `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` … `COMMIT`/`ROLLBACK` context manager with up to 3 jittered retries on SQLITE_BUSY — **no advisory file locks**: cross-process serialisation is SQLite's (BEGIN IMMEDIATE + busy_timeout), proven by the card's concurrency AC (4 writer processes × 500 writes + 1 reader: zero errors, all 2000 rows, reader p95 < 50 ms). `migration_lock()` guards what SQLite cannot see: an `O_CREAT|O_EXCL` lock file (portable, no fcntl/msvcrt), stale after 10 minutes. `ensure_schema()` implements the `PRAGMA user_version` fast path (== LATEST skips all DDL) and refuses DBs newer than the code (`DowngradeError`). `Store` keeps per-thread reader connections (`threading.local`) and ONE process-wide writer serialised by a `threading.Lock`. Stdlib-only, no Hermes imports, no env reads.
- **EM-201: `em/` package scaffold + clock & ULID identifiers** (`plugins/entropicmem/scripts/em/`). `em.clock` is the single source of time truth for the v3 storage core: `utc_now()` (freezable via `em.clock.freeze(dt)` for tests/evals), `to_iso()` (UTC, millisecond precision, `Z` suffix), `parse_iso()` (accepts the v3 canonical form plus the legacy shapes the v2 engine wrote — SQLite `CURRENT_TIMESTAMP` space-separated, `+00:00` and arbitrary offsets, naive-as-UTC), `new_id(prefix)` (type-prefixed ULID: 48-bit ms timestamp + 80 random bits, Crockford base32, sorts chronologically) and `short_id()` (last 8 chars, the citation form). Stdlib-only, no Hermes imports, no environment reads. 41 unit tests including the AC property test (`parse_iso(to_iso(x)) == x` at ms precision) and ULID sort order; mutation-checked.

### Changed

- **Document control: the expected test counts have ONE home (`docs/plan/BASELINE.md`), and the plan pages reference it instead of restating it.** `MASTER_TODO.md`, `docs/plan/NEXT_CHUNK.md` and `docs/plan/REMAINING_PLAN.md` each hand-copied the `passed / skipped / xfailed` triple, so every chunk cost a three-page edit and a missed page read as a real mismatch — which the rule on mismatch ("stop and report") turns into a full re-measure. It happened: on 2026-10-17 a push went red on **all four legs** because `MASTER_TODO.md` said `2755 / 4 / 3` while the other two said `2756 / 4 / 3` — a docs-only failure that cost a full CI re-run; the same content re-pushed fifteen minutes later as `8dc725a` was green on every leg. **The three anchor lines now carry no figure at all**; each points at `BASELINE.md`, which holds the CI figure of record, the three local readings, the **legend** (the triple is `passed / skipped / xfailed` and **`failed` is a fourth number, always 0 on a green run** — a skip is an absent dependency, not a failure), **CI's four skips named individually**, and the measured bare→CI ladder. The guard in `tests/test_master_todo.py` is rewritten to match: it reads the canonical triple from the source's figure-of-record row (keyed by marker, so it cannot grab one of the three *local* readings printed a few rows below and mistake it for drift), and asserts each of the three pages **references the source and states no triple of its own**. The old cross-page-agreement comparison is gone, because there is nothing left to cross-compare — and with it goes the failure mode it existed to catch. Deliberately **not** rewritten: the historical count-drift chain in `REMAINING_PLAN.md` §11, which records what each landing measured and is evidence, not a forward-looking baseline.
  - **Evidence:** 6 tests in `tests/test_master_todo.py` (the source parses and is plausible; every page references it and restates nothing; the page set is pinned by name; the source is not one of the pages it owns; the reference check rejects a page that restates the figure; the parser keys on the marker row and refuses zero or two of them), with the two pure functions (`_canonical_baseline`, `_reference_is_intact`) kept separable so the violating input can be fed directly — the same reason the old comparison was a pure function. The 2026-10-17 `cb88d47` failure is cited in the guard's own comment as the incident.

- **The 50k warm-p95 gate's *measurement* is now a tracked defect item (`EM-608`), and re-running a red `main` job is recorded as a workaround rather than a fix.** `tests/harness/test_prefetch_service_ac.py::test_warm_p95_at_50k_is_under_150ms` reports one p95 over 25 probes; on a shared runner that number moves with the host, and it has now flapped twice in two days on **unchanged behaviour** — 2026-10-16 (`6e86c98`, `test (3.10)`, p95 **153.35 ms**, all 25 samples 117–158 ms) and 2026-10-18 (`bdc1e20`, `test (3.13)`, p95 **151.09 ms**, run `39112375641`, job `117631038052`), both green on the same SHA's branch run and on the rerun. **The gate's verdict currently depends on which runner it lands on, so it does not gate.** `docs/plan/PERF_GATE_CARD_DRAFT.md` specifies the fix as **a statistic, not a rerun** — best-of-N windows, exactly the fix EM-407 applied to the numpy guard a test over — with a dedicated non-blocking job as the fallback, and it **forbids widening the 150 ms budget without the owner**. Also recorded: **the owner recalled three failures and only two are verifiable, because re-running a failed job overwrites its log** — a sweep of every failed CI job's log from 2026-09-20 to 2026-10-19 returns zero warm-p95 hits for exactly that reason. A flake instance must be written down when it happens; GitHub will not keep it.

- **Scope correction: the owner's 2026-10-18 word authorised **EM-601** (one card, both halves), not "S6".** The record said "**S6 IS OPEN** … the S6 card list is agent-owned now" — an extrapolation from the owner naming one card as the session's build, and it is retracted across `MASTER_TODO.md`, `STOP_HERE.md`, `NEXT_CHUNK.md` and `REMAINING_PLAN.md`. What the owner actually said was *"you make the logical calls on the build"*, with the target named as **chunk 36 = EM-601 slice 1**; slice 2's go came explicitly the same day. **S6's remaining cards (EM-602…EM-607), S7 security, packaging and the 3.0 release are not authorised by that word** and stay behind `STOP_HERE.md`'s gates. The wider question — does the word open EM-601 only, or all of S6 and beyond? — is **recorded as an open question for the owner** in `NEXT_CHUNK.md`'s banner, and **nothing else depends on the answer**: slice 2 is explicitly authorised and proceeds.

- **EM-409 — the system prompt: Appendix D's policy text, and Core blocks within `core.token_budget`.** `system_prompt_block()` no longer opens with the ad-hoc `# EntropicMem (active)` base: the block renders **Appendix D's policy text** (§4.5) word for word (the plan's mid-sentence wraps as single-line bullets), and **the tool-name bullets are conditional** — each renders only for the tools the provider actually registers (Appendix D's own note, the card's "phrase conditionally"): the search-the-past bullet names whichever of `entropicmem_search`/`entropicmem_timeline` is present and drops entirely when neither is; `entropicmem_remember`'s and `entropicmem_update`'s bullets follow their tools. The owed alias rewrite is done: the prompt names the new nine and no longer mentions `entropicmem_recall`/`entropicmem_query` (the aliases themselves still route for one minor version). The Core Memory pair (Persona / User Profile, still screened for injection first) is rendered **within `core.token_budget`** (§4.3, default 800; `budget × 4` characters against §3.6's `ceil(len/4)` estimator) — the hardcoded `[:2800]` character slice is gone, and the cap lives in `_core_memory_block()` so every path that injects core (the system prompt, the legacy full-block prefetch mode, the changed-core delta and its baseline hash) is bounded by the one budget. The skill-not-installed fallback is unchanged; the host-withholds-the-toolset behaviour is unchanged (host-side).
  - **Evidence:** 8 new tests (`tests/unit/test_em409_system_prompt.py`: the full-block snapshot — policy plus both core sections; policy alone with core off; the AC's token count — policy ≤ 350, core ≤ budget, total ≤ 350 + budget; a custom `core.token_budget` respected; the four conditional-tool-line cases; the legacy names gone) plus three deliberate repoints, each the changed surface's own claim: `test_core_inject`'s cap test now drives `core.token_budget` (100) instead of the 2800-character slice, and the two byte-identity goldens (`test_provider_v3_retrieval.py`, `test_em403_slice3_wiring.py`) repoint their `system_prompt` field only — the prefetch fields are untouched. Suite **2591 / 16 / 3** bare on 3.11 (was 2583 / 16 / 3), **2592 / 15 / 3** with the digest list in place, **2669 / 2 / 3** on CI-equivalent 3.12 with the digest list (was 2661 / 2 / 3); **8 mutation checks, all red** (the remember guard, the update guard, the search-past conditional, the budget slice, the budget multiplier, a policy word, the core append, the cite bullet) — each restored from a byte copy; `ruff==0.16.2` clean.

- **The shadow's miss ceiling is ARMED, from EM-306's holdout — the pre-registered rule, executed.** `_shadow.PROMOTION["max_v2_miss_rate"] = 0.107143`, computed as `1 − recall@5` of the **v2 adapter** on the EM-306 holdout split (16 scenarios, 28 scored turns; `evals/results/tune-hard-4eb8097.json`). The rule text now carries the number and the file, and keeps the policy statement it must not lose: the rate it gates is **v3-vs-v2**, the bound is **v2-vs-truth** applied to it — a policy choice, not an identity. The un-armed shape (`None` → `not armed`, blocking `met`) still exists and is still loud; its tests now put the value back to `None` deliberately.
  - **What this changes:** a shadow sample can now read `met` if all five conditions pass; before, the verdict could never exceed `cannot conclude`. **Nothing else moves** — the cutover is still the owner's call, and no threshold was moved to make anything pass: 0.107143 is what v2's own holdout misses produced. A test pins the whole chain (holdout v2 recall → result file → `PROMOTION` → the rule text), so any link drifting is caught.
  - Evidence: the observable pin, the rule test, the un-armed path (monkeypatched back to `None`), the render check and the provider pin updated deliberately, not to get green; two rule mutations caught; **2090 / 3 / 3** on both Pythons; ruff clean.

- **The miss-ceiling arming rule now states what it always meant: a policy choice, not an identity.** `_shadow.MISS_CEILING_RULE`'s first draft said the bound was "measured over the same corpus the shadow sample is drawn from" — a category error, since the bound is **v2-vs-truth** (`1 − recall@5` of the v2 adapter on EM-306's eval holdout) while the shadow's rate is **v3-vs-v2** (ids v3 dropped that v2 injected, over ids v2 injected, on real turns). **The threshold is unchanged: `max_v2_miss_rate` is still `None` and un-armed**, and every un-armed surface is unchanged (rate reported, rule printed on every reading, verdict `not armed`, `met` blocked). The rule now names both quantities and says applying the second as a ceiling on the first is a stated policy — "v3 may drop no more of v2's hits than v2 itself misses against truth" — not a like-for-like comparison. Corrected before any real turn was read, per the owner's standing instruction.
  - Evidence: the rule's test now pins `v3-vs-v2`, `v2-vs-truth` and "policy choice, not an identity"; two rule mutations caught.

- **The shadow promotion observable is split in two, by owner ruling on 2026-10-08 — before a single real turn was read, which is the only circumstance in which amending a frozen observable is not fitting it to the sample.** `_shadow.PROMOTION` retires the symmetric `max_divergence_rate = 0.10` and replaces it with two ceilings that measure different failures:
  - **`max_v3_only = 0` stays hard and non-negotiable.** v3 injecting a memory v2 did not have is a correctness/safety property, not a tuning knob, and the render now says so on the line itself.
  - **`max_v2_miss_rate` is the recall side — ids v3 dropped over ids v2 injected — and it is `None`: NOT ARMED.** Its arming rule is pre-registered in code as `_shadow.MISS_CEILING_RULE`: arm it at *v2's own miss rate against a held-out reference* (`1 − recall@5` of the v2 adapter on EM-306's holdout split, over the same corpus), committed with a comment linking the result file. The ruling allowed either an absolute rate with a stated rationale or this relative form; the relative one is registered because no defensible absolute number exists before real turns, **and because it cannot be fitted to the shadow sample — it is measured on a different dataset.**
  - **Un-armed is loud, never silent.** An un-armed ceiling still reports the miss rate, prints the arming rule on every reading (render *and* `--json`), returns verdict `not armed`, and **blocks `met`** — the sample reads `cannot conclude`. The ungated dimension therefore cannot be skimmed as a pass, which is precisely the accident the ruling forbade.
  - **Why it changed:** the measured 240-turn sample was **150 misses and 0 additions**, so the symmetric ceiling failed a system for being *more selective* — the behaviour EM-305's gate exists to produce (adapter noise 0.172 vs v2's 0.219) — while the condition the observable itself called decisive passed on every line. A gate that fails on a non-safety metric blocks a better engine.
  - **The retired number is still reported** (`context.divergence_rate_symmetric`) so the earlier 37.5% finding stays comparable rather than restated: on the same sample the new id-level miss rate is **38.46% (150 of 390 ids)**, with the turn-level counts (90 of 240) beside it.
  - **A test-fixture defect that mutation testing found and review did not:** the first id-level definition test used one id per turn, where id-level and turn-level rates are *numerically identical*, so two mutations — reintroducing the symmetric rate, and silently switching to turn-level — both survived. The fixture now injects 4/2/1 ids across turns so the three candidate definitions disagree (4/7 vs 2/3), and the test asserts they are distinguishable. **26 mutation checks, all caught**, tree restored byte-identical.
  - `tests/unit/test_provider_shadow.py`'s observable pin was updated **deliberately, not to get green**: it now asserts the split shape, that `max_v2_miss_rate is None`, that `max_divergence_rate` is gone for good, and that the arming rule is written down — and its docstring records the ruling and its date.
  - Evidence: **2061 passed / 3 skipped / 3 xfailed** on both Python 3.10 and 3.12 (+6 net tests), `ruff==0.16.2` clean, v2 eval gate unmoved.

- **EM-211 (Chunk 10.4, completing CLI parity): the CLI runs on whichever engine the store needs.** `entropicmem`'s `_engine()` no longer refuses a v3 store — it selects through the same `open_engine` the provider uses, so a v2 store gets the v2 engine and a v3 store the facade, decided by `PRAGMA user_version`, read read-only before either is constructed. The CLI's ported commands (reads from 10.1, maintenance from 10.2) now work against a v3 store, and the v2-only commands still refuse by name (10.3) because their guards run *before* `_engine()`. **EM-211's acceptance criterion is met for every command except those seven**, each of which is deferred to the card that will bring it — recorded as a deliberate, itemised exception rather than a silent one.
  - **The v2 regression pass is the point of this chunk**, because routing changed engine construction for *every* store, not just v3 ones. Tests run the real CLI as a subprocess against a real v3 store (remember → memory list → recall, history, audit, pending, episode stats, timeline) and against a v2 store, including that a v2 store is **not migrated** by the CLI touching it.

- **Fixed alongside it: Chunk 9 had silently made the profile explicit, which would have changed what a v2 store is stamped with.** `MemoryEngine.profile_id()` resolves `explicit > hermes_home basename > 'default'`, so passing `profile_id="default"` overrides the home-derived slug a v2 store has always carried. Chunk 9's provider wiring passed `self._profile_id or "default"`, turning a `None` ("let the engine resolve it") into an explicit `"default"` — so a provider running under a named home such as `~/.hermes-profiles/alice` would have stamped `profile_id="default"` instead of `"alice"`. `open_engine` now takes `profile_id=None` meaning **"the engine decides"**, the v2 branch passes it through untouched, the facade coerces for its own scope, and the CLI passes nothing. Two regression tests pin it — one on the provider path (where the bug lived and where no existing test looked: reverting the fix reddens exactly that one test) and one on the CLI path.

- **EM-211 (Chunk 10.3): the CLI refuses its v2-only features on a v3 store, by name.** Five features have **no v3 backing store at all** — knowledge triples, the embeddings rebuild, vault projection, the shared publish/pull log, and the v2 provenance migration — plus two v2-only forms of commands that are otherwise ported (`recall --related`, `recall --scope shared|all`). The CLI is routed onto the facade in 10.4; without these guards those commands would then fail with an `AttributeError`, and a stack trace is not an answer. Each now exits up front, **before `_engine()` is reached**, naming the card that will bring it (`S3`/`EM-303`, `S5`, `S6`, or "the v2 provenance migration"). The tests monkeypatch `_engine` to raise, so a passing test is one where the refusal fired first — which is what makes them meaningful now, while 10.0's coarser refusal is still in force.
  - The `memory project` guard sits at the top of `cmd_memory`, before any path is resolved, so a command that is about to refuse does not build a `Vault`/`VaultIndex` and create directories for nothing.
  - Evidence: 12 new tests (`tests/unit/test_em_cli_refusals.py`), including one asserting every refusal message names a trigger — a refusal that does not say when it lifts is just a wall.

- **The CLI refuses a v3 store clearly instead of misreading it.** `entropicmem` builds its engine in `_engine()`, and the CLI calls about 28 methods the v3 facade does not implement, so it cannot be pointed at the facade yet. Handing a v3 store to the v2 engine made commands fail confusingly part-way through; `_engine()` now checks the store's `user_version` (read-only) and exits with a clear message naming the situation. It still opens v2 stores, and stores that do not exist yet, exactly as before. This is the first step of the CLI-parity route; until parity lands, the Hermes agent can read a v3 store and the CLI says so rather than guessing. Evidence: 3 new tests (v3 refused, v2 opened, a fresh path opened).

- **EM-211 (Chunk 9): the provider selects its engine by the store's own schema version.** The provider constructed v2's `MemoryEngine` unconditionally. It now picks: a v2 store gets `MemoryEngine`, a v3 store gets the `V3Engine` facade, decided by `PRAGMA user_version`. **All nine** `MemoryEngine(...)` sites in `plugins/entropicmem/__init__.py` route through one `_open_engine()` method, and a drift guard fails if a tenth raw construction appears.
  - **Why selection and not a swap — and why the check runs first.** `V3Engine.__init__` calls `migrate()`, so constructing it over a v2 store would perform the v2-to-v3 cutover as a side effect of merely opening the store. The version is therefore read **read-only, before any engine is constructed**, by a new tested selector (`em/facade/select.py`). The rule: `user_version == 0` → v2 (the v2 engine never sets the pragma; all three committed v2 fixtures are 0, and a store that does not exist yet is created by the v2 engine exactly as before); `1..LATEST` → v3, where the facade's `migrate()` is then a no-op; `> LATEST` → refused, as `migrate()` already refuses it; a versioned store with no `memories` table → refused, so our migrations never run against a database that is not ours. **The cutover stays the owner's separate, deliberate act.**
  - The provider now threads the **gateway identity** into the facade — `scope_user = gateway id`, `is_owner = not _is_guest()` — which is the half of the §3.5 owner-only rule Chunk 7.2 left for the wiring. An empty `owner_user_ids` (the default) means nobody is a guest, so the default install stays the owner context exactly as v2 did.
  - **The CLI is deliberately NOT wired, and this is the sequencing fact worth knowing:** the facade implements the provider's 13 calls (`PROVIDER_CALLS`), but the CLI calls about **28 more** (`list_facts`, `rebuild_fts`, `timeline`, `triple_*`, `publish`/`pull`, `rebuild_embeddings`, …). Routing `_engine()` to the facade would break most CLI commands, so the CLI stays on `MemoryEngine` and CLI-parity is its own card. **The v3 cutover needs that card too**, because after a cutover the CLI would be reading a v3 store with a v2 engine.
  - **Recorded deviation:** `pii_locales` has no facade equivalent — v3 redaction is `pii.redact_pii` with no locale packs — so a v3 store loses the locale-aware PII pass v2 had. The argument is routed to the v2 engine only and cannot leak into the facade.
  - Evidence: 16 new tests (`tests/unit/test_em_engine_select.py`), **1687 passed / 3 skipped / 3 xfailed on both Python 3.10 and 3.12**; eval gate with no gated metric regressed; `perf-smoke` warm p95 6.776 ms against the unchanged 20 ms budget; `ruff==0.16.2` clean.
