Seven mutation checks, each red for the right reason with the tree restored byte-identical: the selector always picking each engine, the newer-than-build refusal, the no-`memories` refusal, a missing file treated as v3, the provider always claiming ownership, and a tenth raw construction. The most important test is that **opening a v2 store leaves it byte-for-byte unmigrated**.
  - **Mutation-tooling note:** the first mutation run gave a false "restored" failure because two mutants differed by one byte ('V2'→'V3') within the same mtime second, so Python reused a stale `.pyc`. Re-run with bytecode writing disabled and caches cleared, all seven were caught cleanly. Worth knowing for any future harness that writes and re-imports a module.

- **Autonomy rule: act, don't wait.** `AGENTS.md` ("Ask the owner only for" → "Autonomy: decide and act") and `REMAINING_PLAN.md` §3.3/§6.6 now make waiting on the owner the exception rather than the default. The agent decides and reports for cards, plan edits, dispatch, code review, CI triage, docs and bug fixes. The owner is asked only for actions that are irreversible or public: real data / the live store (including the v3 cutover), releases, tags and the Hermes catalog entry. A test that cannot pass without weakening it stays a hard stop — report the gate, never weaken the test. §6.6's S4 decision gate becomes an agent call with a written recommendation the owner can veto.

- **The migration guard's live-store deny list is no longer pinned to one clone version.** `assert_safe_db_path()` enumerated `~/.hermes/entropicmem` and the then-current pinned clone `~/.hermes/entropicmem-live-2.8.0`. The host's clone is renamed on every repoint (it is now `entropicmem-live-2.8.1`), so the entry went stale — and it failed *silently*: the catch-all "not inside a temporary or test path" rule still refuses the path, so nothing looked wrong while the specific live-store protection was gone. The guard now matches the family (`entropicmem` or any `entropicmem-live-*`) under `~/.hermes` instead of one version, and a new test asserts the refusal *message* so it cannot pass on the catch-all again. Evidence: `test_guard_refuses_any_versioned_live_clone`, mutation-checked in both directions (reverting to the pinned enumeration goes red on `2.8.1` and `9.9.9`; the tree was restored).

- **Plan: Chunk 4 closed out and Chunk 5 scoped.** `docs/plan/NEXT_CHUNK.md` Part A records the facade reads (`0349b7b4b`, em-qa approved) and Part B now scopes **Chunk 5: EM-211's facade writes** — the 7 `NotImplementedError` stubs, measured from the code at 546 lines of v2 write logic across `remember` (201), `consolidate` (139), `extract_and_store` (63), `forget` (59), `add_episode` (49), `prune_pending` (21) and `touch` (14), each mapped to the `em.store` call that already backs it. The plan fixes its own stale state: §2 SHA/counts, §5 ledger (Chunk 4 and the contributor's `tests/test_vault.py` fix), §6.1 split status, §9 decision, and §11 (state table, next piece, the 1542 expected count, and the EM-211 sub-chunk numbering — reads = Chunk 4, writes = 5, mirror = 6, linker = 7).

- **Docs: Chunk 3.2 closed out on the plan, and Chunk 4 scoped.** `REMAINING_PLAN.md` §2 moves to `e058e93be` and names Chunk 3.2, §5 gains its ledger row, §6.1 records both halves of the EM-210 gaps as done (and retention as settled), §9 keeps one open item (when to start the next chunk) and moves retention to a recorded decision, §11's state table and ordered next steps lead with Chunk 4, and the pre-flight count moves to 1519 with the live-store reading written as a pair. `NEXT_CHUNK.md` files Chunk 3.2 under Part A and scopes **Chunk 4: EM-211's facade reads** in Part B: the read half of `LegacyEngine` over `em.store`, the `legacy_id` lookup, and the engine registered in both parity harnesses, with the writes, the mirror call and the linker left to their own later chunks, and a recon-first sizing step so the number comes from the code before any test is written.
- **CI `perf-smoke` prints the whole warm distribution, not just the gated number.** The job asserts the 1k prefetch warm p95 against the unchanged 20 ms budget (2× §6.4), but a bare `p95 34.815ms exceeds 20.0ms` gave no way to tell one noisy sample on a shared runner from a real regression. It now prints p50, p95, max and the sample count (the numbers it already computed), and on failure points at the recorded 2026-09-27 flap: 34.8 ms on a docs-only commit, which the next commit passed with no code change (now in plan §8, with the 6.175 ms dev-box reference). No threshold moved.
- **CI: the pinned GitHub actions move to their current majors** — `actions/checkout@v4` → `@v7` (8 steps) and `actions/setup-python@v5` → `@v7` (7 steps) across all eight jobs in `.github/workflows/test.yml`. Every run already printed `Node.js 20 is deprecated` eleven times, with GitHub force-running the old pins on Node 24. No job uses what changed between those majors (no `pull_request_target` checkout, no setup-python `pip-install` input). CI-only: no product code, no test and no gate threshold changes.
- **`AGENTS.md`: two catalog rules.** Never rewrite published history or delete a pinned commit, branch or tag: the Hermes catalog pins an exact SHA, and a vanished pin makes the entry uninstallable and breaks `hermes plugins update` for installed copies (asked by the catalog maintainer after the 2.8.0 republish). And never change the provider's tools or the `provides_*` lists outside a release that re-pins the catalog.
- **EM-213, re-scoped and done: `plugin.yaml` declares tools and hooks once.** The duplicate `hooks:` list is removed at the Hermes catalog maintainer's request. `provides_tools`/`provides_hooks` stay. The original F-011 finding asked for them to go, but the 2.8.0 catalog review verified the plugin against exactly those lists, so they are the single, exact declaration (pinned to `register()` by `tests/test_plugin_hardening.py`). The strict xfail that demanded their removal is replaced by `test_f011_plugin_manifest_declares_tools_and_hooks_once`, which pins the re-scoped requirement and is mutation-checked (a re-added `hooks:` list fails it). `hermes plugins validate` still prints its two "declared but not registered" warnings; that is a validator limitation for MemoryProviders, documented in the README.
- **EM-203: `pyproject.toml` packages `em.store.migrations`** alongside `em` and `em.store` (the same silent-omission bug EM-202 hit one level up: without it the migration modules would be missing from the wheel).
- **EM-203: `db.LATEST_USER_VERSION` now resolves lazily from the migration registry** via `resolve_latest_user_version()`, which `ensure_schema()` calls. The registry lives in a module that depends on `db`, so `db` could only pick the value up as an import side effect — importing `em.store.db` on its own left it at 0 and `ensure_schema` would then reject any already-migrated database as a "downgrade". The lazy import avoids that without creating a circular import. Pinned by a test that runs in a fresh interpreter and asserts `em.store.migrations` was never imported.
- **EM-203: `.gitignore` no longer swallows the committed migration fixtures** (`!tests/fixtures/db/*.db`). The blanket `*.db` rule would have silently excluded them, and because the tests skipped on a missing fixture the card's main acceptance criterion would have gone vacuous in CI rather than failing. The migration tests now hard-fail on a missing fixture, and assert every fixture contains only the two clearly synthetic rows.
- **EM-202: Windows is a supported platform — the v2 engine and CLI no longer import platform lock modules.** `memory_engine.py` dropped its top-level `import fcntl` (the exact line that made `import memory_engine` fail on Windows) and the CLI's `_provider_running()` dropped its inline fcntl probe; both now use `em.store.locking.FileLock` (fcntl on POSIX, `msvcrt.locking` byte-range on Windows, identical semantics, interoperating with locks taken by older builds). The engine's reentrant write-lock counter is unchanged. A drift-guard test fails if a direct `fcntl`/`msvcrt` import grows back outside `em/store/locking.py`. The CI `windows-import` job loses `continue-on-error` and now runs the store test suite on windows-latest.
- **EM-202: `pyproject.toml` packages `em.store`** alongside `em` (the wheel was silently missing the subpackage).
- **EM-201: version is now single-sourced from `em.__version__`** (`3.0.0.dev0`, PEP 440 dev for the v3 line). `pyproject.toml` declares `dynamic = ["version"]` read via `[tool.setuptools.dynamic] attr = "em.__version__"` (this also fixes the previously broken flat-layout package discovery — wheels now build, packaging `em/`); the CLI `entropicmem.py` imports it instead of re-declaring a literal; `plugin.yaml` carries the same value, pinned by `tests/unit/test_em_version.py`. `skills/entropicmem/SKILL.md` intentionally stays at 2.8.0 (it describes the installed skill's behaviour, which is still 2.8.0 until 3.0 ships; bumped at EM-806/EM-904).
- **CI test matrix adds Python 3.13** and `pyproject.toml` gains the 3.13 classifier: Hermes Agent supports 3.13 and the full suite (927 passed, 2 skipped, 5 xfailed) already passes on it at 2.8.0. Python 3.14 is deliberately not added (Hermes cannot run on it).
- **CHANGELOG wording:** the EM-212/EM-213 known-limitation note no longer cites the old PR number (pre-cutover numbering doesn't exist in this repo); it now says "an earlier attempt was closed unmerged, pre-2.8.0 history".

### Fixed

- **EM-603's 5k AC de-flaked — the wall-clock budget becomes a ratio against the same run's full rebuild.** `tests/unit/test_em603_mtime_index.py`'s AC asserted `warm_ms < 300` against a raw `time.perf_counter`, so it moved with the host: on a contended Windows runner the *same green* commit read **440 ms** with a **59.8 s** cold rebuild and the `windows-import` job went red (run `39216670780`, job `117942029064`); the branch push on that SHA was green and a re-run landed green, but a re-run is a workaround, not a gate — the EM-608 lesson (EM-604's entry above records that instance). The AC now measures the card's claim in the machine-independent form the incremental path exists for: the budget is **`max(300 ms, 5% of this run's own cold rebuild)`**. The card's **300 ms still binds on any machine whose full rebuild is under 6 s** — measured here at **28.8 ms warm against 4115.7 ms cold, 0.7%** of cold, a ~7× margin — and a slow or contended runner scales the budget with itself instead of failing a correct change; the absolute figure is kept as the floor, **not dropped, and not silently weakened**. The walk's other half — that `iter_note_stats` resolves no path per file, the cost a wall-clock budget can only catch on a slow host (it read **398 ms** on the CI 3.12 runner when it regressed, and passes at 136 ms locally even with the path work restored) — is now pinned **structurally** by the new `test_the_walk_does_not_resolve_a_path_per_note`, which counts `Path.resolve` calls during a warm refresh. **Evidence:** 20 tests in the file (was 19); **3 mutation checks, all red**, each restored from a byte copy — the per-note stat elision removed (caught by the AC test and six others), the diff bypassed for a straight full rebuild (the AC test reads **101.63%** of cold), and the walk re-resolving each path (caught by the new structural test **alone**, which is exactly why it exists). No product code changed.

- **EM-602 slice 2 — `note_id` is the full vault-relative path, and an existing `index.db` migrates to `user_version` 2.** `Note.note_id` used to drop the path's top directory (`path.relative_to(path.parent.parent)`), so `A/Sub/Note` and `B/Sub/Note` both read `Sub/Note` — **one primary key for two notes** — and the id is stored in three places (`notes_meta.note_id`, `notes_fts.note_id`, and the `wikilink` endpoints of `graph_edges`). The rule is now `note_id_for_path()`: the vault-relative path, POSIX separators, extension stripped — unique by construction, and unchanged for shallow notes (`Knowledge/Note` still reads `Knowledge/Note`, which is why the existing index and graph tests needed no repoints). **The `index.db` migration** runs once per database, driven by `PRAGMA user_version`: the old id is not invertible (it dropped a component) but each row's own `path` is, so ids are recomputed from the path; the two primary keys are renamed in two passes through a transient NUL-prefixed id, so a rename can never collide with itself; the `kind='wikilink'` endpoints of `graph_edges` are remapped through the same map; `notes_fts` moves with `notes_meta`, so a joined search still finds the note; and `kind='triple:*'` edges are left alone — a different id space, so the migration is scoped to wikilink edges rather than rewriting a foreign one. Idempotent: the index is a cache of the vault, and a `rebuild()` right after the migration changes nothing. **A Windows fix is recorded:** the first push's `windows-import` job failed because the *test fixture* computed legacy ids with the platform separator (on Windows they are backslash-shaped) while asserting the POSIX form — the product was right, and the fixture now builds its expectation from the same helper while a new test forges the Windows shape explicitly and asserts it migrates to POSIX. **Evidence:** **10 new tests** (`tests/unit/test_em602_note_ids.py` — the rule including a `.v2.md` name and a Windows-shaped path; the old collision reproduced; nested notes getting distinct rows and edges; a fresh store at version 2; a legacy index — old ids, `user_version` 0, an entity triple edge *and* a triple edge whose text equals a legacy note id — migrating with FTS searchable afterwards and both triple edges untouched; idempotence; rebuild-agrees-with-migration; and the forged Windows shape). **6 mutation checks, all red**, each restored from a byte copy: the rule reverted to the legacy one; the migration disabled; `notes_fts` not renamed; the wikilink endpoints not renamed; every edge kind renamed (the scope filter dies); and `user_version` not stamped. Suite **2818 / 16 / 2** bare on 3.11 (was 2808 / 16 / 2), **2819 / 15 / 2** with the digest list, **2896 / 2 / 2** CI-equivalent 3.12; CI's 3.12 count **2894 / 4 / 2** (read green from the job log at `c29addc`, `windows-import` included); `ruff==0.16.2` clean; the 3.10 leg green; the privacy word-hash scan found no collision. **EM-602 is now complete — both slices.**

- **EM-602 slice 1 — the frontmatter writer and reader hardened: the vault can no longer corrupt itself on a hand edit.** Two silent-corruption defects in `em_internal/vault.py`, fixed by moving the logic into a new shared module — **`em/vault/frontmatter.py`** — which the v2 module now shims, so one implementation serves the vault and the projector. (1) The writer built its lines with f-strings (`title: "{title}"`), so a title containing `"`, a backslash or a newline produced a block that read back differently, or would not parse at all, while the note still looked saved. (2) The reader split the whole file on `---` (`text.split("---", 2)`), so a horizontal rule or a code fence in the body truncated the note. **The writer** now quotes only what YAML requires (plain text stays plain: `title: Test Title`), escapes `"`/`\`/control characters, and falls back to a **block scalar** (`|-`/`|`) for multi-line values; date-like and number-like strings are quoted so YAML never re-reads `2026-01-02` as a date or `1234` as an int. **The reader** accepts a delimiter only on the file's first line and on a line of its own, prefers **PyYAML** when importable and otherwise uses the subset parser in the same module — the two are round-tripped against each other, and that check found a real bug (block-scalar chomping) which is now pinned. **Dates stay strings:** a hand-written unquoted `updated: 2026-01-02` is normalised back to `"2026-01-02"`, because callers do `date.fromisoformat(note.updated)` and YAML's own typing would otherwise turn a hand edit into a `TypeError`. **Wikilinks** are parsed as a grammar (`[[Target]]`, `|alias`, `#heading`, `^block`, `![[embed]]`), and `Vault.extract_wikilinks` strips alias/heading/block, so `[[Note|alias]]` resolves against the note titled `Note` instead of the literal `Note|alias`. **Deliberate repoints, each the changed surface's own claim:** `tests/test_vault.py`'s `title: "Test Title"` byte-pin and `tests/test_graph_incremental.py`'s three `text.replace('title: "…"')` edits now use the unquoted plain form the writer emits. **Evidence:** 26 new tests (`tests/unit/test_em602_frontmatter.py` — the delimiter rule including an indented/`----`/`--- ` marker and a `---` in the body; the quoting/escaping/block-scalar writer; a 300-case random title/tag round-trip; PyYAML agreement and the subset fallback; the date-stays-a-string pin; the five wikilink forms; the shim); **7 mutation checks, all red**, each restored from a byte copy — the delimiter split, quote escaping, the block scalars, the quoting predicate, the date normalisation, `extract_wikilinks`, and the shim's writer — plus a **green control** (PyYAML disabled, subset parser only) proving the fallback carries the behaviour. Suite **2808 / 16 / 2** bare on 3.11 (was 2782 / 16 / 2), **2809 / 15 / 2** with the digest list, **2886 / 2 / 2** CI-equivalent 3.12; CI's 3.12 count **2884 / 4 / 2** (read green from the job log at `607d54f`; +26 over `c53b182`'s 2858/4/2 read); `ruff==0.16.2` clean; the 3.10 leg green; the privacy word-hash scan found no collision. **Slice 2 — `note_id` as the full vault-relative path and the `index.db` `user_version` 2 migration — landed the same day (see the entry above); EM-602 is complete.**

- **Silent data loss on `remember`/`update` fixed — oversize content is now refused, never truncated.** `em/util/jsonschema_lite.validate_arguments` implemented §4.4's `maxLength` clause as *clamp-with-a-warning*, and the clamped value was then **persisted**: a live 4,314-character summary (2026-10-17) was cut to its first 4,000 characters and stored `active/created`, making a clipped fact permanently indistinguishable from a complete one at recall time. The validator gains a **keyword-only `fatal_length_keys`** mode in which an over-long value **raises** `ArgumentError` (the same fatal shape types and enums already use) instead of clamping; the default is the empty set, so every existing caller is byte-identical, and the tool layer names `remember`/`update` `content` in `FATAL_LENGTH_KEYS`, so the refusal rides the existing `ArgumentError → {"ok": false, "reason_code": "invalid_arguments"}` branch — which runs **before any store write**, leaving no memory row and no audit entry. Every other string cap (`query`, `reason`, …) still warns and clamps. **Evidence:** **16 new tests** — `tests/unit/test_em_content_ceiling.py` (content at the ceiling stores byte-for-byte; over it is refused with the field, the length and the limit named, and the `memories`/`audit_log` row counts are unchanged; `update` in both modes; the one-constant and one-map pins; the pinned refusal wording; the guidance pinned on both descriptions; the packer's skip-never-cut behaviour at the raised cap; and the 20k containment measurement) plus **4** in `test_em404_jsonschema_lite.py` (the fatal mode raises; the default still clamps; the fatal mode is keyed by field name). **8 mutation checks, all red**, each restored from a byte copy: `FATAL_LENGTH_KEYS` emptied; `handle_tool_call` dropping the map; the schemas re-hardcoding 4,000; the fatal branch changed to clamp; the refusal's `reason_code` changed; the guidance sentence dropped from `remember`; the fatal mode applied to *every* key (the default-clamp contract dies); and `validate_arguments` never threading the set down. Suite **2782 / 16 / 2** bare on 3.11 (was 2766 / 16 / 2), **2783 / 15 / 2** with the digest list, **2860 / 2 / 2** CI-equivalent 3.12; `ruff==0.16.2` clean; the 3.10 leg green; the privacy word-hash scan found no collision.

- **EM-608 — the 50k warm-p95 gate now measures a statistic, not one window (the flake fixed at its source).** The card's p95 AC (`tests/harness/test_prefetch_service_ac.py`) and its wired twin (`tests/harness/test_em403_slice3_wired_ac.py`) each took **one** p95 over 25 probes, so the verdict moved with the runner, not the code: on unchanged SHAs `6e86c98` read p95 **153.35 ms** (on the 3.10 leg; all 25 samples 117–158 ms, *uniformly* inflated — the opposite tell from a genuine regression, which shows a stall tail) and `bdc1e20` read **151.09 ms** (3.13 leg, run 39112375641 / job 117631038052), each green on a rerun of the same SHA. Re-running a red job until it lands on a quiet runner is a workaround, not a gate, so the fix is EM-407's, generalised and shared: the new **`tests/harness/perf_stats.py`** carries `window_p95` (the gate's own nearest-rank definition, unchanged, so no recorded reading shifts), `timed_windows(run, windows=5, probes=20)` and `best_window_p95`, and both ACs now time **five 20-probe windows** and require the **best** window's p95 to hold **150.0 ms — the budget is untouched**. A transient contention cannot inflate all five windows; a genuine regression inflates every one and still fails. Each AC emits every window's p95 as a warning on green runs too, so drift under the assertion is visible over time, and `timed_windows` hands each probe its **own index**, so all 100 probe queries stay distinct and nothing hits the cache to depress the number. **Evidence:** 8 new tests (`tests/harness/test_em608_perf_stats.py`) — the nearest-rank definition; the window shape and the distinct indices; a fast run passing; a uniformly slow run failing *every* window (the property a best-of-N must not trade away); the recorded flake's exact shape (one contended window among four clean) absorbed; a near-budget slowdown pinned as the stated rule; and a structural pin that **both** ACs still call `timed_windows`/`best_window_p95`, keep `150.0`, and are not back to one 25-probe window. **6 mutation checks, all red**, each restored from a byte copy: `best_window_p95`'s `min`→`max` (flake absorption and the near-budget rule go red); the statistic forced to pass (`0.0`; the regression test goes red); `timed_windows` flattened to one window (the shape test goes red); `best_window_p95` collapsing to the overall p95 over concatenated samples (absorption goes red); and each AC reverted to its pre-EM-608 single-window form (the structural pin goes red). On this box one bare run's first two windows read 42.95 / 51.01 ms — a real contended window, absorbed, which is the fix working. Suite **2766 / 16 / 2** bare on 3.11, **2767 / 15 / 2** with the digest list, **2844 / 2 / 2** CI-equivalent 3.12 (was 2758 / 2759 / 2836); `ruff==0.16.2` clean; no product code changed.

- **F-004 resolved — full per-user isolation on the v3 line; the strict xfail flips to a passing guard.** Two Hermes users sharing one profile's v3 store (`user_id`s alice and bob, one pool) can no longer read each other's memories: each tool write is attributed to its gateway principal (`scope_user`, `visibility='user'`), the read rule stays §3.5's own-or-profile-wide (`scope_sql` and `MemoryStore._in_scope`), and every keyed surface re-checks the target row under the caller's own scope — `entropicmem_update` (both `edit` and `supersede`), `forget` by id and through the confirm-token flow, the four `review` actions, and `search` by ids all answer `not_found`/missing on another user's row, and episodes' reads are exact-bucket. **No product code changed** — the isolation was already correct on the v3 line; the chunk certifies it with evidence and pins it.

  - **Evidence:** 14 tests. `tests/regressions/test_findings_v27.py::test_f004_cross_user_isolation` flips from strict xfail to a passing guard (the store is *created* as v3 first — `em.facade.select` maps a not-yet-existing file to the v2 engine, whose pool semantics are the documented pre-cutover reality, so the guard also asserts `_v3_store()` on both providers; xfail count 3 → 2). `tests/harness/test_f004_shared_profile.py` (new, beside the group-chat harness, which keeps one host with per-turn authors) owns the two-provider surface: per-user write attribution, both-directions prefetch and search isolation, the five foreign-row refusals, both-directions episode isolation, rows written profile-wide (bot author) staying shared across both users — the migration ruling itself, plan §9 item 9 (b), keep profile-wide — and the **recorded under-read**: episodes' exact-bucket rule hides a profile-wide episode from a user-mode caller, pinned as-is rather than changed silently (the semantic call rides the cutover card; nothing over-reads in either direction). **5 mutation checks, all red**, each restored from a byte copy: `scope_sql`'s user-bucket clause (via the `test_em_retrieval_scope_sql` SQL↔rule cross-check, ×3); the `MemoryStore.get` scope refusal (the keyed pins, ×4); the ids path's re-check; the episodes' `scope_user` clause (×2); and the `_in_scope` row rule itself — including as a two-layer combination on top of the `scope_sql` clause — the one removal that makes the leak reach the flipped guard's block assertions. Two further single-layer probes are recorded as **absorbed by design**: removing only `scope_sql`'s clause or only `get`'s refusal still leaves the block path leak-free, because the two layers compensate each other (the no-leak-reaches-output invariant is itself what the fifth check pins). Suite **2693 / 16 / 2** bare, **2694 / 15 / 2** with the digest list, **2771 / 2 / 2** CI-equivalent 3.12.
  - **The pre-cutover boundary is unchanged on v2:** the live v2 store keeps pool semantics until the owner cutover, which is exactly what makes the cutover the owner's act rather than a side effect of opening a store.

- **Cold-start pointers made portable across the owner's boxes.** The `Dev-docs` folder retired on the owner's Linux box but is still present on the OpenCode box, so the master-plan pointer (`NEXT_CHUNK.md`) and the privacy-digests pre-flight command (`REMAINING_PLAN.md` §11) now name **both** known locations, and the command resolves whichever exists. The stale local digest count in that block is corrected to **2680**. Docs only; no product change; no count moves.

- **Drafted, docs only — the remember content ceiling card (`docs/plan/CONTENT_CEILING_CARD_DRAFT.md`).** Captures the two defects a live `entropicmem_remember` incident exposed on 2026-10-17: the 4,000-character cap under-runs the facts the storage-fidelity rule says it should hold, and `jsonschema_lite`'s `maxLength` implementation destroys the tail instead of refusing (the truncated fact still registered `active/created`). The draft proposes one shared ceiling constant (recommended 8,000), a fatal-oversize mode for `content` that answers `invalid_arguments` before any store write, and a performance-containment argument (serving/recall are budget- and top-k-bound; the embedding window and all CI perf gates stay untouched). Pointer added to `STOP_HERE.md`'s owner-gated list; the count triads move +1 for the card's `tests/test_docs_links.py` case (derivation stated, not a fresh read).

- **Cold-start pointers repointed after the Dev-docs folder retired.** `NEXT_CHUNK.md`'s master-plan pointer named `~/Documents/EntropicMem Dev docs/EntropicMem_v3_Master_Plan.md`, a path that no longer exists — the plan now lives outside the repo at `~/.hermes/attachments/EntropicMem_v3_Master_Plan.md` (the repo stays self-sufficient for the transcribed parts in plan §6.2). The pre-flight in `REMAINING_PLAN.md` §11 hard-coded the Opencode box's clone path — it now names both known clones — and its privacy-digests command pointed at the retired digests path; it now uses `~/.hermes/attachments/privacy-digests.txt` with the fail-closed note for boxes without the file. Docs only; no product change; no count moves.

- **Docs accuracy: the EM-501/EM-502/EM-503 landing records' CI-run claims corrected.** The check-runs API shows **no check-runs on the code SHAs** of the EM-501, EM-502 and EM-503 landings (`54d0a81`, `2a6a892`, `83a785e`, `2576dcc`, `2468e4e`, `4a8a4c2`, `1f88e8f`, `ca463a0`, `597aac8`, `01ed837`, `d331c5e`) or the brand commit (`bf911c8`): on those pushes CI ran only on the **docs tip that carried each branch** (`d23ea63`, `74186aa`, `8a16624`, `d10daf9`, `3cf79e4`, `0fd6af6`, `7f5dcee` — all 11 jobs success at ff-merge). Every landing from chunk 35 through EM-412 verifies exactly as recorded — each cited SHA carries its own green runs. The records in `MASTER_TODO` (the pre-flight provenance, the gates table), `REMAINING_PLAN` (§2's Development line, the Last-landed chain, the pre-flight comment) and `NEXT_CHUNK` (the Part A headers for EM-501, EM-502 and EM-503, and the stale fourteenth-pass lead, which still named `main` at `2a6a892`) now name the SHA that actually carried the runs. The drift guard forced §11's Development line to the newest cited docs tip (`7f5dcee`). Docs only; no product change; no count moves.

- **Supersession end-to-end through the provider — the 2026-10-15 coverage gap closes.** No test drove a real supersession through the provider and read the served block: `MemoryStore.predecessors`, `diversity.load_predecessors`, `render_superseded_note` and the packer's `was` field were each asserted in isolation, but the chain EM-502/EM-503 will feed went unexercised. `tests/unit/test_em_supersession_e2e.py` now drives it: on a v3 store with `ENTROPICMEM_V3_RETRIEVAL=1` a fact is superseded through the `entropicmem_update` tool (`mode=supersede`), and the wired `prefetch()` serves the successor with §3.6's `(fact · updated <date>; was: <old>)` note quoting the superseded row — the old value appearing **only** in that note, never as a live bullet — with a control proving the note is the supersession's, not a rendering artefact. **3 mutation checks, all red, each restored from a byte copy:** the packer's `was` emptied; the service dropping `predecessors=retrieval.predecessors`; the pipeline dropping `predecessors=noted`. Suite **2631 / 16 / 3** bare, **2632 / 15 / 3** with the digest list, **2709 / 2 / 3** CI-equivalent 3.12; CI's 3.12 count **2707 / 4 / 3**.

- **§11 drift guard, and a count drift the caveat audit missed.** Two `test_master_todo.py` checks now cover `REMAINING_PLAN.md` §11 beyond the reconciled and pre-flight SHAs: every SHA in the state table's `main` rows must be a real ancestor of HEAD, and the **Development line** must not lag any landing the **Last landed chunk** row records. The guard was **red on the row it was written for** — §11 still said `874b3d4` (EM-410) after EM-412's code landed as `637a269` — and green once the row moved (`637a269`, doc-only). **A count drift the audit missed is also fixed:** the CI-equivalent 3.12 figure read 2704 / 2 / 3, but adding `docs/plan/PRUNE_CARD_DRAFT.md` (`690e9d9`) had already grown the `test_docs_links` parametrization by one case, so the live figure was **2705 / 2 / 3**; the guard's +2 now makes it **2707 / 2 / 3**. Reconciled across the three pages and the §11 history chain: bare **2629 / 16 / 3**, digest list **2630 / 15 / 3**, CI-equivalent **2707 / 2 / 3**, CI 3.12 **2705 / 4 / 3** (read at `ae525b6`). Also recorded, docs only, no code — F-004 full per-user isolation as a **pre-cutover blocker** (`MASTER_TODO` Open blockers, plan §9 item 9), the **adapter-defaults decision** spelled out for the owner (plan §9 item 4), an **owner run checklist for P0c** (`NEXT_CHUNK.md` Step 1), a **prune fold-vs-own-card recommendation** (`PRUNE_CARD_DRAFT.md`), and **S5 scope + the recorded-gap cards** (plan §6.4). `.gitignore` now covers the local `.venv311/`.

- **EM-407 follow-ups: the 50k search guard reads the best of five windows, `save_config` validates before it writes, and the spec/calibration default split is pinned as deliberate.** Three changes from the owner's verification queue. (1) The numpy AC's single 20-probe window made its p95 the second-worst sample, so one runner stall failed it wholesale — the 2026-10-15 CI flap (3.13 leg) read 13 samples at 9–13 ms then a stall tail to 113 ms (p95 31.61 ms) while every other leg passed; the guard now requires the best of five 20-probe windows to hold p95 < 25 ms — a genuine regression inflates every window and still fails, a transient stall no longer does — and every window's p95 is emitted as a warning on each run, green included, so drift that stays under the assertion is visible over time. (2) `save_config` validates every key against the typed rules — legacy flat spellings accepted the way the loader accepts them, unknown keys refused rather than filed under a key nothing reads — **before anything touches disk**; it writes through a per-call private temp file (`tempfile.mkstemp`, so racing savers cannot share a path and whole-file last-write-wins holds), removes the temp on any failure, preserves the destination's mode, and documents the Windows locked-destination contract (`PermissionError`; the original survives). (3) The eval adapter's no-argument default and the runtime's config defaults are **deliberately different owners**: the adapter walks into the pipeline's spec defaults (the frozen suites' and baselines' numbers), the provider ships EM-306's calibration, and `test_the_default_adapter_keeps_the_spec_and_the_runtime_ships_the_calibration` pins both sides plus the divergence (rrf 0.60→0.50, importance 0.15→0.25; the gate values are identical). Evidence: 8 new tests; suite **2549 / 16 / 3** bare and **2550 / 15 / 3** with the digest list **in place** on 3.11, **2627 / 2 / 3** on CI-equivalent 3.12 with the digest file; `ruff==0.16.2` clean; the guard re-run 5× green and replayed against the recorded failing window (old formula 31.61 ms red; best-of-five green; an all-window regression still red).

- **`save_config` no longer resolves a blank `hermes_home` to the process working directory.** `Path("")` is `Path(".")`, so `save_config(values, "")` merged `plugins.entropicmem` into whatever `config.yaml` happened to sit in the CWD and rewrote that whole file — comments and layout included. It now refuses a blank home and raises, which is what §4.1's fail-closed `save_config` does with any failure. The host always passes `str(get_hermes_home())`, so this is defence in depth rather than a live path: guessing where to write a user's config is worse than not writing it. Found by driving EM-401's hook surface — the test that called it with `""` had been dropping a `config.yaml` into the repository. Two tests: a blank (and whitespace, and `None`) home raises and writes nothing, and a real home still merges without touching a sibling plugin's section. **Mutation-checked three ways** (guard removed; only `None` caught; guard moved after the path is computed). Landed alongside two hygiene corrections from the same run: `.gitignore` now covers `uv*.lock` (`git add -A` swept a stray `uv 2.lock` into a commit once), and `AGENTS.md`'s mutation rule records that a mutation must be restored from a **copy**, never `git checkout -- <file>` — on uncommitted work it discards the work, and on a new untracked file it fails and leaves the mutation applied.

- **The abstention gate read only the `pinned` column, so a `kind='constraint'` row the pinned generator had just surfaced was filtered out.** §3.6's generator table attaches "bypasses gate (still budgeted)" to the pinned generator, whose input is `pinned=1` **or** `kind='constraint'`; `gate.load_rows` built `RowInfo.pinned` from the column alone, so a constraint with no lexical overlap was dropped by the hard filter instead of bypassing it — the generator and the gate disagreeing about the same input. Found by P0b's end-to-end run (the same shape of defect as P0a's raw-id comparison), fixed in the loader (`pinned=bool(row["pinned"]) or row.kind == 'constraint'`) and pinned at both layers: the loader unit test, with an ordinary row as the control so "pin everything" fails too, and an `apply_gate` test where a zero-coverage constraint survives. Mutation-checked both ways. No shipped eval dataset uses the kind, so no scored number moves.

- **A wall-clock assertion in EM-302's deadline AC test was replaced with two deterministic ones.** `test_every_generator_returns_within_5ms_of_an_expired_deadline` measured `elapsed <= 5 ms`, and it flaked on the Windows runner at **16 ms for a generator that does no work at all past its check** — a wall-clock bound on a shared runner measures the runner, not the generator. The AC is now asserted structurally (an expired generator returns nothing **and issues no SQL**, which a stopwatch cannot state but a recording connection can) **plus** a stronger exact check: with a clock that expires after the first tick, exactly **one** page may be fetched, so a deadline check placed after the query instead of before it now fails. **No budget was widened and no coverage was lost** — neither replacement relaxes an assertion, and the second asserts strictly more than the stopwatch did.

- **v3 redaction was dropping the configured PII locale packs.** `pii.redact_pii` has always taken `locales`, and v2 passed its `pii_locales` through on every write; the v3 store called `redact_pii(content)` with none, so a store configured for an opt-in EM-115 pack (only `za` today — SA phone format, 13-digit ID) silently lost that detection on a v3 store. `MemoryDraft` now carries `pii_locales`, `MemoryStore._redact` passes them, and `V3Engine` takes and forwards them (the selector routes them to **both** engines, because they are the caller's packs, not a v2 detail). **The tier gate is not a bug and is unchanged:** v3 redacts every detected type but only for `sensitive`/`secret` rows (pinned by `test_public_content_is_not_redacted`), whereas v2 destroyed only `api_key`/`password` — which the write policy blocks outright — on every write. That difference is now a recorded deviation rather than an undocumented one. Evidence: 3 new tests, including a spy that proves the packs reach `redact_pii` end to end.

- **EM-212 (rest of the card): the engine's six shared-name modules live under a package, so nothing shadows the host process.** The plugin's `scripts/` goes on `sys.path`, so `from vault import …` resolved through the process-wide `sys.modules` — and the Hermes host runs many plugins in one interpreter. Another plugin's `vault` (or `index`, `security`, `policy`, `embeddings`, `retrieval`) was returned instead of ours, or ours shadowed theirs. The first step (2026-09-27) made `_backend` load its own `vault.py` by path; **the modules themselves have now moved under `scripts/em_internal/`**, so the import system registers `em_internal.vault` and friends and never the bare names. Every importer was updated — the provider, the CLI, `graph_server`, `em/`'s two lazy `policy` imports, and the tests — and `_backend`'s path-loading shim is gone, because a qualified import cannot collide and keeps the module identity in step with `import`.
  - `_backend._own_module` is deleted. `resolve_paths` now does a normal `from em_internal.vault import resolve_vault_path` after putting `scripts/` on `sys.path`, with the plugin's own `scripts/` as the fallback. The decoy-module tests still pass and still leave a foreign `sys.modules["vault"]` untouched — trivially so now, since we never import that name at all.
  - **The strict xfail flipped to a passing test**, not deleted: `test_em212_plan_ac_no_unprefixed_engine_modules_in_process` now loads `memory_engine` in a clean interpreter and asserts none of the six appears in `sys.modules` (they appear as `em_internal.*`). It was mutation-checked by recreating a top-level `vault.py` shim and importing it, which turns it red.
  - **Three silent coverage losses were repaired on the way**, each of which the move would have hidden: `test_f005` globbed `scripts/*.py` non-recursively and stopped seeing the moved modules (now `rglob`, with a mutation that adds a runtime `HERMES_HOME` read to a moved module proving it fires); `test_plugin_imports` derived its module list from the same non-recursive glob and would have stopped checking the six for the 2026-08-14 bug class; and the packaging guard in `test_em_version` only required `em.*`, so `em_internal` could have shipped missing from the wheel — it now derives **every** package under `scripts/` from disk and fails on the first omission.
  - **What is not done, and why:** the rest of `scripts/` (14 modules — `textutil`, `temporal`, `pii`, `stopwords`, `session_digest`, `injection_screen`, `triple_extract`, `graph_*`, `memory_engine`, `parity_audit`, `entropicmem`) still carries unprefixed names. The card's AC names only the six, and those six are what `hermes plugins validate` warned about, so this is the scoped fix; moving the remainder is a deliberate follow-up, not a silent one.
  - Evidence: 52 files touched (6 renames); **1671 passed / 3 skipped / 3 xfailed on Python 3.10 and 3.12** (the xfail count dropping 4→3 is the flip); `ruff==0.16.2` clean; eval gate with no gated metric regressed; `perf-smoke` warm p95 3.973 ms against the 20 ms budget; `tests/unit` green standalone for `windows-import`; provider, CLI and `graph_server` all still import. **The second half of the AC is confirmed by CI, not assumed.** The `plugin-validate` job runs `hermes plugins validate --install-deps plugins/entropicmem`; it passed on the merged SHA with `✓ loadable`, `✓ capability probe`, `✓ declared tools`/`hooks`, `✓ built-in tool collisions — no collisions` and `✓ security scan — safe`, and **no module-shadow warning**. The only two warnings are the known, expected `provides_tools`/`provides_hooks` "declared but not registered" pair (the validator cannot see tools a MemoryProvider exposes via `get_tool_schemas()`); they pre-date this change and are kept on purpose.

- **The v3 facade could not read its own timestamps on Python 3.10, so `consolidate` archived nothing and decay never applied.** `em.clock.to_iso` writes `2026-10-06T16:23:31.807Z`, and Python 3.10's `datetime.fromisoformat` rejects the `Z` suffix (3.11+ accepts it). The facade reused v2's `_parse_ts` for two things — `_decay_factor` (`max(updated_at, last_accessed)`) and `_consolidation_candidate` (age from the same stamps) — so on 3.10 every v3 timestamp parsed to `None`. The effect was silent and wrong: `consolidate` found no candidates ever (a row whose stamps cannot be parsed has no age), and `recall_with_relevance` returned `decay_score == 1.0` for everything. Both now use a facade-local `_parse_ts` built on `em.clock.parse_iso`, which normalises the suffix on purpose (its docstring says so) and is the v3 layer's own parser; v2's parser is never borrowed again. **Found by CI, not by the local run**, because the failure is 3.10-only and the local interpreter was 3.12. Evidence: reproduced for real on a Python 3.10.19 venv (the five consolidate tests red exactly as CI reported), fixed, then green on both 3.10 (1626 passed) and 3.12; two regression tests simulate v2's 3.10 behaviour by patching `memory_engine._parse_ts` to return `None`, so any interpreter catches it — mutation-checked by pointing the facade back at the v2 parser, which turns both red on 3.12.

- **`tests/test_master_todo.py` could not run under CI's shallow checkout, so it failed on every Python leg.** The guard resolves the commit `MASTER_TODO.md` was reconciled against and asserts it is an ancestor of `HEAD`, but the `test` job used `actions/checkout@v7` with its default `fetch-depth: 1`, leaving only the tip: `git cat-file` could not find the recorded SHA, and the file was declared unfalsifiable. The `test` job now fetches full history (`fetch-depth: 0`), the same setting `identity-guard` already used for the same reason, so the ancestry check is real rather than assumed. Verified by reproducing the failure in a `--depth 1` clone and the pass in a full clone. The guard itself was not weakened.

- **The v3 concurrency AC test could compute its p95 from an empty sample list.** `tests/unit/test_em_store_concurrency.py` — EM-202's proof that "4 writer processes × 500 writes + 1 reader" need no advisory lock — read `latencies[int(len(latencies) * 0.95) - 1]`, and that index is `-1` when the reader recorded nothing: `IndexError: list index out of range`. The 3.13 CI leg hit it once, when the four writers finished before the reader process (spawn + imports) had taken its first read. The reader now keeps sampling until the stop flag is set **and** it holds at least 20 samples (`MIN_SAMPLES`, with a 60 s deadline); the parent asserts that floor, so an empty list can never reach the percentile; and the index is nearest-rank via `max(0, math.ceil(0.95 * n) - 1)`. The 50 ms budget and the acceptance criterion are unchanged — this makes the measurement real instead of accidental. Mutation-checked: a reader that takes no samples now fails the floor assertion.
- **EM-212: `_backend` no longer resolves engine modules through the shared module namespace.** `resolve_paths` did `from vault import resolve_vault_path` after putting `scripts/` on `sys.path`. The Hermes host runs many plugins in one process, so a `vault` module another plugin had already imported was returned instead. EntropicMem then crashed (`ImportError`) or silently used the other plugin's vault path. It now loads its own `scripts/vault.py` by file path under a private module name (`_entropicmem_scripts_vault`) and leaves the other plugin's module alone. The acceptance test `test_f010_no_bare_module_imports_in_backend` could never pass as written: it located the file through `find_spec("entropicmem._backend")`, and in the test process that name is the CLI script, so the strict xfail was "passing" on a lookup error. It now reads `_backend.py` directly and also catches plain `import vault`. `tests/test_backend_namespace.py` reproduces both failure modes with a decoy module. Mutation-checked: restoring the bare import fails all three. This is the first step of EM-212: the plan's full card (engine modules under a package namespace, so no unprefixed module is registered in the host process) is still open. It is pinned by the new strict xfail `test_em212_plan_ac_no_unprefixed_engine_modules_in_process`.
- **CI `perf-smoke` now measures a real p95, not the single slowest sample.** The job ran `evals.perf --probes 5`, and `percentile()` is nearest-rank, so with 5 samples the "p95" was `max()` — one stray slow probe on an otherwise-unchanged prefetch path could fail the gate (it flapped once, then passed on retry). It now runs `--probes 20`, so p95 is the 19th of 20 samples and a single outlier cannot fail the job. The 20 ms budget is unchanged. Verified with five local before/after runs of `evals.perf --sizes 1000`.
- **Docs: every network touchpoint is listed in one place** (README, "Network Access"): opt-in embeddings (Hugging Face download), user-initiated `ingest <url>`, the exported graph HTML (D3, marked and Google Fonts from public CDNs when opened), the loopback-only graph server, and the backup upload to your rclone remote. It also notes where the shared store and `graph export` write by default. `graph_export.py`'s docstrings no longer claim a "vendored/offline fallback" for D3 and marked; none exists. Matches the Hermes catalog disclosure as amended in the 2.8.0 review.
- **The CLI honours `HERMES_HOME` for the shared publish store.** The CLI resolved its own database paths from `HERMES_HOME`, but built every `MemoryEngine` (19 sites) without `hermes_home`, and `shared-init` called `shared_path()` with none. The shared store therefore defaulted to `~/.hermes/entropicmem-shared/` under a custom `HERMES_HOME`, while the provider, which passes its home, used `$HERMES_HOME/entropicmem-shared/`: CLI publishes and the running agent used different logs. Every CLI engine now comes from one `_engine()` helper that passes the resolved home, `MemoryEngine.shared_init()` accepts `hermes_home`, and an AST test fails on any bare `MemoryEngine(...)` in the CLI. The tests run the real CLI with `HOME` and `HERMES_HOME` both inside a temp directory. Reported in the Hermes catalog review of 2.8.0.
- **EM-208 follow-up: `entropicmem audit verify` is read-only.** It opened the database through `Store`, i.e. read-write, which chmodded the parent directory to 0700 and the file to 0600 and switched the journal to WAL, even on a v2 database it then refused to check. It now opens `mode=ro` and closes the connection explicitly.
- **EM-208 follow-up: `MemoryStore.purge` forgets entity sightings and entity links.** A sighting row holds the phrase in its `meta` key, so a hard-deleted memory's names outlived it; purge now removes the memory from every sighting list and deletes emptied rows, a promoted phrase's sighting row is deleted, and lists are capped at 2 ids. Purge also deletes `memory_entities` rows first (the FK has no `ON DELETE CASCADE`, so purging a linked memory used to raise `IntegrityError`).
- **EM-208 follow-up: sentence-opening words are no longer proposed as entities.** `candidate_phrases` kept them despite its docstring, so two memories starting "Deployed …" created an entity "Deployed". A single-token opener is dropped and a multi-token opener loses its first token; `known_phrases` (no stripping) is used to match already-registered entities so they still link at a sentence start.
- **`pyproject.toml` packages `em.formation`.** EM-208 added the subpackage without listing it, the same silent-omission bug EM-202 and EM-203 hit: the wheel would have shipped without the entity linker. A test now fails if any `em` subpackage is missing from the list.
- **EM-204: a fresh install was misdetected as a v2 database.** `0001` always leaves the *empty* v2 shape behind, so "a `facts` table exists" proves nothing about provenance. `0002` originally renamed those empty shells to `v2_*` and recorded `migrated_from='v2'` for a database that had never held a v2 row, leaving new installs littered with dead tables. The migration now decides from whether any source table holds a *single row*: a fresh install drops the empty v2 shells and produces a clean v3 schema with `migrated_from='none'`.
- **EM-204: `episodes`, `embeddings` and `audit_log` name collisions could have silently kept the v2 shape.** v2 and v3 both use those three names and the v3 DDL is `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS`, so if the v2 tables were renamed *after* the v3 DDL the `IF NOT EXISTS` would have been satisfied by the old v2 tables and v3 would never have been created — no error, just the wrong schema. Renaming now happens before any v3 DDL, and tests assert v3-specific columns (`window_seq`, `owner_type`, `prev_hash`) exist rather than merely that a table does.
- **EM-204: window episodes were classified as sessions.** Real ids are `ep_sess_{sid}_w{n}`, so testing the `ep_sess_` prefix first matched *every* window episode and labelled it a session — silently, since the column is just a string. The `_w\d+$` suffix is now tested before the prefix. Note the shipped v2 data contains no window episodes at all (0 of 1570 facts), so this rule is forward-looking and nothing in the real store would have caught it later.
- **EM-204: the FTS sync check was false assurance.** `INSERT INTO <fts>(<fts>) VALUES('integrity-check')` compares the index against the *content table*, so it passes even when the index is completely empty; `SELECT count(*)` on an external-content FTS table reads through to that same content table and therefore always agrees with it. Both reported a healthy index while holding zero documents. The parity check now reads the `<fts>_docsize` shadow table, which holds one row per actually-indexed document, and a test pins the false-assurance behaviour directly so the weaker probe cannot come back.
- **EM-206-prep: the privacy guard now covers the whole repo, and CI checks commit identity.** On 2026-09-26 an employer name reached `tests/fixtures/db/build_rich_v2.py` and `tests/unit/test_em_migration_v3_core.py` in published history, and five public merge commits carried a personal work email. Both are now guarded:
  - `tests/evals/test_no_personal_data.py` scans **every tracked text file** via `git ls-files` instead of a hand-picked glob list that omitted `tests/unit/` and `tests/fixtures/` (the exact blind spot the leak went through). Binaries are skipped, the guard excludes itself, and a new test asserts those directories stay covered so the list cannot silently shrink again. Two tokens were **removed** from the banned set because a repo-wide scan cannot ban them: they hashed to `fp` and `discovery`, ordinary words that appear in honest product code (`injection_screen.py`, `memory_engine.py`, `pyproject.toml`, `CHANGELOG.md`). Every real identifier stays covered, and a test pins both facts.
  - `scripts/check_commit_identity.sh` plus a new `identity-guard` CI job fail when any commit in range carries an author or committer email that is not `*@users.noreply.github.com`, `noreply@github.com` or `noreply@anthropic.com`. The **push-to-main** trigger is the alarm that matters: a GitHub web merge only creates its merge commit after CI has run on the PR, so the PR check cannot see it. The job checks both author *and* committer, since it is the committer field that GitHub sets from the merging account's email. Verified by mutation: a non-noreply author fails, a noreply author with a non-noreply committer fails (the real web-merge case), a clean range passes, and an empty range exits 0 instead of crashing.
- **EM-204: three EM-203 tests asserted a stale "migrated to latest == v2.7 shape" claim.** They were correct when `0001` *was* the latest migration and went false the moment `0002` landed. Rather than delete the coverage they were split by which migration's contract they actually pin: the v2.7-shape test now pins the registry to `0001` alone, a new parametrized test asserts the legacy ids and rows are still readable at *latest* (guarding `0002` against destroying what it must preserve for one release), and the empty-DB test asserts a clean v3 schema with no `v2_*` leftovers.
- **Graph server: malformed Host headers now return 400 instead of 500** (S1 follow-up). Starlette < 1.7 raised `ValueError("Invalid IPv6 URL")` while parsing a Host like `[::1` before the EM-114 allowlist ran, so the request died as a 500 (fail-closed, nothing served). The hardening middleware now reads the path from the raw ASGI scope and catches parse errors around the allowlist check only, so malformed hosts always get the same 400 `invalid Host header` response with security headers attached. Downstream handler errors are never masked: a corrupt `graph.json` still surfaces as an honest 500 (pinned by a regression test). Verified on starlette 1.0.0 (raises natively) and 1.7.0 (current pin).
- **Graph server: startup/shutdown hooks migrated from the deprecated `@app.on_event` to a `lifespan` handler** (S1 follow-up). Same behaviour: the non-loopback bind refusal, the per-run token file write and its shutdown cleanup are unchanged; the EM-114 security tests were updated to drive the lifespan and stay green.
- **`tests/test_phase11.py` tar extraction passes `filter="data"`** where supported (Python ≥ 3.12), matching the product import path and silencing the 3.13 deprecation warning. Test-only.

- **EM-412 follow-up: the shadow report's stale caveat now says why vectors stay inert.** `_shadow.py`'s printed `CAVEAT` said "no vectors or cosine until EM-303", but EM-303 shipped — the real reason the shadow's v3 side carries no vectors is that it is fed no query embedding (EM-412's wiring stops at the live read path). Updated the one-line string and the docstring, and `tests/unit/test_provider_shadow.py` pins the new wording; an audit-pointer sweep across `MASTER_TODO.md`, `REMAINING_PLAN.md` and `NEXT_CHUNK.md` records the fix, points the EM-303 prune follow-up at the drafted `docs/plan/PRUNE_CARD_DRAFT.md`, and drops the stale `EM-212` xfail note that flipped green in Chunk 8.

## [2.8.1] - 2026-09-27

A safety patch for the Marketplace release. It carries the fixes from the Hermes catalog review of 2.8.0 (NousResearch/hermes-agent#122476), plus small S1 follow-ups. No new features. The only behaviour change users will notice: vector embeddings are now opt-in (see Security).

### Security

- **CLI `ingest` re-validates every redirect hop (SSRF via redirect).** `validate_url` checked only the first URL, and `urllib` then followed redirects on its own, so a public page answering `302` to `http://169.254.169.254/` (cloud metadata) or a loopback port reached it anyway. All fetches now go through `safe_fetch`, whose redirect handler runs the same validator on each `Location`, refuses non-http(s) targets and stops after 5 hops. A drift-guard test fails if `urlopen` is used directly again. Reported in the Hermes catalog review of 2.8.0 (NousResearch/hermes-agent#122476). Mutation-checked: without the per-hop check, the metadata and loopback redirects both go through.
- **Vector embeddings are opt-in: no model is downloaded unless you enable them.** With `sentence-transformers` importable in the host venv, `remember` built `SentenceTransformer("all-MiniLM-L6-v2")` with no config gate, so the first write downloaded a model from Hugging Face (network egress from a plugin disclosed as local-only). `embeddings.get_embedder()`, the one place a model is built, now requires an explicit opt-in: `embeddings_enabled: true` in the provider config (default `false`), or `ENTROPICMEM_EMBEDDINGS=1` for the CLI. `remember`, `recall_hybrid`, `rebuild_embeddings` and `embedding_stats` all honour it. When embeddings are off, stored vectors are kept and recall uses FTS5 only. **If you relied on semantic recall, set `embeddings_enabled: true`.**
  - Encoding now also runs **before** the engine's write lock is taken, not while it is held. A first-use model download used to stall every other writer for its whole duration.
  - Docs said the model was "BGE-small"; it is, and always was, `all-MiniLM-L6-v2`. README, ARCHITECTURE and MEMORY_MODEL are corrected.
  - Tests use a fake model class that counts constructions. Mutation-checked for: gate ignored, engine gate ignored, encoding inside the lock, provider ignoring the config. Reported in the Hermes catalog review of 2.8.0.
- **Privacy guard covers the whole repository, and CI checks commit identity.** `tests/evals/test_no_personal_data.py` scans every tracked text file (and fixture databases as raw bytes) instead of a hand-picked glob list. The personal-word denylist is private: it comes from the `ENTROPICMEM_PRIVACY_DIGESTS` Actions secret or a local file, never from the repo (unsalted hashes of short words are reversible). Where the secret exists CI requires it, so a missing list fails instead of skipping. Failures name file, line and list position only, never the word. A new `identity-guard` job (`scripts/check_commit_identity.sh`) fails when any commit's author or committer email is not a noreply address. On push it checks the whole reachable history and fails closed on an unresolvable range.

### Fixed

- **The CLI honours `HERMES_HOME` for the shared publish store.** The CLI resolved its own database paths from `HERMES_HOME`, but built every `MemoryEngine` (19 sites) without `hermes_home`, and `shared-init` called `shared_path()` with none. The shared store therefore defaulted to `~/.hermes/entropicmem-shared/` under a custom `HERMES_HOME`, while the provider, which passes its home, used `$HERMES_HOME/entropicmem-shared/`: CLI publishes and the running agent used different logs. Every CLI engine now comes from one `_engine()` helper that passes the resolved home, `MemoryEngine.shared_init()` accepts `hermes_home`, and an AST test fails on any bare `MemoryEngine(...)` in the CLI. The tests run the real CLI with `HOME` and `HERMES_HOME` both inside a temp directory. Reported in the Hermes catalog review of 2.8.0.
- **Docs: every network touchpoint is listed in one place** (README, "Network Access"): opt-in embeddings (Hugging Face download), user-initiated `ingest <url>`, the exported graph HTML (D3, marked and Google Fonts from public CDNs when opened), the loopback-only graph server, and the backup upload to your rclone remote. It also notes where the shared store and `graph export` write by default. `graph_export.py`'s docstrings no longer claim a "vendored/offline fallback" for D3 and marked; none exists. Matches the Hermes catalog disclosure as amended in the 2.8.0 review.
- **Graph server: malformed Host headers now return 400 instead of 500** (S1 follow-up). Starlette < 1.7 raised `ValueError("Invalid IPv6 URL")` while parsing a Host like `[::1` before the EM-114 allowlist ran, so the request died as a 500 (fail-closed, nothing served). The hardening middleware now reads the path from the raw ASGI scope and catches parse errors around the allowlist check only, so malformed hosts always get the same 400 `invalid Host header` response with security headers attached. Downstream handler errors are never masked: a corrupt `graph.json` still surfaces as an honest 500 (pinned by a regression test). Verified on starlette 1.0.0 (raises natively) and 1.7.0 (current pin).
- **Graph server: startup/shutdown hooks migrated from the deprecated `@app.on_event` to a `lifespan` handler** (S1 follow-up). Same behaviour: the non-loopback bind refusal, the per-run token file write and its shutdown cleanup are unchanged; the EM-114 security tests were updated to drive the lifespan and stay green.
- **`tests/test_phase11.py` tar extraction passes `filter="data"`** where supported (Python ≥ 3.12), matching the product import path and silencing the 3.13 deprecation warning. Test-only.
- **CI `perf-smoke` now measures a real p95, not the single slowest sample.** The job ran `evals.perf --probes 5`, and `percentile()` is nearest-rank, so with 5 samples the "p95" was `max()`: one stray slow probe could fail the gate on unchanged code. It now runs `--probes 20`, so p95 is the 19th of 20 samples. The 20 ms budget is unchanged.

### Changed

- **README states that native Windows is not supported on 2.8.x** (the engine imports `fcntl`; use Linux, macOS or WSL). This was already true of 2.8.0, where CI's `windows-import` job is allowed to fail, but it was not written down anywhere a user would see it. Portable locking (EM-202) ships with 3.0.
- **EM-213, re-scoped and done: `plugin.yaml` declares tools and hooks once.** The duplicate `hooks:` list is removed at the Hermes catalog maintainer's request. `provides_tools`/`provides_hooks` stay. The original F-011 finding asked for them to go, but the 2.8.0 catalog review verified the plugin against exactly those lists, so they are the single, exact declaration (pinned to `register()` by `tests/test_plugin_hardening.py`). The strict xfail that demanded their removal is replaced by `test_f011_plugin_manifest_declares_tools_and_hooks_once`, which pins the re-scoped requirement and is mutation-checked (a re-added `hooks:` list fails it). `hermes plugins validate` still prints its two "declared but not registered" warnings; that is a validator limitation for MemoryProviders, documented in the README.
- **CI test matrix adds Python 3.13** and `pyproject.toml` gains the 3.13 classifier: Hermes Agent supports 3.13 and the full suite (927 passed, 2 skipped, 5 xfailed) already passes on it at 2.8.0. Python 3.14 is deliberately not added (Hermes cannot run on it).
- **CHANGELOG wording:** the EM-212/EM-213 known-limitation note no longer cites the old PR number (pre-cutover numbering doesn't exist in this repo); it now says "an earlier attempt was closed unmerged, pre-2.8.0 history".

## [2.8.0] - 2026-09-25

Sprint 1 hotfix release (EM-101 to EM-118). Retrieval stops injecting noise and stops forgetting durable facts; writes stop silently overwriting; background work keeps its profile; Core Memory moves into the system prompt; export/import is consistent and fail-closed; the graph servers gain Host-allowlist, CSP and per-run-token hardening. Measured on the eval suites against the 2.7.0 baselines: ci recall@5 1.000 → 1.000, abstain_correct 0.000 → 1.000, noise_rate 0.286 → 0.143, must_not_ok 0.714 → 1.000; hard abstain_correct 0.000 → 1.000, noise_rate 0.303 → 0.214, recall@5 0.961 → 0.939. The hard recall dip is the deliberate EM-104 trade-off: five turns lost recall and one gained. Each of the five expected memories shared *only stopwords* with its query ("in", "is", "the", "we"), which is the same matching that made 2.7.0 inject filler for every junk query; they need semantic retrieval (S3). The hard ageing fixtures' ages were also corrected in this release.

**Known limitations (not fixed in 2.8.0):** lexical-only synonym recall. Hard-suite ageing recall@5 is 0.733, below the 0.90 plan target, because the four misses share no words with the stored fact; it is accepted for 2.8.0 and targeted at S3 semantic retrieval (EM-303). Full per-user isolation (S4; `owner_user_ids` is an interim owner/guest guard), async prefetch (S4), episode recall (S3), and EM-212/EM-213 plugin namespace and manifest work (S2; an earlier attempt was closed unmerged, pre-2.8.0 history) are also not included. Each is pinned by a strict xfail in `tests/regressions/test_findings_v27.py`.

### Changed

- **Documentation aligned with 2.8.0 behaviour (EM-117).** The README config table gains the S1 keys (`core_inject_mode`, `locale_packs`, `owner_user_ids`, `guest_hidden_domains`, `allow_agent_consolidate`, `mirror.background_review`) and corrects the `prefetch_denied_sources` default (EM-115) and the `core_memory_enabled` description (EM-116). The README security bullet, `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` and both `SETUP.md` copies now describe the real graph servers (FastAPI server: Host allowlist, CSP, static or per-run token; stdlib `graph serve`). A new README "Known Limitations (2.8.0)" section covers lexical-only synonym recall (hard-suite ageing 0.733, S3), the interim per-user guard (S4), synchronous prefetch (S4), episode recall (S3) and EM-212/213 (S2). The smart-context section of `HERMES_INTEGRATION.md` now matches the S1 retrieval: absolute scoring with a coverage gate, a 0.35 threshold, a current-turn query, disclosure off, and whole-fact packing with provenance.

### Added

- **2.8.0 eval baselines (EM-117).** `evals/baselines/v2.8.0-ci.json` (ci: recall@5 1.000, abstain_correct 1.000, noise_rate 0.143, must_not_ok 1.000) and `evals/baselines/v2.8.0.json` (hard, 180 turns: recall@5 0.939, ndcg@5 0.894, abstain_correct 1.000, noise_rate 0.214), generated at the 2.8.0 release commit. The `evals-ci` job now compares against `v2.8.0-ci.json`; the `v2.7.0*` baselines stay as the pre-S1 reference.
- **Hermes host harness (`tests/harness/`).** `FakeHost` drives a memory provider exactly the way Hermes `MemoryManager` does — full `initialize()` kwarg set, `prefetch` on its own thread with the 8 s join and stuck-provider skip, `sync_turn`/`queue_prefetch` on a single FIFO worker, `<memory-context>` wrapping with 10k-char spill, byte-identical replay of prior blocks (cumulative prompt-token accounting), and the real hook order for normal turns, `/new`, `/undo`, compression (checkpoint API v2, fail-closed), delegation, and shutdown with the 5 s drain. Profile scoping is context-local (real `hermes_constants` override when importable, ContextVar simulation otherwise) and `HERMES_HOME` is poisoned with a decoy after `initialize`, so post-init env reads are caught. Ships the EM-003 acceptance tests: a 20-turn smoke run with metrics, and a two-profile bleed test (xfail strict until EM-102).
- **Performance bench (`evals/perf.py`, EM-005).** Seeded synthetic corpora (1k / 10k / 50k memories) measured through the real engine and provider paths: `remember` p50/p95 (no embedding), `prefetch` p50/p95 cold/warm, tool search p95, DB size per memory, and a concurrent-writer test (CLI writer process + provider reader) reporting prefetch p95 under write load. `python -m evals.perf --sizes 1000,10000` prints a table and stores `evals/results/perf-*.json`. Stdlib only; informational §6.4 budget flags (CI enforcement lands in EM-007).
- **Eval framework (`evals/`)** — scenario-driven measurement of the v2 retrieval pipeline (EM-001). JSONL datasets with `$N`/`$noise` refs, seeded deterministic noise, adapter interface (`load`/`search`/`prefetch`) with a real `engine_v2` adapter, per-query metrics (recall@5, mrr, ndcg@5, abstain_correct, noise_rate@prefetch, prefetch_tokens, latency_ms), suites `ci`/`full`/`hard`/`external`, `python -m evals run --suite ci --adapter v2 [--compare baseline]` writing `evals/results/<suite>-<sha>.json` plus a Markdown table. The `ci` suite forces the optional ML stack off (sys.meta_path blocker + engine flag patch) so it is stdlib-fast and deterministic even where sentence-transformers is installed; embeddings are exercised only by `full`. `benchmarks/` is unchanged. (EM-002 completion: `--suite hard` wires the 12-category `evals/datasets_hard/` scenario set through the same runner, embeddings off like `ci`; the tracked `evals/baselines/v2.7.0.json` baseline — S0 exit criterion #1 — is generated from the `rollback/pre-s1-20260924` tag's engine in a throwaway worktree, 180 turns / 12 categories.)

### Fixed
- **Eval `--compare` gates only the §6.3 metrics (EM-117).** `compare_deltas` flagged every metric, so the `evals-ci` job failed on the S1 branch because `prefetch_tokens` rose 27.9 → 35.0, an intended EM-107 trade-off (provenance on injected bullets), while every gated metric improved. It would also have failed on any `latency_ms` jitter above 0.02 ms. Only `recall@5`, `ndcg@5`, `abstain_correct`, `noise_rate` and `must_not_ok` (`GATED_METRICS`, matching the CI workflow comment) now fail the gate; the other metrics stay in the table and are marked `info` when worse. Rows gain `gated`/`worsened`. Tested at the unit and CLI level (a non-gated worsening exits 0; a gated `noise_rate` regression exits 1).
- **`entropicmem graph serve` hardening (EM-114 follow-up).** The CLI served `--dir` through a bare `SimpleHTTPRequestHandler`: any bind was accepted (`--bind 0.0.0.0` published `graph.html`, which can embed note bodies, plus every other file and a directory listing to the network), there was no Host check, and the docs' "loopback bind enforced" claim was false. It now uses the new stdlib-only `graph_static.py`: a non-loopback bind exits `2` unless `ENTROPICMEM_GRAPH_EXPOSE=1` (and then prints an unauthenticated-exposure warning); the Host allowlist uses the same rules as the FastAPI server against the actually bound port (`--port 0` works); the same CSP/nosniff/no-referrer headers are sent; and only `/`, `/graph.html` and `/graph.json` are served. There is still no token, which is documented, with the FastAPI server as the authenticated option. `tests/test_graph_static.py` (22 cases, real sockets) covers Host accept/reject, path allowlisting (`/secret.md`, `/index.db`, traversal), headers on 200/404/400, HEAD/501, bind refusal in the module and the CLI, and a drift guard that fails if the CSP string or Host parsing diverges from `scripts/graph_server/server.py`.
- **Graph server request hardening (EM-114).** Additive patch to the canonical `scripts/graph_server/server.py` (no second `graph_server` package; the uncommitted plugin-side experiment was not adopted). (1) *Host allowlist:* an HTTP middleware refuses, with `400 invalid Host header`, any request whose `Host` does not name `127.0.0.1`/`localhost`/`[::1]` (or a loopback bind address) on the port taken from the listening socket (ASGI `scope["server"]`, so it follows the real `--port`; a portless Host means `:80`). With `ENTROPICMEM_GRAPH_EXPOSE=1` the concrete bind host and the new opt-in `ENTROPICMEM_GRAPH_ALLOWED_HOSTS` list are allowed too; wildcard addresses never are. It runs before the token gate and defeats DNS rebinding against the tokenless loopback trust plane. (2) *Security headers:* `Content-Security-Policy` (`default-src 'none'`; scripts only inline + `d3js.org` + `cdn.jsdelivr.net`; styles inline + Google Fonts; `img-src 'self' data:`; `connect-src 'self'`; `base-uri`/`form-action`/`frame-ancestors`/`object-src` `'none'`), `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff` and `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer` on every response, errors and Host rejections included. (3) *Random per-run token:* `secrets.token_urlsafe(32)` at process start is the credential whenever `ENTROPICMEM_GRAPH_TOKEN` is unset. It is written owner-only (`0600`, atomic) to `$HERMES_HOME/entropicmem/graph_server.token` at startup and removed at shutdown; with a static token configured the static token is the only accepted one and a stale file is deleted. `/refresh` therefore always needs a token (loopback included) but no longer needs a static secret to be usable. Status codes are unchanged: no token and no static token gives `403`, a missing token with a static token configured gives `401`, a wrong token gives `401`. `/health` stays open (exempt from token and Host checks) and adds `token_source`. Body-bearing reads still need the token when exposed, and a poisoned `notes_meta.path` still returns `200` with `full_body == ""` and no outside content. Tests: 31 new cases in `tests/test_graph_server_security.py` (Host accept/reject matrix incl. rebinding, wrong port, portless, wildcard, malformed; actual-port tracking; exposed bind host and allowed-hosts; Host-before-token ordering; headers on success/404/400; CSP covers every external origin `graph.html` loads; token randomness across two processes; per-run vs static token precedence; token file mode and lifecycle). 26 of them fail against the pre-EM-114 server. The three graph test clients now use `base_url="http://127.0.0.1:8075"`, because TestClient's default `Host: testserver` is correctly refused. Manually verified with real uvicorn and headless Chromium: `graph.html` renders and its search and note fetches work under the CSP, and the only violation was a deliberate cross-origin fetch.
- **S1 carry-over fixes (Sourcery reconciliation, pre-S1 `main` findings).** *Privacy:* the remaining realistic-looking phone numbers and real email domains in `gen_hard_scenarios.py` / `evals/datasets_hard/{update,multi_user}.jsonl` are replaced with reserved fictional values (`+1 555 0100`–`0199`, `example.com`/`example.org`); `tests/evals/test_hard_dataset.py` gains a guard that fails on any other phone number or email domain in the hard datasets or their generator, and the G1 employer-string check in `tests/test_extraction_hygiene.py` matches the employer by SHA-256 instead of spelling it out. Git history was not rewritten. *Hard-suite generator:* `temporal` was missing from `datasets` (a clean regeneration wrote 55 of the 60 scenarios); ported verbatim, byte-identical to the committed `temporal.jsonl`. The ageing fixtures had inverted ages (the superseded "previous" fact was 5/10 days old against a 30/120/400-day-old current fact); the previous fact is now 90 days older than the current one. Removed a dead `range(0)` comprehension in `update_port_01`; added a `__main__` guard (importing no longer writes files), a script-relative output dir, and UTF-8 I/O. New tests regenerate into a temp dir and diff against the committed JSONL, check that import has no side effects, and check ageing age ordering. *Dataset validation:* `expect_ids` must fully match `\$(\d+)` with `0 <= idx < n` (`$-1` was accepted). *Eval adapter:* `force_no_embeddings()` raises `RuntimeError` when `sentence_transformers`/`torch` are already imported (a loaded module cannot be un-imported), records everything it patches, and the new `restore_embeddings()` removes the `_MLBlocker` from `sys.meta_path` and restores the engine/embeddings flags and `embed_text`; `EngineV2Adapter.shutdown()` releases the lock (reference-counted across live adapters). *Perf bench:* `run_perf(probes<1)` raises `ValueError` before touching disk instead of dividing by zero in `_measure_concurrent`. *CI:* the `windows-import` job sets `PYTHONPATH=plugins/entropicmem/scripts`; the perf-smoke comment now matches its 20 ms assertion; the `test` job installs `pyyaml` (always present on a Hermes host; without it `_backend` cannot read `config.yaml` and the two EM-102 config-precedence tests fail). *`.gitignore`:* `evals/results/*` instead of `evals/results/`, so the `!evals/results/perf-*.json` negation actually re-includes perf results. *Harness:* `FakeHost._prefetch_turn` binds the session id at submit time instead of reading `self._session_id` from the worker thread; overlapping hosts that shut down out of order now unwind `HERMES_HOME` to the pre-first-host value (hosts are spliced out of a live-host chain) instead of leaving a dead host's decoy in the env.
- **De-personalised product defaults (EM-115, G1).** `DEFAULT_DOMAINS` is the generic seven (Knowledge/People/Projects/Procedures/Preferences/Events/Infrastructure — the employer/campaign domains move to user config); `prefetch_denied_sources` is `["auto_extracted","test"]` only (test-scaffolding sources removed from product defaults); the secret-block policy message no longer names a specific secrets manager; region-specific PII (South African phone + 13-digit ID) moved to opt-in `locale_packs` (config `locale_packs: ["za"]`, default `[]` = generic patterns only), threaded from plugin config through `MemoryEngine(pii_locales=…)` into `scan_pii/check_pii/redact_pii(locales=…)`. New `tests/test_depersonalise.py` (8 tests); two existing tests adjusted to the new contracts (ZA phone now opt-in; denied-sources list is 2 entries). Full suite 834 passed / 2 skipped / 5 xfailed.
- **Mirror built-in replace/remove (EM-110, L3).** `on_memory_write` only mirrored `add`, so built-in `replace`/`remove` left stale mirror rows. `replace` now locates the mirror by `make_id(previous_content)` (fallback: substring match of `old_text` against facts tagged `mirrored`), forgets the old row and mirrors the new content; `remove` (empty content) locates and forgets the mirror via `forget(confirm=True)`; unmatched removes touch nothing. Writes with `write_origin == "background_review"` are skipped unless `mirror.background_review: true`. New `tests/test_mirror_lifecycle.py` (8 tests incl. the AC: add → replace → remove = 0 stale mirrors). Full suite 826 passed / 2 skipped / 5 xfailed.
- **Interim gateway privacy guard (EM-118, H1).** New config `owner_user_ids`: when the gateway supplies a `user_id` outside the (non-empty) owner list, the session runs in guest mode — prefetch excludes `sensitivity IN (sensitive, secret)` and `guest_hidden_domains` (default `["People","Finance"]`), Core Memory injects Persona only (never User Profile), `entropicmem_remember` writes are stamped `source=guest_tool` + tag `user:<id>`, and `entropicmem_patch_core` is refused. Owners are unaffected. Empty `owner_user_ids` with a gateway `user_id` logs a ONE-TIME warning naming the shared-pool limitation (full per-user scoping remains S4). New `tests/test_privacy_guard.py` (9 tests: guest exclusions + owner-unaffected, Persona-only core, guest write stamping, patch_core refusal, order-independent one-time warning). f004 (`test_f004_cross_user_isolation`) marker corrected: it pins FULL alice/bob isolation, which the S1 plan explicitly defers to S4 as a known limitation (its marker wrongly said EM-109). Full suite 818 passed / 2 skipped / 5 xfailed.
- **Core Memory out of per-turn prefetch (EM-116, L8).** `prefetch()` re-injected the full Core Memory block on every turn (linear token growth).
